# Failure ledger

A missed target or a failed prediction is a result, not a bug. Every miss gets an entry here, with the same depth as a hit. The `scholar` worker writes the entries. The orchestrator supplies the expected value, the measured value, the ranked causes and the diagnostics. The scholar adds the literature check.

A failing code check is a different thing (an engineering problem, handled by the rules in `CLAUDE.md`) and does not go here unless it turns into a missed prediction.

## Entry format

Entries are appended in order. Newest last. Each entry uses this template.

```
### F-NNN  Short title
- Date, session, work package, step:
- Prediction on record (with the page and section it comes from):
- Expected value or outcome, with its tolerance:
- Measured value or outcome, with parameters (beta, omega, N, grid, box, T) and the result file path:
- Size of the miss against the tolerance (target, within about 3 times, beyond):
- Ranked causes (orchestrator): 1 ... 2 ... 3 ...
- Diagnostics run now (up to three cheap ones), what each returned:
- Diagnostics proposed (expensive ones), not run:
- What the miss rules in, and what it rules out:
- Literature check (scholar): has published work already seen and explained this? Sources with links and tags
  (READ, ABSTRACT, SECONDHAND) and the searches run. Write "no source found" where that is the case.
- Cross-reference: PRIOR_ART.md section, SESSION report section, reviewer finding if any:
- Status: open | explained | rerun not needed | superseded
```

## Rules for entries

- No rerunning to rescue a result. The entry records what happened and why.
- Numbers carry their parameters. A number from a different potential, dimension or amplitude regime is context, not a prediction, and is labelled so.
- Literature claims follow the evidence rules in `PRIOR_ART.md`: a link and one tag per claim, and "no source found" when nothing turned up.
- If a miss was already anticipated in `PRIOR_ART.md` (for example, the unstable-branch radial run settling onto the stable branch instead of dispersing, or a Step 2 energy retention below 90% near a radiation peak), the entry says so and links the section.

## Entries

Entries add one field to the template, "Interpretation notes (scholar)", for points that change how the miss should be read. The orchestrator's text is kept as supplied; scholar additions are marked.

### F-001  Absorber validation (WP1B check 4): free Gaussian in an L = 50 box does not match the L = 100 reference

- Date, session, work package, step: 2026-10-01, Session 1, WP1B (engine v2), check 4. It gates the box design for Step 2 (plan section 4, "Parameter selection for Step 2", Box bullet).
- Prediction on record: `docs/SWIRL_PLAN_2026-10-01.md`, Appendix A, WP1B check 4; section 5 standard 4 (absorbing boundaries validated against a box twice the size).
- Expected value or outcome, with its tolerance: relative L2 error at most 0.05 over r < 10 at every sampled time up to T = 75, for a free Gaussian (Set B: m = 1, g4 = 0.30, g6 = 0.055, phi0 = 0.5, R = 2.5) in an L = 50 periodic box with the absorber on, against an L = 100 run with no absorber. The plan adds "if L = 50 cannot reach it, report the smallest box that does", so it allowed for this outcome. The entry exists because of the size of the miss and because its mechanism bears on the Step 2 box.
- Measured value or outcome: result file `outputs/engine/check4_absorber.json` (worktree agent-ad6ebac785fbd2772); design scan `outputs/engine/absorber_design_scan.json`; test `tests/engine/test_check4_absorber.py`. Absorber `SPONGE_DEFAULT`: spherical gamma(r) phi_t, gamma_max = 2, width 12, cubic ramp, r_in = L/2 - 12. dx = 0.78125, dt = dx/8, T = 75.
  - L = 50: phi-only relative L2 max 1.744 at T = 75 (0.559 at t = 66 was the first figure quoted; the maximum is the honest headline). State-norm metric sqrt(|phi|^2 + |phi_t|^2) max 0.463. Error over initial norm 0.060. The reference norm on r < 10 falls to 3 to 6 percent of its initial value at the late samples, so the phi-only metric is ill-conditioned there. Error stays below 0.5 percent until t of about 36.
  - Box scan (phi-only max): 1.744, 0.78, 0.232, 0.048, 0.0021 at L = 50, 56.25, 62.5, 68.75, 75. Smallest passing box L = 68.75 (set by the T = 75 sample). L = 62.5 passes within 1.2 times on the state-norm metric (0.059).
  - Sanity: L = 125 with the absorber on against the reference, 3e-9.
- Size of the miss against the tolerance: at L = 50, 35 times the target on the phi-only metric and 9 times on the state-norm metric (beyond about 3 times). At L = 62.5: 4.6 times phi-only (beyond), 1.2 times state-norm (within, pass with note). L = 68.75 passes.
- Ranked causes (author and reviewer): (1) Low and moderate k components (k about 0.6, group speed about 0.5) reflect off the sponge front. Onset times scale with r_in: first 1e-3 error at t = 35.5, 48.2, 58.4, 73.6 for r_in = 13, 16.1, 19.25, 22.4. (2) The interior is smaller than the dispersing slow packet, so the sponge damps the packet itself. (3) Metric conditioning.
- Diagnostics run now: box scan above. Onset times against r_in. L = 100 with the L = 50 sponge geometry gives the same numbers to 4 digits, so periodic wrap is ruled out. Sponge strength at L = 50: phi-only max 0.34, 0.28, 0.70, 1.74, 2.94 for gamma_max 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 4; best of a 28-variant scan 0.163 (3.3 times target). Fast packet: for a k0 = 2.4 packet the default sponge leaves 0.0021 of the state norm and a weak sponge leaves 0.287, so weak sponges are not a fix for Step 2.
- Diagnostics proposed (expensive ones), not run: none supplied by the orchestrator. Scholar suggestions (cheap, from the literature check below): (a) add wide, weak designs (widths 25 to 40, gamma_max 0.3 to 1) to the 1D scan `swirl/engine/sponge1d.py`, which now covers widths 8 to 14 and gamma_max 0.3 to 4, and report the returned fraction for k0 between 0.2 and 1.0; (b) judge a Step 2 box by the published test, energy in a shell against total energy while an oscillon sits on its plateau, rather than relative L2 on a decaying free tail.
- What the miss rules in, and what it rules out: Rules in: the interior radius must be about 22 or more for slow free packets observed to T = 75 with this sponge; Step 2 boxes and the linear twin must use a large interior. Rules out: fast or third-harmonic reflection and periodic wrap as the cause.
- Literature check (scholar): Seen before and explained qualitatively; not quantified for this sponge. [Honda's thesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-ph/0009104) (READ) says the massive Klein-Gordon equation has no closed-form outgoing condition because the radiation speed is not unique, so sponge-type conditions are approximate and can back-scatter, and [Honda & Choptuik 2002](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-ph/0110065) (READ) say Sommerfeld conditions reflect significantly for massive fields. [Gleiser & Sornborger 2000](https://arxiv.org/abs/patt-sol/9909002) (READ) use the same gamma(rho) phi_t sponge as the engine for 3D phi^4 oscillons, start the damping at about 20 times the oscillon scale, validate by shell energy (r < 5 R_0) to better than 1e-4 against an expanding grid, and report that long-wavelength modes are damped less efficiently than short ones and reflect from the far end of the layer, curing it with a larger grid and a later start of damping. Their layer is about 150 wide with gamma at the far end of about 0.6 against m = 1.4 (DERIVED from the extracted formula), roughly 5 times weaker relative to m and 12 times wider than the engine default. [Li, Yamaguchi, Zhang 2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.13276) (READ, App. B) use the same form for the beta = 4/3 sextic model with damping starting at r = 64. Every published radial run I read puts the absorber 50 to 100 from the core (PRIOR_ART section 6 and Addendum A1). Even a perfectly matched layer for nonlinear Klein-Gordon ([Antoine & Zhao 2022](https://arxiv.org/abs/2101.02528), READ) has an in-layer attenuation factor that tends to zero as the mode frequency approaches the mass (DERIVED from their eq. 2.5), and the free 3D Klein-Gordon field decays only as t^(-3/2) ([Komech & Kopylova 2010](https://arxiv.org/abs/1003.3799), READ intro), so the late field at r < 10 is a slow tail that a short layer neither absorbs nor leaves undisturbed. No source found for a reflection coefficient against k for this sponge near the gap, or for a rule on gamma_max against m. A plane-wave estimate (Addendum A1, N1, DERIVED) puts critical damping at gamma = 2 sqrt(k^2 + m^2), so gamma_max = 2 m is critical for the slowest modes; the project's own 1D scan has its knee at k0 between 0.4 and 0.7 (returned energy fraction 0.069 at 0.4, 0.0021 at 0.7, 1.9e-5 at 1.0, `PROJECT DATA`), and the F-001 onset times correspond to k of 0.50 to 0.56 (N2), the same band. Searches 21 to 28 and 36 in Addendum A3.
- Interpretation notes (scholar): (i) This is a known limitation class (slow waves near the mass gap), not a failure to absorb radiation of the kind Step 2 produces: the k0 = 2.4 result stands. (ii) The error over initial norm is 0.060; read as an amplitude ratio the energy-like fraction is about 4e-3 (DERIVED, rough: the state norm is not the energy). The 1.744 is large because the reference signal is 3 to 6 percent of its start, which is the t^(-3/2) tail. (iii) The literature validates absorbers on a plateau-supporting target (shell energy during an oscillon plateau), a better-conditioned test for Step 2 than relative L2 of a free tail; the box-doubling rule in the plan is compatible with it. (iv) The 22 interior radius is far smaller than published practice (20 oscillon scales, 50 to 100 from the core); that is a cost choice in 3D, but it means a Step 2 run that releases slow quanta while it settles ([Zhang et al. 2020](https://arxiv.org/abs/2004.01202), footnote 15, READ, describes them) will see them again unless the interior is large. (v) The 1D scan already flagged that below k0 of about 0.5 its number is not a reflection coefficient, so the design was evidently chosen with fast radiation in mind (the comment above `SPONGE_DEFAULT` in `swirl/engine/evolver.py` says as much).
- Cross-reference: `PRIOR_ART.md` section 6 (Boundaries; the slow-quanta bullet anticipated the problem in qualitative form), Addendum A1 and A3; SESSION_01 report section: not yet written; reviewer finding: none supplied to the scholar.
- Status: explained (qualitatively, by a known limitation and by the project's own 1D data). Open as a design constraint on the Step 2 box, an orchestrator decision; rerun not needed.

### F-002  Step 1 radial check: no profile at beta = 0.611 or 0.70 stays localized to T = 500

Updated 2026-10-01 after the independent WP1A review (reviewer re-ran the radial PDE with a different scheme). The first-pass text is kept; the review results and the reframing are added where marked. Note on names: D1 to D3 below are the orchestrator's review diagnostics; my derived notes in `PRIOR_ART.md` Addendum A are called N1 to N10.

- Date, session, work package, step: 2026-10-01, Session 1, WP1A radial time-evolution check with its lifetime scan, Gamma map and the beta = 0.85, 1.0 and 4/3 extensions; Step 1.
- Prediction on record: `docs/SWIRL_PLAN_2026-10-01.md` section 4, Step 1, "Prediction on record" (profiles with dN/domega < 0 stay localized, the other branch does not, the legacy Gaussian disperses); Appendix A, WP1A, radial check (beta = 0.611 and 0.70, T = 500); section 4, "Parameter selection for Step 2" (beta = 0.70 suggested, and the rule for a contradicted stability prediction). The plan notes these predictions come from recollection and were unchecked.
- Expected value or outcome, with its tolerance: dN/domega < 0 profiles stay localized to T = 500; the other branch does not; the legacy Gaussian disperses. The plan gives no numeric threshold for the radial check; the Step 2 Go value (at least 90 percent of the energy inside 3 half-widths) is used here only for scale.
- Measured value or outcome: spherical solver, full field equation, phi_t = 0 single-frequency profile initial data. Sponge validated by box doubling to 2e-8; halving dr and dt changes the retained fraction by less than 4e-5. Result files in worktree agent-a63e9fc3746934e76, `outputs/family/`: `lifetime_scan.json`, `gamma_map.json`, `radial_checks.json`, `radial_beta0.6111.json`, `radial_beta0.7000.json`, `family_summary.json`, `literature_comparison.json`, with plots.
  - beta = 0.611 and 0.70: every profile dies before T = 500 on both branches. E(r < 3 half-widths)/E0 at T = 500 is 0.0004 to 0.004. Death time (E_in below half its t = 50 value): beta = 0.611: 123 (dN/domega > 0, omega = 0.965), 173 (omega = 0.885), 237 (omega = 0.777, E/(mN) = 0.95, shed 31 percent). beta = 0.70: 166 (omega = 0.977), 270 (omega = 0.897), 344 (omega = 0.805, shed 29 percent). Plateau radiation rate Gamma = |dE/dt|/E of about 5e-4 to 2.6e-3 (5e-4 to 2e-3 in the review summary). The core omega drifts up and death follows when it reaches about omega_c (0.925 at beta = 0.611, 0.937 at 0.70), i.e. energetic death when N falls to N_min.
  - 25-profile Gamma map at beta = 0.70 (omega 0.71 to 0.92): all die at t = 95 to 380, Gamma 5e-4 to 7e-4, no dips, shed fraction 0.3 to 49 percent.
  - beta = 0.85: survivors at T = 500 for most of omega 0.73 to 0.91 (retained fraction 0.4 to 0.96); a sharp dip at omega = 0.779 (fraction 0.957, Gamma 5.9e-5) next to omega = 0.789 to 0.799, which die.
  - beta = 1.0: E/(mN) = 0.90 alive at T = 3000 (fraction 0.77).
  - beta = 4/3: omega = 0.909 retains 0.995 at T = 500, Gamma 3e-6 (the dip matches Ibe 2019 at Q = 10^2.14), 0.956 at T = 5000. Gamma at beta = 4/3 spans 3e-6 to 4e-4 against 5e-4 to 2e-3 at beta of 0.70 or below.
  - Legacy Gaussian disperses (0.004 at T = 100), as predicted. Stable-branch profiles outlive unstable-branch ones by 1.5 to 2 times, so the ordering holds; localization does not.
  - Independent review (added): the reviewer re-ran the radial PDE with a different scheme (finite-volume second order on phi, velocity-Verlet, dr = 0.02, dt = 0.004, cubic sponge). Every death time agrees to 0.5 time units or better and E_loc/E0 to 1.5e-3 or better. The reviewer's own shooting profile solver agrees with the original to 1e-11.
  - Review-stage diagnostics (added, orchestrator). D1: every stable-branch lump at beta of 0.70 or below sheds and radiates down to E_loc of about E_min of the single-frequency family (beta = 0.70: 1153 to 1109 against E_min 1180.9; beta = 0.611: 1083 to 1039 against 1097.3; Set-B-like units), then dies in a burst within about 40 time units (core amplitude 8.4 to 0.5). The unstable-branch runs did not simply disperse: at beta = 0.611 the omega_c + 0.04 profile relaxed from omega 0.962 to 0.903 (below omega_c = 0.925), onto the stable side, and died later; at beta = 4/3 an omega_c + 0.02 profile survives to T = 3000 (fraction 0.84). D2: amplitude sensitivity at beta = 0.70, omega = 0.8966: initial profile scaled by 0.97 / 0.99 / 1.00 / 1.01 / 1.03 / 1.06 gives death times 176 / 261.5 / 269.5 / 279.5 / 269.5 / 260.5, so the outcome is not fine-tuned and no amplitude rescues it. D3: matched N/N_min = 1.3 / 1.8 / 2.5, T = 600, death time (or E_loc/E0 at 600 where alive): beta = 0.611: 216.5 / 235 / 231; 0.70: 308.5 / 342.5 / 344.5; 0.80: 482.5 / 546.5 / 576; 0.85: alive (0.63 / 0.58 / 0.44); 1.0: alive (0.79 / 0.63 / 0.76); 4/3: alive (0.95 / 0.997 / 0.92). Lifetime is smooth and monotone in beta with no step at beta = 5/6. Initial shedding at N/N_min = 1.8 falls from 34 percent (beta = 0.611) to 0.03 percent (beta = 4/3). The beta = 0.70 "deep" run already had N = 1.79 N_min.
- Size of the miss against the tolerance: retained fraction at T = 500 of 4e-4 to 4e-3 against about 0.9, two to three orders of magnitude (beyond) at beta = 0.611 and 0.70. Not a miss at beta of 0.85 and above, where stable-branch profiles survive to T = 500 over much of omega 0.73 to 0.91 (retained fraction 0.4 to 0.96); the Step 2 no-go (no member survives for any beta of 5/8 or more) is not triggered.
- Ranked causes (author): (1) Real, fast radiative decay at low beta for these large-amplitude profiles (nonlinear share -0.41 to -0.83), Gamma about 10 times the beta = 4/3 values, leading to energetic death. (2) The single-frequency phi_t = 0 profile is a poor quasi-breather at low beta (third-harmonic factor 1 + 1/(8 beta) = 1.20 at 0.611; shedding up to 30 percent; settled omega shifted up by about 0.09); cause 2 feeds cause 1 by putting N close to N_min after shedding. (3) Numerical artefact, judged unlikely (box doubling, resolution halving, linear twins, massless exact solution, beta = 4/3 literature reproduction); now excluded by the independent re-run above.
- Diagnostics run now: lifetime scan over all beta and omega; omega before death against omega_c; beta = 4/3 external validation and Gamma map; and, at review, D1 to D3 and the independent re-run listed above.
- Diagnostics proposed (expensive ones), not run: multi-harmonic quasi-breather initial data at beta = 0.70 to separate causes 1 and 2 (still open: whether quasi-breather initial data change the low-beta lifetime); a Gaussian (amplitude, width) scan at beta = 0.70 to ask whether any radial initial data gives a long-lived oscillon there; longer runs at beta = 0.85 near omega = 0.78. Scholar suggestion (cheap): extend the beta = 0.70 Gamma map down to omega of about 0.55 to 0.71 (the dip question, note N7). My earlier suggestion of a beta scan across 5/6 is superseded by D3.
- What the miss rules in, and what it rules out. First pass: Rules in: in this model with these initial data, long-lived stable-branch oscillons appear from beta of about 0.85 upward and not at beta of 0.70 or below. Rules out: a solver or boundary bug (then pending the independent re-run). Updated after review: Rules in: a steep beta dependence of lifetime in this model with phi_t = 0 single-frequency initial data, with energetic death at E of about E_min. Rules out: solver and discretisation artefacts (independent scheme agrees), amplitude fine-tuning (D2), a false-vacuum threshold (D3, no step at 5/6), and a thin N margin (the deep run had N = 1.79 N_min). Still open: whether multi-harmonic quasi-breather initial data change the low-beta lifetime (cause 2).
- Literature check (scholar): Partly seen; the steep beta dependence is not explained by any source I could find. No source found for any 3D lifetime or decay-rate measurement of this real phi^4-phi^6 model at beta below 4/3: the published points are beta = 4/3 ([Mukaida 2017](https://arxiv.org/abs/1612.07750), [Ibe 2019](https://arxiv.org/abs/1901.06130), [Li 2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.13276), all READ, one g6 each), about 1.67 in 2D from noise ([Kasuya 2003](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-ph/0209358), READ) and beta of about 17 to 33 in 3D lattice runs ([Amin, Easther, Finkel 2010](https://arxiv.org/abs/1009.2505), READ; mapping in Addendum A2). [Zhang et al. 2020](https://arxiv.org/abs/2004.01202) sec. 7.5 (READ) finds multiple dips for a phi^6 potential and gives no parameters. What is documented: (1) Energetic death, and D1 matches it. Li et al. (READ) place the death point at the energy minimum (dE/domega = 0) and mark that energy as the end of the oscillon's decay; Ibe and Mukaida (beta = 4/3), [Nagy & Takacs 2021](https://arxiv.org/abs/2105.01089) (3D sine-Gordon, sudden collapse at omega_c = 0.9395) and [Fodor 2008](https://arxiv.org/abs/0802.3525) (same energy minimum for phi^4) say the same, all READ; a fast final burst is consistent with [Fodor 2006](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-th/0609023) (READ) noting the final decay is quick. The measured omega_c of 0.925 and 0.937 are within 1 percent of the universal-curve N-minimum estimates 0.933 and 0.942 (N8), and the beta = 0.611 E_min of 1097.3 matches the plan's own table value E = 1098 at omega = 0.922 (plan section 3) to 0.1 percent. (2) The unstable branch: this outcome was anticipated (`PRIOR_ART.md` headline 3, section 5 row 1b, section 7 item 3). [Carles, Klein, Sparber 2021](https://arxiv.org/abs/2012.11637) (READ) find in the 3D cubic-quintic NLS that a perturbed unstable-branch soliton disperses if the perturbation lowers N and relaxes onto the stable branch, shedding radiation, if it raises N. The relaxation 0.962 to 0.903 at beta = 0.611 and the beta = 4/3 survivor at omega_c + 0.02 are the second outcome. Mukaida's statement that configurations near the unstable branch decay immediately (READ) gives no perturbation details and the project's data do not support "immediately". (3) Initial data: [Amin & Shirokoff](https://arxiv.org/abs/1002.3380) (READ) find the single-frequency corrections strongly suppressed only for g_AS much greater than 1 (example about 5), and our beta of 0.611 to 1.0 is g_AS of 0.18 to 0.30 (DERIVED mapping); phi_t = 0 single-frequency data settle with radiation and free low-momentum quanta ([van Dissel 2023](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.16072), Zhang 2020 footnote 15, READ), so shedding is expected in direction, but there is no source for its size. The measured fall of shedding from 34 percent to 0.03 percent across beta = 0.611 to 4/3 agrees in direction with the third-harmonic factor 1 + 1/(8 beta), yet that factor changes by only 10 percent over the range (1.205 to 1.094; N9), so it cannot by itself carry a change of three orders of magnitude; the attribution is plausible and untested, and the open multi-harmonic run is the discriminator. High-precision quasi-breather initial data show almost no initial transient in sine-Gordon (Nagy & Takacs, READ), which supports that run. (4) Potential shape: [Olle, Pujolas, Rompineve 2020](https://arxiv.org/abs/1906.06352) footnote 8 (READ) give a heuristic that very long-lived oscillons need V'' not very negative anywhere. For this potential V''_min/m^2 = 1 - 3/(2 beta), which is -1.46, -1.14, -0.77, -0.50, -0.125 at beta = 0.611, 0.70, 0.85, 1.0, 4/3 (DERIVED, N5); the smooth rise of lifetime with beta in D3 has the same ordering, but the heuristic was not tested on this model. A false-vacuum threshold, which I had suggested from the structure Drogosz et al. ([2026](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23616), READ; different potential) find around an extra local minimum, is not supported: D3 shows no step at 5/6 (N6). (5) Sensitivity to initial data: the published lifetime structure is in the initial radius, with fine resonances for phi^4 ([Honda & Choptuik](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-ph/0110065), Drogosz et al., READ); D2 shows a smooth, weak amplitude dependence at beta = 0.70, with the 0.97 case dying early as expected for a start close to the critical charge (Mukaida, READ: profiles starting near Q_cr die quickly). (6) Rates: the measured plateau Gamma of 5e-4 to 2e-3 at beta of 0.70 or below is about 1.3 to 20 times the published beta = 4/3 peak range of 1e-4 to 4e-4 (Ibe, `READ (figure)`), and Zhang et al. report that the single-frequency radiation calculation overestimates measured rates at large amplitude, so no published calculation would have predicted rates this large. Gamma dips are explained as zeros of the leading radiating harmonic's source (Zhang, Ibe, Mukaida, READ); no source for beta below 1, and a derived scaling (N7, low confidence) puts the first beta = 0.70 dip near omega = 0.69, just below the scanned range. Searches 29 to 35 in Addendum A3. Verdict: the mechanism is explained; the steep beta dependence is not explained by any source; the data lie outside every published regime.
- Interpretation notes (scholar): (i) Narrower claim, as the review found: the dN/domega sign is not what failed; the lifetime is. The criterion concerns adiabatic stability to long-wavelength perturbations ([Amin & Shirokoff](https://arxiv.org/abs/1002.3380); `PRIOR_ART.md` section 2.2), and "stays localized to T = 500" also needs Gamma T well below 1; at beta = 0.70, Gamma = 5e-4 already gives 0.25. The 1.5 to 2 times longer life on the stable branch is what the criterion predicts, and every stable-branch lump dies by energetic death at E of about E_min, the published picture. (ii) The "other branch does not stay localized" half is the half the data cut against, and the brief had said it could go either way (CKS 2021): relaxation onto the stable side (beta = 0.611) and survival (beta = 4/3, omega_c + 0.02, fraction 0.84 at T = 3000) are the second of the two CKS outcomes, so the plan's statement that the other branch does not stay localized is not generally true in this model. (iii) The brief's thin-N-margin hazard (`PRIOR_ART.md` section 7 item 4) is not the explanation (the deep run had N = 1.79 N_min); its hazards about unknown Gamma below beta = 1 and the initial-data mismatch (items 5 and 7) are the live ones. (iv) The Step 2 no-go is not triggered, but the suggested production value beta = 0.70 fails on the radial evidence for every omega tried and every matched N/N_min up to 2.5. Literature support is strongest for beta = 4/3 (three independent references, and the project reproduces the Ibe dip at Q = 10^2.14, which also validates the solver); beta = 0.85 mostly survives to T = 500 (retained 0.4 to 0.96 over omega 0.73 to 0.91, with a sharp dip at 0.779 and a few dying neighbours) and D3 shows survival at matched N/N_min of 1.3 to 2.5 at beta of 0.85 and 1.0 (retained 0.44 to 0.79 at T = 600); beta = 1.0 has no published counterpart. (v) Because lifetime is smooth and monotone in beta (D3), "beta of 0.85 and above" is a place where the lifetime crosses the T = 500 to 600 window, not a sharp threshold; the longer-T behaviour at beta of 0.85 and 1.0 is untested beyond 600 apart from the beta = 1.0 run to 3000. (vi) Compare the Gamma values with the published beta = 4/3 peaks, not with the dips. (vii) The absence of dips in omega 0.71 to 0.92 at beta = 0.70 is not evidence that none exist (N7). (viii) The orchestrator's proposed Gaussian scan has literature support: lifetime depends on initial radius with fine structure (Honda & Choptuik, Drogosz et al.).
- Cross-reference: `PRIOR_ART.md` section 2.2 (energetic death), 2.4 and 2.6, section 5 rows 1a, 1b and 4b, section 7 items 3, 4, 5, 7; Addendum A2, A3 (N5 to N9); SESSION_01 report section: not yet written; reviewer finding: the independent re-run above (agreement to 0.5 time units on death times). See also F-003 for a related reporting gap in the same work package.
- Status: open. Mechanism explained in outline (energetic death at E of about E_min); steep beta dependence unexplained by any source; cause 2 test (multi-harmonic initial data) pending; solver and discretisation artefacts excluded by the independent re-run.
- Update (scholar, Session 2, with F-016): the "0.956 at T = 5000" in the beta = 4/3 bullet of the measured value above, kept as supplied, has no data file (`docs/WP2_PARAMETER_PICK.md` line 54). The traceable radial value for the omega = 0.9091 profile is 0.97553 at T = 5000 (`outputs/step2/diagnostics/primary_lifetime.json`; 3D 0.975498, `outputs/session2/wpB/compare_radial.json`). It entered Session 2's B1 prediction and is recorded in F-016.

### F-003  WP1A check 5: physical-density energy drift of the radial solver exceeds the target

- Date, session, work package, step: 2026-10-01, Session 1, WP1A check 5 (radial solver, absorber off); Step 1. Found in the independent review; it was not mentioned in the original report.
- Prediction on record: `docs/SWIRL_PLAN_2026-10-01.md` Appendix A, WP1A, check 5: the radial solver conserves energy with the absorber off, target 1e-5 relative, and the legacy Gaussian control disperses.
- Expected value or outcome, with its tolerance: relative energy drift of at most 1e-5 over the run with the absorber off.
- Measured value or outcome: `outputs/family/radial_checks.json` (worktree agent-a63e9fc3746934e76), keys `conservation_T100` and `conservation_T500`, profile at beta = 0.6111, omega = 0.8854, dr = 0.05, dt = 0.0125. Discrete energy E_h: 9.85e-9 (T = 100), 6.3e-8 (T = 500); this is the quantity the semi-discrete scheme conserves by construction and is what the original report quoted (9.8e-9 at T = 100). Physical-density energy (second-order gradient plus trapezoid quadrature), used for E0 and every E_loc fraction: 1.26e-4 at T = 100 and 2.05e-4 at T = 500. The legacy Gaussian control disperses.
- Size of the miss against the tolerance: 13 times the target at T = 100 and 21 times at T = 500, beyond about 3 times, so a miss by the project's rule. The E_h figure passes by three orders but does not test the diagnostic.
- Ranked causes (orchestrator): the O(dr^2) quadrature error of the diagnostic, not an evolution error.
- Diagnostics run now: resolution test (dr and dt halved: change in retained fraction at most 3.8e-5) and the independent integrator (reviewer's different scheme: E_loc/E0 agrees to 1.5e-3 or better). Both bound the effect on every verdict quantity.
- Diagnostics proposed (expensive ones), not run: none supplied. Scholar suggestion (cheap): report energies and fractions with a fourth-order or Simpson diagnostic, or from E_h, and show the physical-energy drift falling as dr^2 when dr is halved, which would confirm the cause.
- What the miss rules in, and what it rules out: Rules in: a diagnostic quadrature error of order 1e-4. Does not touch any verdict (orchestrator): the fractions and death times in F-002 are bounded by the resolution test and the independent integrator.
- Literature check (scholar): minimal; nothing here needs the literature to explain it. Published radial oscillon runs report energy conservation of one part in 1e3 down to one part in 1e5: [Copeland et al. 1995](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-ph/9503217) (READ: better than 1e-3, and 1e-5 at high resolution), [Gleiser & Sornborger 2000](https://arxiv.org/abs/patt-sol/9909002) (READ: 4e-4 over 6500 time units with a fourth-order symplectic scheme), [Honda & Choptuik 2002](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-ph/0110065) (READ: a few tenths of a percent, who add that energy conservation says little about boundary reflection). So the plan's 1e-5 sits at the tight end of published practice and the measured 1.3e-4 to 2e-4 for the physical energy is within the published range. That a scheme conserves its own discrete energy while a differently discretised energy drifts is standard: a symplectic integrator conserves a modified Hamiltonian and the original only to O(h^p) ([Hairer, lecture 3](https://www.unige.ch/~hairer/poly_geoint/week3.pdf), READ), and for the spatial discretisation see note N10 (DERIVED). No source found that names a drift of this kind for oscillon codes. Details in Addendum A4.
- Interpretation notes (scholar): (i) The original 9.8e-9 tested the time integrator only. The target is about the energy used in the diagnostics, so the honest figure is the physical-density one, and the report should state both. (ii) The drift of 2e-4 is small against every number that decides F-002 (retained fractions of 4e-4 to 4e-3 against 0.9) and against the checks that bound it; it matters only for any claim that needs a conservation figure of 1e-5 from the diagnostic energy. (iii) This is a gap in what was reported, caught by review, not a change in any result.
- Cross-reference: `PRIOR_ART.md` Addendum A4 (N10) and A3 searches 37 and 38; F-002; SESSION_01 report section: not yet written; reviewer finding: the physical-density drift and the independent integrator comparison.
- Diagnostic run after the final report review (orchestrator, runner): dr halved twice at fixed dt/dr, same profile, absorber off, T = 100 (`outputs/step2/diagnostics/f003_dr_scaling.json`): physical-density variation 1.26e-4 / 3.16e-5 / 7.90e-6 at dr = 0.05 / 0.025 / 0.0125, ratios 3.99 and 4.00. The O(dr^2) quadrature cause is confirmed; the evolution is not involved.
- Status: explained and confirmed (diagnostic quadrature error, O(dr^2)); rerun not needed.

### F-004  WP3 secondary run 1b (beta = 0.85, omega = 0.7793, 3D): E_loc retains 0.795 at T = 1000 against a predicted 0.93

- Date, session, work package, step: 2026-10-01, Session 1, WP3 (Step 2 gate), run 1b (secondary), with the diagnostics run afterwards (commit 780b2b0).
- Prediction on record: `docs/WP2_PARAMETER_PICK.md`, "Predictions on record for WP3 (written before running)", row 1b, secondary.
- Expected value or outcome, with its tolerance: E_loc(r < 3 half-widths = 10.909, T = 1000)/E0 = 0.93, range 0.85 to 0.96, marginal Go (the Step 2 Go line is at least 0.90 with measured omega below m). Basis: radial retained fraction 0.957 at T = 500, extrapolated with a constant Gamma = 5.9e-5. Risk named in advance: the 3D grid could detune the sharp radial dip and the lump die like its neighbours at omega = 0.789. The WP2 table lists no published point at beta = 0.85.
- Measured value or outcome: 3D, N = 192, L = 70, dx = 0.365, dt = 0.04, default sponge. E_loc/E0 = 0.9572 at T = 500, 0.9269 at 750, 0.7954 at 1000; measured omega 0.787 rising to 0.806. Linear twin 0.003 at T = 1000. E plus absorbed conserved to 3.6e-5. The energy lost from r < 10.9 leaves the box: absorbed 0.18 E0 at T = 1000, only 0.012 E0 between r = 10.9 and 20. Result files: `outputs/step2/results/run1b_secondary.json`, `run2b_secondary_twin.json`; diagnostics `outputs/step2/diagnostics/radial_T1000.json`.
- Size of the miss against the tolerance: 0.795 against 0.93 predicted, 0.055 below the bottom of the stated range and 0.105 below the Go line. The energy lost by T = 1000 is 20.5 percent against 7 percent predicted (2.9 times). The T = 500 value (0.957 against 0.957 radial) was right; the miss is in the 500 to 1000 interval.
- Ranked causes (orchestrator): (1) The prediction method, a constant-Gamma extrapolation, was wrong: Gamma grows as the lump loses energy and omega drifts up toward omega_c, an accelerating energetic death as in F-002 at lower beta, here slower. (2) The dip at omega of about 0.78 is a dip in the early-time rate only, and the lump drifts out of it as omega rises (0.787 to 0.806 by T = 1000). (3) 3D detuning: ruled out by D1 (3D equals radial to 6e-4).
- Diagnostics run now: D1: the identical profile in the radial solver to T = 2000 tracks the 3D run to 9.4e-5 for t up to 500 and 6.3e-4 for 500 < t up to 1000 (0.9573 / 0.9270 / 0.7959 at 500 / 750 / 1000), then 0.314 at T = 1500 (E_loc of about 1360 against the single-frequency E_min = 1309.7; omega 0.934 against omega_c = 0.949) and 0.0058 at T = 2000 (omega 1.001); radial death time 1145.5. Radial Gamma is 5.2e-5 (t 200 to 500), 3.0e-4 (500 to 1000), 2.9e-3 (1000 to 2000). D2: the primary (beta = 4/3, omega = 0.9091) radial against 3D agree to 7e-5 to T = 1000; radial Gamma 3.0e-6 / 3.2e-6 / 3.6e-6 over the same windows, 0.990 at T = 2000. D3: the 3D windowed Gamma first exceeds 3 times its t = 200 to 500 value at t of about 690.
- Diagnostics proposed (expensive ones), not run: none supplied. Scholar suggestion (cheap, uses radial data already in hand): predict by integrating along the family, tau = integral of dE / (E Gamma(E)) from E_ini down to E_min, with Gamma read from radial runs as a function of E or omega (the published form, `PRIOR_ART.md` A5), and check that it reproduces the radial death time 1145.5 before using it for any further prediction.
- What the miss rules in, and what it rules out: Rules in: at beta = 0.85 this oscillon has a finite lifetime of about 1100 to 1500 by the same energetic-death route; 3D adds nothing measurable to the radial evolution for either pick to T = 1000. Rules out: a 3D or non-spherical effect, grid detuning, boundary effects (the twins fall to 0.003 to 0.004).
- Literature check (scholar): The mechanism is documented in the same model at beta = 4/3 and in other potentials; I found no source for beta = 0.85 or any value below 4/3, and none that states "1/Gamma at the start overestimates the lifetime" as a rule. What is documented: (1) An oscillon evolves adiabatically along a one-parameter family with slowly rising omega until energetic death ([Zhang et al. 2020](https://arxiv.org/abs/2004.01202), READ; [Cyncynates & Giurgica-Tiron 2021](https://arxiv.org/abs/2104.02069), READ; [van Dissel et al. 2023](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.16072), READ; [Nagy & Takacs 2021](https://arxiv.org/abs/2105.01089), READ, sudden collapse at omega_c in 3D sine-Gordon). (2) Gamma is a function of the instantaneous state, not a constant: for the same potential at beta = 4/3, [Ibe et al. 2019](https://arxiv.org/abs/1901.06130) (READ) give Gamma against Q with dips at the zeros of the 3 omega source, show simulations from different starting omega following one Gamma(Q) curve, and estimate lifetime as tau = integral from Q_cr to Q_i of omega dQ / (E Gamma), an integral along the path (eq. 55); [Li, Yamaguchi, Zhang 2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.13276) (READ, eq. 2.29 and 2.30) do the same in energy, ending at E_death. (3) The dips are called attractors that enhance lifetime while the lump lingers in them ([Mukaida et al. 2017](https://arxiv.org/abs/1612.07750), READ), and in [Cyncynates & Giurgica-Tiron](https://arxiv.org/abs/2104.02069) the dips migrate in frequency as the parameters change, so whether a dip is long-lived depends on how far it lies below the energetic-death frequency. Zhang et al. add that the over-estimate in the dips prevents accurate lifetime estimates where dips exist (sec. 8, READ). So the F-004 pattern, a constant rate read from inside a dip (Gamma 5.2e-5) that rises 6 times by 500 to 1000 and 56 times by 1000 to 2000 as omega drifts out of it and toward omega_c, is the published picture; an early Gamma used as 1/Gamma overestimates here (1.9e4 against the radial death time 1145.5, 17 times; 2.9 times if the 500 to 1000 value is used; N11 in `PRIOR_ART.md`, DERIVED). The sign of the correction is not general: in 2D phi^4 the energy-loss rate falls with time, so an early rate underestimates ([Salmi & Hindmarsh 2012](https://arxiv.org/abs/1201.1934), READ). The size of the growth at beta = 0.85 (Gamma to 2.9e-3) exceeds every published beta = 4/3 value (at most 4e-4, Ibe, `READ (figure)`), as in F-002. On cause 3: [Zhang et al.](https://arxiv.org/abs/2004.01202) sec. 7.5 (READ) find that analytic dip positions shift against numerics for phi^6, so dip position is a sensitive feature and the risk named in advance was a fair one; D1 shows it did not occur here, and the 3D equals radial result agrees with nodeless states not fragmenting ([van Dissel et al.](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.16072), READ). Searches 39 and 40 and Addendum A5.
- Interpretation notes (scholar): (i) The miss is in the prediction method and not in the physics: published lifetimes are integrals along the evolution. My first brief (`PRIOR_ART.md` section 7 item 5) advised comparing 3D E_loc with exp(-Gamma t) for a Gamma read from the radial run; that advice is superseded (correction at the end of A5) and the WP2 prediction followed it. (ii) The mechanism that rescued the primary run is the same one: the lump leaves a dip at a rate set by its own Gamma, so a dip with Gamma of 3e-6 (beta = 4/3) is left about 17 to 20 times more slowly than one with 5e-5 to 6e-5 (beta = 0.85) (N11, DERIVED); that supports the constant-Gamma prediction for the primary run, whose radial Gamma stays flat at 3.0e-6 to 3.6e-6 over the three windows (D2), but the primary should still end by energetic death at some later time (Ibe's beta = 4/3 run from omega_ini = 0.910 lived about 1e4, READ). (iii) The WP2 risk list named detuning of the dip by the grid, and not the lump's own drift out of the dip; the dip's narrowness in omega (0.779 sharp, 0.789 and 0.799 dying, F-002) made drift of 0.019 by T = 1000 enough. The profile label omega and the measured core omega differ, so the dip is crossed in measured omega, as in Zhang et al.'s picture of Gamma along omega(t). (iv) For the gate, the primary run decides; the secondary was informative only (WP2 text). F-004 does not change the Step 2 no-go test, which needs no member to survive at any beta of 5/8 or more; this one survived to T of about 1100.
- Cross-reference: `docs/WP2_PARAMETER_PICK.md` (row 1b); `PRIOR_ART.md` section 7 item 5 and Addendum A5 (N11, correction); F-002 (the same energetic-death route at lower beta); SESSION_01 report section: not yet written; reviewer finding: none supplied.
- Status: explained (prediction method; mechanism documented at beta = 4/3 and in other potentials). Rerun not needed. Open: whether the radial Gamma(E) path integral reproduces the death time 1145.5 (suggested check, not run).
- Update (scholar, Session 2): the suggested check was run in WP-A as validation c and did not reproduce the death time: see F-007 (a Gamma(E) map from other runs gives 11,540 against 1145.5; the integral fed with the run's own Gamma(E) gives 1496, +31%). The published integral is sound; the map is where it failed.

### F-005  WP3 linear-twin prediction: twins are at 0.014 to 0.018 at T = 100, not below 0.01

- Date, session, work package, step: 2026-10-01, Session 1, WP3 runs 2 (primary twin) and 2b (secondary twin), Step 2 control.
- Prediction on record: `docs/WP2_PARAMETER_PICK.md`, "Predictions on record for WP3", rows 2 and 2b. The plan's own control target (`docs/SWIRL_PLAN_2026-10-01.md`, Step 2, Control) is below 10 percent in the same time as the run.
- Expected value or outcome, with its tolerance: twin E_loc/E0 below 0.01 by T = 100 and below 0.01 at T = 1000. Basis quoted: "radial twin 1e-5 at T = 500". Literature quoted: linear packets leave in about 5 time units (Copeland 1995).
- Measured value or outcome: 3D twins 0.0181 (primary) and 0.0137 (secondary) at T = 100. Radial twins (sponge at r = 100) give 0.0198 and 0.0150 at T = 100 (`outputs/step2/diagnostics/radial_T1000.json`), so the T = 100 value is free-field dispersal of a large lump and not a box effect. The 1e-5 quoted as the basis came from the beta = 0.611 profile (1.5e-5 at T = 500, `outputs/family/radial_beta0.6111.json`); the primary's own radial twin is 1.7e-4 at T = 500. Separately, the 3D twins show a non-monotone revival absent in the radial runs: 0.0295 against 0.0014 at t = 250, 0.036 at t of about 283, and 0.0041 against 2.7e-5 at T = 1000 (figures as supplied; I did not check which twin each belongs to). E plus absorbed is conserved to 2e-7. The plan's control target is met with margin: below 0.10 by t = 55. Result files: `outputs/step2/results/run2_primary_twin.json`, `run2b_secondary_twin.json`.
- Size of the miss against the tolerance: at T = 100, 1.4 to 1.8 times the 0.01 written in WP2 and 5 to 7 times smaller than the plan's control line (0.10), which it meets. The T = 1000 value 0.0041 is 0.4 times the 0.01 written, so the prediction holds there. The revival excess against the radial twin is about 20 times at t = 250 and about 150 times at T = 1000 (arithmetic on the supplied numbers).
- Ranked causes (orchestrator): (1) The prediction basis was taken from the wrong profile (orchestrator error). (2) The slow free-field tail of a wide, large-amplitude lump. (3) For the revival: low-k reflection or re-entry at the L = 70 sponge.
- Diagnostics run now: the radial twin comparison above (separates cause 1 and 2 from the box); the check that the quoted basis came from beta = 0.611.
- Diagnostics proposed (expensive ones), not run: an L = 140 twin to test cause 3.
- What the miss rules in, and what it rules out: Rules in: the twin control is valid for the 10 percent target. Standard 4 (box validated against twice the size) is NOT validated for the twins beyond T = 300 or for slow waves: F-001 validated only T up to 75, and the L = 140 box check used the primary, which radiates about 0.4 percent. Rules out: an engine error (E plus absorbed conserved to 2e-7).
- Literature check (scholar): minimal, from sources already read. A free 3D Klein-Gordon field decays as t^(-3/2) at fixed position ([Komech & Kopylova 2010](https://arxiv.org/abs/1003.3799), READ introduction), so the energy in a fixed volume falls as t^(-3). DERIVED check: the radial primary twin 0.0198 at T = 100 scales to about 0.0013 at t = 250 and 2.0e-5 at T = 1000, against the reported radial 0.0014 and 2.7e-5, within about 10 to 35 percent. So the radial twin follows the free-field tail law and the T = 100 value is expected for a lump of this size; the quoted "leave in about 5" ([Copeland et al. 1995](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-ph/9503217), READ, for a small Gaussian bubble) does not apply to the slow tail of a large lump. The revival is the F-001 class: slow near-gap waves are the hard case for a sponge and for any finite box (`PRIOR_ART.md` section 6 and Addendum A1, [Zhang et al. 2020](https://arxiv.org/abs/2004.01202) footnote 15, READ); the first brief anticipated radiation re-entering a box in `PRIOR_ART.md` section 5 row 4c ([Hindmarsh & Salmi 2006](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-th/0606016), [Amin & Mou 2020](https://arxiv.org/abs/2009.11337), READ, for periodic boxes). No source found for a twin value of this size. No new searches run.
- Interpretation notes (scholar): (i) The 0.01 in WP2 was a stricter number than the plan asked for; the plan's control (below 0.10) is met with margin, so the diagnostic is not broken. (ii) The quoted basis should be re-derived per profile, not carried from another beta; the t^(-3) tail gives a way to predict the twin: E_loc at time t of roughly E_loc(100) times (100/t)^3. (iii) The 3D revival means a Step 2 run to T = 1000 sees a slow-wave background of order 0.004 of E0 at L = 70 (twin); small against the gate but not zero, and it would add to E_loc of the nonlinear runs, if the nonlinear runs shed slow quanta as they settle (Zhang et al. footnote 15).
- Cross-reference: `docs/WP2_PARAMETER_PICK.md` rows 2 and 2b; F-001; `PRIOR_ART.md` section 5 row 4c and Addendum A1; SESSION_01 report section: not yet written; reviewer finding: none supplied.
- Status: explained (wrong prediction basis, free-field tail; revival undiagnosed, consistent with the F-001 class). Open: the L = 140 twin.

### F-006  WP1D AWS throughput projection: per-run speed on c7i.4xlarge about 2.5 times below the projection

- Date, session, work package, step: 2026-10-01, Session 1, WP1D (benchmark) projection tested by the WP3 cloud batch.
- Prediction on record: `outputs/benchmark/BENCHMARK_TABLE.md`, "AWS assumptions": c7i per-core speed equals an M4 performance core (stated there as unverified and likely optimistic) and two 4-thread runs side by side each run at the Mac 4-thread rate with no extra bandwidth penalty (stated as optimistic). The table also says AWS numbers need a short trial on the instance before relying on them.
- Expected value or outcome, with its tolerance: per-run rate on c7i.4xlarge equal to the Mac 4-thread rate, 9.4 steps/s raw at 192^3 (7.2 with diagnostics, same table); WP3 batch of 2 to 3 hours and about 2.1 USD.
- Measured value or outcome: run3 (192^3, 4 threads, sharing the 8-core machine with run4 at 288^3) 2.84 steps/s including diagnostics; run4 0.86 steps/s against about 2 to 3 expected; total batch 3.9 h against 2 to 3 h; cost 2.80 USD against about 2.1 USD (`docs/CLOUD_LEDGER.md`: 3.90 h, 2.80 USD, instance terminated). Like for like (with diagnostics, 2 concurrent jobs): Mac runs 1 and 2 ran at 5.0 steps/s each with 2 threads, so the Mac per run was about 1.8 times faster than the c7i at 4 threads.
- Size of the miss against the tolerance: run3 2.5 times below the 7.2 projection and run4 2.3 to 3.5 times below; batch time 1.3 to 1.95 times over and cost 1.33 times over (0.7 USD). By the project's rule most of these are within about 3 times, a pass with a note; the entry exists because the projection drove the decision to rent. Within the 50 USD budget (ledger total 2.80 USD).
- Ranked causes (orchestrator): (1) Memory bandwidth shared by two or three 4-thread jobs (the Mac benchmark showed side-by-side runs falling to 0.42 to 0.61 of the solo rate, `BENCHMARK_TABLE.md`). (2) Per-core speed of the Xeon 8488C below the M4 performance core for this FFT- and bandwidth-bound kernel. (3) Hyperthreads: 16 vCPU is 8 physical cores, so 2 x 4 threads fully occupies them with no headroom.
- Diagnostics run now: none. A 5 to 10 minute single-job c7i benchmark was proposed in WP1D and not done.
- Diagnostics proposed (expensive ones), not run: the single-job benchmark on the target instance before any further budgeting; then 2 concurrent jobs, to separate causes 1 and 2.
- What the miss rules in, and what it rules out: Rules in: for this engine, renting buys parallel slots, not per-run speed; benchmark on the target instance before budgeting. Rules out: nothing definitively. Cost impact: 0.7 USD over the estimate, within budget.
- Literature check (scholar): not applicable (a hardware throughput projection, not a physics result). No source consulted.
- Interpretation notes (scholar): (i) The Mac side-by-side measurement already predicted the direction of cause 1; the AWS projection assumed away both penalties it listed. (ii) With cause 1 and 3 together, the 1.8 times Mac advantage cannot be split between per-core speed and bandwidth without the single-job trial.
- Cross-reference: `docs/CLOUD_LEDGER.md`; `outputs/benchmark/BENCHMARK_TABLE.md`; `docs/AWS_REPORT.md`; SESSION_01 report section: not yet written; reviewer finding: none supplied.
- Status: explained (probable causes, undiagnosed). Open: the single-job benchmark.

### F-007  WP-A checks c and d: the path-integral lifetime estimate misses both validations (prediction A1)

- Date, session, work package, step: 2026-10-01, Session 2, WP-A (lifetime map across beta), validation of the path-integral estimate, checks c and d. The plan made this validation a precondition for using the estimate to choose which profiles to run long (`docs/SESSION_02_PLAN.md`, WP-A, second bullet).
- Prediction on record: `docs/SESSION_02_PREDICTIONS.md`, Block 1, row A1. Basis stated there: Ibe eq. 55 and Li eq. 2.30 are this integral, and our runs follow one adiabatic path (F-004 D1). Literature column at the time: published practice (Ibe 2019, Li 2025, READ); Zhang 2020 warns that estimates are poor where dips exist (READ).
- Expected value or outcome, with its tolerance: the estimate reproduces the beta = 0.85 death (measured 1145.5, the Session 1 death rule) and the beta = 4/3 T_90 (measured 10,630.5), each within 30%, from a Gamma(E) map that excludes the validation runs themselves (starts within 0.002 in omega of the validation start were dropped: 1 start at beta = 0.85, 3 at beta = 4/3).
- Measured value or outcome: radial solver, dr = 0.05, dt = 0.0125, box 200 with the sponge from 100, windows W = 300, transient excluded for t < t_s = 150 + 2 R_lump (183.6 at beta = 0.85, 209.0 at beta = 4/3); map runs used 48 and 63. Result files `outputs/session2/wpA/validation.json`, `ratemap_beta0.8500.json`, `ratemap_beta1.3333.json`; notes in `docs/WPA_BUILD_NOTES.md` sections 2 and 3.
  - c, beta = 0.85, omega = 0.7793: T_death predicted 11,540 (band 11,430 to 11,653) against 1145.5, +907%.
  - d, beta = 4/3, omega = 0.9091: T_90 predicted 33,006 (band 30,578 to unbounded; a floor node is in range) against 10,630.5, +210%.
  - An earlier validation with a different grid cap (alpha^2 up to 0.16) gave 11,540 and 33,023. The author lowered the cap to 0.14 before reading its results (the near-thin-wall bubbles set R_lump close to the sponge start at beta = 1.6). Both runs are on file (`validation_superseded_a2hi016.json`, `ratemap_beta1.3333_superseded_a2hi016.json`); beta = 0.85 is unaffected by the cap.
- Size of the miss against the tolerance: c is 10.1 times the measured value, an error of 9.07 against a tolerance of 0.30 (30 times the tolerance). d is 3.1 times the measured value, an error of 2.10 (7 times the tolerance). Both beyond about 3 times, so both are misses by the project's rule, and both predicted lifetimes are too long. The estimate carried its own warning in both cases (flags in `validation.json`: c has no map node in range and its largest gap is ln 2, the whole path; d has 8 nodes in range and a largest gap of 4.3%), but no threshold for acting on the flags was on record before the runs.
- Ranked causes (orchestrator): (1) Gaps in the map's energy coverage, bridged by log-linear interpolation of Gamma. At beta = 0.85 the map has no node between E = 1411 and 4256 and the whole validation path (4062 down to 2031) lies in the gap: starts at omega0 of 0.814 or more shed straight to near E_min, starts at 0.784 to 0.809 die before the first window, and deep starts (omega0 of 0.774 or less) converge on one state at E about 4300 with Gamma about 1.8e-5. At beta = 4/3 the validation start sits on the shoulder of a very deep dip (a neighbouring start at omega0 = 0.9065 has Gamma of 3e-9 to 5e-9, below the 2e-8 detection floor), and the interpolant is 2 to 7 times too low across a 4.4% gap in E that carries 90% of the predicted time. (2) Windows of 300 time units are too long where Gamma exceeds 1e-3 (beta = 0.85 only). (3) The start-energy estimate: ruled out (using the validation start's own value makes both worse: 12,817 and 33,997).
- Diagnostics run now (three): (i) The validation runs' own Gamma against the map at the same measured E: agreement to 0.01 dex at beta = 4/3 wherever the map has samples, over 3000 time units; 0.85 dex apart across the gap; 0.3 to 1.5 dex apart at beta = 0.85. (ii) The integral fed with the run's own Gamma(E): 10,673 at beta = 4/3 (+0.4%); 1496 at beta = 0.85 (+31%, attributed to the window length). (iii) The beta = 4/3 prediction split into sampled and unsampled parts: 3151 against about 3330 measured (-5%) over the sampled part; 29,646 against about 7100 over the gap.
- Diagnostics proposed (expensive ones), not run: from `WPA_BUILD_NOTES.md` section 3: seed map runs from evolved states (snapshots of one long run) so that the fast-transit ranges are sampled; adaptive windows, W = min(300, 0.05/Gamma), admitting fast-dying runs; return "no prediction" above a gap threshold. Scholar suggestions (cheap, from the literature check below; suggestions, not tasks): (a) the first proposal is the published stitching practice ([Li et al. 2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.13276) run from three omega_ini so that one run continues the previous one, READ), and [Olle et al. 2021](https://arxiv.org/abs/2012.13409) (READ, App. C) add a check made per start that the state has stopped moving in parameter space after the fast phase, before its Gamma is used. (b) For rates at or below the 2 sigma floor (about 2e-8) Olle (READ, App. B) read Gamma from the outgoing flux at a fixed radius averaged over about 1e2/omega, which has no energy-slope floor; it has its own cautions (travel-time lag, and free quanta released while a start settles, [Zhang et al. 2020](https://arxiv.org/abs/2004.01202) footnote 15, READ), so try it on the beta = 4/3 run first. (c) The semi-analytic Gamma of [Ibe 2019](https://arxiv.org/abs/1901.06130) and Li 2025 (READ), computed on the family profiles already on disk, gives an independent dense Gamma(E) to hold against the map across its gaps; Zhang et al. sec. 7.5 (READ) find that for this potential the analytic dips shift against the numerics, so use it to check dip position and depth, not to replace the map.
- What the miss rules in, and what it rules out: Rules in: trajectories from different starts follow one Gamma(E) curve where both are sampled, so the path-integral method itself holds; a map built from short runs at single-frequency starts cannot sample states that those starts do not settle into, and those are exactly the dip states that set the lifetime. Rules out: an error in the integral (synthetic tests to 3.5e-4), and the start-energy estimate. Consequence taken: path-integral rankings are provisional; the long stage runs two picks per beta (path-integral best, and lowest measured windowed rate), and the long runs decide.
- Reviewer check (done 2026-10-01, independent code): both predictions recomputed to the same values (11,539.85 and 33,006.12); the driver reproduces both Session 1 lifetimes (1145.5, frac within 4e-9; 10,630.5, frac within 7.8e-8). The reviewer re-ranks the causes. Validation c: (1) the window design (W = 300 starting at t_s) discards the fast-dying starts, whose saved series do cover E = 3750 to 1400; re-windowing them at 50 time units, with no new run, gives 934 (interpolated start energy) or 1773 (true start energy) against 1145.5; the statement in the build notes that only the validation start passes through that range is false. (2) The remaining unsampled range E = 3635 to 4256, where the lump spends about 750 of its 1145 time units, bridged by a chord that lies below the true curve. (3) W = 300 against a 20-fold change in rate: +31% alone; with W = 60 the run's own Gamma gives 1160 (+1.3%). Validation d: (1) the log-linear chord across the floored node at the rate null (E = 2953); interpolating sqrt(Gamma) linearly in ln E on the same map gives 10,769 (+1.3%). (2) The exclusion rule (+-0.002 in omega) removed three starts and created the 4.3% gap; dropping only the near-duplicate gives 12,340 (+16%). (3) The floor value, minor. Ruled out: units, the E coordinate, the transient cut, the start energy. The estimate is biased upward: all 38 dying starts in `ranking.json` are predicted at or above their measured death. These variants are diagnostics run after the fact; the A1 miss stands. Corrections are appended to `docs/WPA_BUILD_NOTES.md`.
- Literature check (scholar): Partly seen. The mechanism is documented in structure; the size and the specific failure are not. No source found that reports a path-integral or decay-rate lifetime estimate failing by a factor of 3 to 10 at a dip, none that benchmarks an estimate built from a measured map on a dip, and none that states a coverage criterion for such a map. Searches 83 to 87 and Addendum B in `PRIOR_ART.md`. (1) Published agreement is good but away from deep dips. [Cyncynates & Giurgica-Tiron 2021](https://arxiv.org/abs/2104.02069) (READ, sec. IV B): phi^4 explicit lifetime 6000 per m against 5900 per m predicted. [Olle et al. 2021](https://arxiv.org/abs/2012.13409) (READ, Table 1): errors of 6.11, 7.33, 5.20 and 5.83 per cent for four monodromy cases (p = -5 to -2, lifetimes 9e5 to 1.2e7), where the dip is shallow (sec. 3.2). Ibe 2019 (READ, sec. IV, Fig. 5 text) and Li 2025 (READ, sec. 2.3) compare Gamma along the path with simulation and give the lifetime as an integral, with no lifetime error stated; figures not examined. So the plan's 30% had no published basis, and the published 2 to 7 per cent does not extend to dips. (2) Where errors near dips are mentioned, the direction is opposite. [Zhang et al. 2020](https://arxiv.org/abs/2004.01202) (READ, sec. 8) say the analytic Gamma over-estimates the measured rate and that the over-estimate in the dips prevents accurate lifetime estimates, with no factor; the CGT Fig. 14 caption (READ) gives a factor-of-a-few disagreement in radiated power for a QCD-axion oscillon, with the dip region carrying most of the lifetime. These compare an analytic Gamma with simulation. Their Gamma is too high, so the lifetime is too short; the WP-A map's Gamma is too low across a gap, so the lifetime is too long. (3) The structure that makes a gap fatal is published. Dips carry most of the lifetime ([Zhang 2020](https://arxiv.org/abs/2004.01202) introduction, summary of results and Fig. 2 discussion, READ; [Zhang 2020b](https://arxiv.org/abs/2011.11720) sec. 2.1, READ: lifetime is the area under dt/dE = 1/(Gamma E), and a lump starting below omega_dip spends most of its life in the dip; CGT Fig. 14, READ). The path is a staircase: [Li et al. 2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.13276) sec. 2.3 (READ) describe long stays on platforms that are the Gamma dips, with fast falls between them. So any factor error in Gamma on a dip passes one for one into the lifetime, and the states between platforms are crossed fast and sampled by few starts. (4) Reachability from single-frequency starts: no source found for the statement that some states on the adiabatic path cannot be reached from single-frequency initial data. Related and READ: [van Dissel et al. 2023](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.16072) sec. III (not every single-frequency start relaxes to a state; a nodeless start probes the adiabatic curve up to energetic death, but strictly only from single-frequency data, and states with nodes need more than one initialization); Olle 2021 sec. 3.1 and Fig. 8 (whether a start relaxes depends strongly on (A, sigma), and relaxed states cluster); [Mukaida et al. 2017](https://arxiv.org/abs/1612.07750) sec. 3 and Fig. 4 (starts at R_ini = 10, 7, 5 reach different attractor points, and a start below an attractor skips it). The project's own pattern (deep starts converging on one state at E about 4300, starts above the dip shedding past the gap) is the same clustering and skipping. That omega only rises along the path is a consequence of the Zhang 2020b and Li 2025 picture (DERIVED, `PRIOR_ART.md` N20). (5) The construction is Olle's. WP-A builds Gamma(E) as Olle's relax-and-fast-forward does (short runs from sampled starts, Gamma read along them, points joined), with differences that matter: Olle relaxes each start for 1e4 per m against t_s of 184 and 209 here; samples a two-parameter family of cosh starts against one-parameter single-frequency starts; reads Gamma from the outgoing flux rather than the slope of ln E_lump; and states that t_relax must be much smaller than the lifetime, a ratio of 0.16 at beta = 0.85 and 0.020 at beta = 4/3 here against 8e-4 to 1e-2 in Olle's Table 1 (DERIVED, N19; context only, since t_s is a window exclusion and not a relaxation time on the same kind of start). Ibe, Li and Zhang compute Gamma semi-analytically on a dense family, which has no coverage gaps but has the errors in (2). (6) Window length: Ibe (READ, sec. IV) sets the averaging window much shorter than 1/Gamma (100 against about 1e4). W = 300 is 0.30 of 1/Gamma at Gamma = 1e-3 and 0.99 of it at 3.3e-3 (DERIVED, N21), so cause 2 breaks a condition the published papers state. Verdict: seen in structure (dips carry the lifetime, the path is a staircase, starts land on different attractors), not seen in size, and the coverage failure of a simulation-built map is not described by any source I found.
- Interpretation notes (scholar): (i) Was it anticipated in my brief? Partly. S7 row A1 said both validation points sit on dips, "the case Zhang flags", and S8 item 16 said a miss beyond 30% on a dip would be worth recording, not rescuing. Neither named the cause. Two errors in the brief are corrected in `PRIOR_ART.md` Addendum B3: S7 said the plan "agrees with practice", true of the integral but not of how Gamma(E) was built (published Gamma(E) is semi-analytic on a dense family; the one published map built from runs, Olle 2021, relaxes for 1e4 and checks relaxation per start, and I used it only as an accuracy benchmark); and the Zhang caveat has the opposite sign to the miss that happened. The brief gave the location of the risk and not its mechanism or size. (ii) The two misses are not one failure. At beta = 0.85 the path is a single gap and the run's own Gamma gives +31% besides, which the orchestrator attributes to windows too long for a rate that rises from 5e-5 to 1e-3; the gap is also the fast-transit range, and the map starts at omega0 of 0.784 to 0.809 (dying at 253 to 474, before the first window ends at t_s + 300 = 484) contributed nothing, so from the description above they probably crossed the gap and were discarded. If so, causes 1 and 2 interact; I have not checked which energies those runs passed through. At beta = 4/3 the integral is right to 0.4% when given the run's own Gamma, and the whole error is one 4.3% interval on the wall of a dip: 4.3% of ln E holds about two thirds of the measured time and 90% of the predicted time (`PRIOR_ART.md` N20). (iii) The 1% gap threshold in `WPA_BUILD_NOTES.md` section 3 comes from these two points and is post hoc; two points cannot calibrate it, and no source gives one. The estimate's own flags (largest gap 69% and 4.3%) did separate these two cases from a clean one, so the flags are worth keeping and the threshold is worth testing on a case with a known answer, for example a map built from one half of a long run and validated on the other. (iv) Seeding the map from evolved states (the proposal on record) is what Li 2025 does by hand with three starts, so the literature supports it; nothing supports a particular node spacing, and at beta = 4/3 the Gamma contrast across neighbouring starts is about three decades or more (3e-9 to 5e-9 as measured, below the 2e-8 floor, at E = 2953 against 1.2e-5 at E = 2718, 8% apart), so any spacing set in omega0 is coarse for a deep dip. (v) A rate of at most 2e-8 at beta = 4/3: [Ibe 2019](https://arxiv.org/abs/1901.06130) (READ) cannot show rates below about 1e-7 (runs end at t = 1e5), and the dips in his analytic curve were read by eye in Session 1 at about 1e-6 to 1e-7 (`PRIOR_ART.md` section 2.6, READ (figure)). If the WP-A figure holds it lies at least 5 times below that reading; I have not compared dip positions and this is not a novelty claim. The long run at omega0 = 0.9065 will measure it. (vi) The decision to run two picks per beta is consistent with the literature: no source supports ranking by a path-integral estimate across a gap, and the best-by-measured-rate pick is the only one that does not depend on interpolation. At beta = 4/3 the ranking is a near tie set by a floored node (T_death of about 5.2e5 for every start above the dip, `WPA_BUILD_NOTES.md`), so it carries no information either way. (vii) The result also settles the open item of F-004: the path integral reproduces the radial death time only when fed with the run's own Gamma(E), and then only to +31% at beta = 0.85.
- Cross-reference: `PRIOR_ART.md` Addendum B (B1 to B6, N19 to N21), section S7 row A1, S8 item 16, Addendum A5 (the published integral, N11); F-004 (proposed this check); `docs/WPA_BUILD_NOTES.md` sections 2, 3 and 6; `outputs/session2/wpA/validation.json`; SESSION_02 report sections 3, 5 and 8; reviewer finding: see the "Reviewer check" line above.
- Status: explained in outline (cause located by diagnostics i to iii; the integral and the start-energy estimate cleared); rerun not needed. Open: (a) whether a map seeded from evolved states closes the gaps (a method question, not run); (b) the reviewer check.

### F-008  WP-A check f: sponge validation by box doubling at beta = 0.75 gives 3.3e-4 against 1e-4

- Date, session, work package, step: 2026-10-01, Session 2, WP-A build, check f. `CLAUDE.md` standard 4 (absorbing boundaries validated against a box twice the size) with the 1e-4 target on frac set in the WP-A build task (`docs/WPA_BUILD_NOTES.md` section 2, check f).
- Prediction on record: standard 4 and the 1e-4 target above. No row in `SESSION_02_PREDICTIONS.md`.
- Expected value or outcome, with its tolerance: maximum difference in frac of at most 1e-4 over T = 3000 between the working box and a box twice the size, beta = 0.75, alpha^2 = 0.07 (omega = 0.82999).
- Measured value or outcome: radial, dr = 0.05, dt = 0.0125, box 200 with the sponge from 100 against box 400 with the sponge from 200, sponge strength 1.0 with a quadratic ramp. Maximum difference 3.29e-4 at t = 2687.5. While the lump is alive the difference is 3.3e-7: the lump dies at 417.5 in both boxes. The excess appears only after t = 1500, as energy returns from the sponge into the emptied box. Maximum difference by 500-long window: 4.6e-7, 3.3e-6, 3.4e-5, 2.3e-4, 3.0e-4, 3.3e-4. frac in the working box falls to 5.8e-5 at t = 1500 and rises to 3.0e-4 at 2500 (2.6e-4 at 3000); in the doubled box it is 3.3e-6 at 3000. Companion check at beta = 5, same alpha^2, T = 3000 (box 387, doubled to 774): 2.96e-7 and still rising (2.3e-11 to 3e-7 across the 500-long windows). Result file `outputs/session2/wpA/checks.json` (keys `box_doubling_beta0.7500`, `box_doubling_beta5.0000`).
- Size of the miss against the tolerance: 3.3 times the target, just beyond about 3 times; recorded as a miss. It is a difference in a quantity that is itself about 3e-4 of E0 after the lump has gone, so it matters only for what the quantity is used for (see the effect below).
- Ranked causes (orchestrator): (1) Slow near-gap waves returning from the sponge after the lump has dispersed (the F-001 and F-005 class). (2) Nothing else proposed.
- Diagnostics run now: the window breakdown above, which places the excess after t = 1500 and after death; the box doubling at beta = 5.
- Diagnostics proposed (expensive ones), not run: a doubled-box long run at beta = 5 is being run (about 6 minutes per the build notes). F-005's L = 140 twin proposal, which tests the same cause in 3D, is still open.
- What the miss rules in, and what it rules out: Rules in: while the lump is alive the sponge is validated to 3.3e-7, about 300 times below the target. Rules out (orchestrator): any effect on T_death, because runs stop at death. Not ruled out: an effect on late-time frac for runs that stay alive to T = 30,000 at high beta, where the beta = 5 difference is small (3e-7) but still rising at T = 3000; the doubled-box long run decides.
- Literature check (scholar): not needed for this entry and none was run; sources already read. The cause class is the one recorded in F-001 and F-005: long-wavelength modes are damped less efficiently by this kind of sponge and reflect from the far end of the layer, which [Gleiser & Sornborger 2000](https://arxiv.org/abs/patt-sol/9909002) (READ) cure with a larger grid and a later start of damping, the logic of a doubled-box check; [Zhang et al. 2020](https://arxiv.org/abs/2004.01202) footnote 15 (READ) describe slow quanta released as a lump settles; the free 3D Klein-Gordon field decays as t^(-3/2) ([Komech & Kopylova 2010](https://arxiv.org/abs/1003.3799), READ introduction). No source found for a quantitative return time or amplitude for this sponge.
- Interpretation notes (scholar): (i) The shape in the working box, a fall to 5.8e-5 at t = 1500 and then a rise to 3.0e-4 by 2500 while the doubled box stays at 3.3e-6, is the signature of a returning pulse and not of a slowly decaying tail, which would fall monotonically as t^(-3) (F-005). The same non-monotone revival was seen in the 3D twins (F-005), so the L = 140 twin there and the doubled-box long run here test one mechanism at two different settings. (ii) The returned energy is small in absolute terms (about 3e-4 of E0 inside 3 half-widths), so it changes no verdict that depends on frac near 1 or on death. It would matter for a claim that needs frac to 1e-4 at late times in a run that is still alive.
- Cross-reference: F-001 and F-005; `PRIOR_ART.md` section 6, Addendum A1; `docs/WPA_BUILD_NOTES.md` section 2 (check f) and section 6 item 3; `outputs/session2/wpA/checks.json`; SESSION_02 report sections 3 and 8; reviewer finding: none supplied.
- Status: explained (probable cause, same class as F-001 and F-005; no direct diagnostic beyond the timing). Open: the doubled-box long run at beta = 5.


### F-009  WP-C single-lump phase control: prediction C0b misses at theta = 90

- Date, session, work package, step: 2026-10-01, Session 2, WP-C build, item 5 (single-lump phase control, radial solver), Step 3. The control gates the pair runs (`docs/SESSION_02_PLAN.md`, WP-C "Control" bullet and section 4, first stop condition).
- Prediction on record: `docs/SESSION_02_PREDICTIONS.md`, Block 1, WP-C table, row C0b, with the pass criterion fixed above the table: for theta = 45, 90, 135 and 180 degrees against theta = 0, |frac_theta(T) - frac_0(T)| <= 0.01 at T = 300, 500 and 1000, measured omega within 0.005, no death; "a larger difference is a finding and the pair runs wait". Start: phi = Phi(r) cos(theta), phi_t = -omega Phi(r) sin(theta), beta = 4/3, omega = 0.909149. Literature column at the time: a phi_t = 0 single-frequency start is not the quasi-breather (van Dissel 2023, READ); pair runs in the literature use phase-shifted copies (Amin & Mou 2020, Xue 2025, READ); no source for phase dependence of the settling. My own entries were S7 row C0b ("Consistent; size depends on eps, not given in any source") and the derived note N18 (`PRIOR_ART.md`). Both are corrected below.
- Expected value or outcome, with its tolerance: theta = 45, 90, 135 match the theta = 0 lump with differences of order 1e-3 or less (C0b); pass if the differences in frac are at most 0.01 at T = 300, 500, 1000 and omega is within 0.005. C0a: theta = 180 equals theta = 0 to rounding.
- Measured value or outcome, with parameters and the result file: radial solver `swirl.family.radial.Radial`, dr = 0.05, dt = 0.0125, R_box = 200, sponge from 100, T = 1000, frac = E(r < 3 half-widths = 10.03)/E0 of the same run, omega the median core frequency over [T - 50, T]. Files `outputs/session2/wpC/phase_control_radial.json` (runs, verdict) and `phase_control_diagnostics.json` (D1 to D3); scripts beside them; table in `docs/WPC_BUILD_NOTES.md` section 3. Largest difference in frac over T = 300, 500, 1000: theta = 180, 2.9e-15 (C0a hit); 45, 9.3e-4; 135, 1.1e-3; +22.5 / -22.5, 1.9e-3 / 5.5e-4; +67.5 / -67.5, 8.4e-3 / 8.5e-3 (both pass, near the line). **theta = 90: 6.6e-3 / 1.09e-2 / 2.46e-2 at T = 300 / 500 / 1000, and omega off by 6.6e-3 / 6.9e-3 / 7.9e-3.** Settled omega at T = 1000: 0.90967 (theta = 0), 0.90883 (45), 0.91271 (67.5), 0.91760 (90). No run dies. The theta = 90 lump has frac 0.9696 at T = 1000 against 0.9942 for theta = 0.
- Size of the miss against the tolerance: C0a is a hit and C0b is a hit at 45 and 135. At 90 the difference in frac is 0.66, 1.09 and 2.46 times the 0.01 criterion at T = 300, 500, 1000, and omega is 1.3 to 1.6 times the 0.005 (all within about 3 times, which the general rule in `CLAUDE.md` would score as a pass with a note). The predictions file fixed this criterion and states that a larger difference is a finding and the pair runs wait, so it is recorded as a miss, and it is a miss against the predicted size ("order 1e-3 or less") by 6.6 to 25 times. **Block 1's criterion lists theta = 90 among the phases it controls**, and the plan's WP-C run list includes a 90-degree relative phase. The plan's pair data (phases theta1 and theta2) did not fix how the two phases are split, and a split (0, 90) uses a theta = 90 start (`WPC_BUILD_NOTES.md` section 3, "What it means for pairs"). So theta = 90 was a used phase in Block 1's terms, and the control failed at it.
- Ranked causes (orchestrator): (1) The energy of the single-frequency start's t = 0 slice depends on theta (2843.8 at 0, 2861.3 at 22.5, 2878.8 at 45, 2742.1 at 67.5, 2605.2 = omega N at 90), so each start settles onto the family member with its energy; the theta = 90 start is a lump with N 9.5% lower (omega 0.9157) that radiates ten times faster (2.9e-5 against 3.1e-6). (2) A phase-specific instability of the theta = 90 start: ruled out by D2. (3) A solver artefact: unlikely, theta = 180 reproduces theta = 0 to 3e-15.
- Diagnostics run now (three, numerics): D1: every start phase that a symmetric pair design uses passes (the table above). D2: a theta = 0 start of the family profile at the theta = 90 run's settled omega (0.9157, N = 2595, 9.5% below the primary's 2865.5) tracks the theta = 90 run: omega differs by 3.1e-4, 3.3e-4, 3.9e-4 at T = 300, 500, 1000; loss rate over t = 200 to 1000 is 3.0e-5 against 2.9e-5 (theta = 0 primary: 3.1e-6); frac within 1.9e-3. D3: the slice-energy table (`phase_control_diagnostics.json`, key `D3_slice_energy`). Scholar's cheap checks from files already on disk are in the interpretation notes: the slice energy is reproduced from the profile alone to all printed digits and decomposed (N22), and slice energy predicts the pattern of settled omega over all five phases (N23).
- Diagnostics proposed (expensive ones), not run: pair initial data from a true quasi-breather: take a settled 3D single lump at two times separated by dtheta/omega and superpose spectrally shifted copies (`WPC_BUILD_NOTES.md` section 3), which removes the ansatz mismatch. Scholar suggestion (cheap): the quasi-breather start can be built in the radial solver first. Time-shift a settled radial run (snapshot pairs of (phi, phi_t) at t0 and t0 + theta/omega) and repeat the control at theta = 90; the literature supports a time shift as the construction (see the literature check). If the control then passes at 90 with differences of order 1e-3, that confirms the ansatz as the cause.
- What the miss rules in, and what it rules out: Rules in: single-frequency starts at different phases are different lumps; the settled omega over the phases the pair design uses spans 0.9087 to 0.9123 (T = 500: 0.9087 at +-45, 0.9095 at 0 and 180, 0.9093 at +-22.5, 0.9122 at +-67.5), which the pair analysis must carry because it changes kappa and A (at d = 24 a shift of 0.003 in omega moves e^(-kappa d) by up to 18%). Rules out: a phase-specific instability (D2).
- Consequence taken (orchestrator): pair phases are assigned symmetrically, theta = -/+ dtheta/2, and dtheta = 180 uses (0, 180), so no start at theta = 90 is used and every start used passes the criterion in the radial runs. The same control is repeated in 3D as a gate before any pair run. The orchestrator reads the plan's stop condition (section 4: "If WP-C's single-lump phase control fails ... stop after WP-C") as not triggered, because the plan's control is "a single lump started at each phase used behaves like the phase-0 lump" and the phases used now pass. This is a reading of the control, not what Block 1 specified, and it is flagged to Aaron as a deviation. The criterion numbers in Block 1 are unchanged.
- Reviewer check (done 2026-10-01, independent code): the miss is real (dfrac 6.6e-3, 1.09e-2, 2.46e-2; domega 0.0066, 0.0069, 0.0079). The cause is supported more strongly than the build notes show: the slice-energy offset divided by dE/domega = omega dN/domega = -4.08e4 predicts the settled omega shift, -0.00086 / +0.0025 / +0.0059 at 45 / 67.5 / 90 degrees against -0.0008 / +0.0026 / +0.0066 observed. Two further readings from the reviewer: the miss was foreseeable from the t = 0 energy alone (E0(90) = omega N = 2605 against 2801), so prediction C0b was written without the cheapest check (standard 6); and the criterion normalises frac by each run's own E0, so it measures localisation, not lump identity: the +-67.5 lumps pass while being about 4% lighter with omega 0.0027 higher. On the gate reading (control taken over the phases used after the design changed): the reviewer calls it fair, not a rescue, and adds that the underlying finding is unresolved and will bear on WP-E, where stepped-phase rings cannot be paired symmetrically.
- Literature check (scholar): Not seen in this form. No source found that states that a single-frequency ansatz Phi cos(theta), -omega Phi sin(theta) has a phase-dependent slice energy, or that reports where a real-field start at a shifted phase settles. What is documented, by question (searches 88 to 98 and Addendum C in `PRIOR_ART.md`; full table in C1):
  - **How relative phase is imposed in published pair studies.** Complex fields use a U(1) rotation, which is exact: [Battye & Sutcliffe 2000](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-th/0003252) (READ, eq. 3.2 and the text under it) put e^(i alpha) on one Q-ball and note that for a single Q-ball the phase can be set to zero at t = 0 without loss of generality; [Hertzberg, Li, Schiappacasse 2020](https://arxiv.org/abs/2005.02405) (READ, sec. 3.5, eq. 3.25) do the same for a non-relativistic wave function.
