The real-field counterpart of that rotation is a time shift, which is a symmetry of the exact periodic solution and not of the truncated ansatz, so the complex-field papers cannot meet this problem. Battye & Sutcliffe add that their ansatz's total charge depends on alpha through the overlap, so the energy functional cannot be used to compare phases (READ, eq. 3.3 and the text under it); this is the same remark as `WPC_DERIVATION.md` section 2. For real fields: [Amin & Mou 2020](https://arxiv.org/abs/2009.11337) (READ, App. A.3, eqs. A.8 and A.13) start from phi = M f(r) cos(omega t), build the pair from two boosted copies of equal phase and frequency, and cite Hertzberg, Schiappacasse & Yanagida ([arXiv:2001.07476](https://arxiv.org/abs/2001.07476), not opened) for other phases, so no relative-phase start is built there. [Xue et al. 2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.01597) (READ, sec. V) extract oscillons from simulations (full harmonic content) and make the pi case by multiplying the field and its derivative by -1, an exact symmetry; they say the relative phase of mismatched oscillons "cannot easily be set in advance", and (sec. III) that the outcome of a Galilean boost depends on the phase of the oscillon. [Campos, Manton, Mohammadi 2026](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12633) (READ, v1, eq. V.1, 1+1 dimensions) set the relative phase delta as a shift of the time argument of the whole Fodor oscillon (harmonics included, truncated at third order), and state that the Fodor expansion is not an exact solution and that, for two-oscillon evolution, the size of its deviation depends on delta, largest at delta = pi, with more radiation for delta near pi. [Gálvez Ghersi & Braden 2024](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.04750) (READ, eq. 3 and sec. VI) start from the exact sine-Gordon breather at an initial phase theta_0, note that for D = 1 the initial energy is independent of theta_0, and find (Fig. 4 and Fig. 16 caption) that after a dimensional deformation the settled oscillon frequency has cusps centred on theta_0 = pi/2, and that their fitted relations hold for theta_0 other than pi/2; they say the cusps soften as the dimensional transition is made slower (different potential and a deformed dimension; I found no explanation of the cusp in the text). So the real-field constructions that vary the phase are time shifts of full-harmonic data, which is also the project's expensive proposal.
  - **The single-frequency start in the oscillon literature** is used at one phase only. [van Dissel et al. 2023](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.16072) (READ, sec. III) use phi(r, 0) = Phi(r), phi_t = 0, say that true oscillons carry non-negligible higher harmonics, compare the energy of the ansatz with that of the quasi-breather (sine-Gordon, difference small; the text does not say whether that energy is the period average or a slice) and report that quasi-breather starts changed the lifetimes only slightly. No phase scan.
  - **Our N18.** Incomplete, and wrong on the order of the energy: see the interpretation notes and `PRIOR_ART.md` C3.
  - **(b) The phase dynamics.** The pendulum structure is published, but not for real oscillons and not with the family's d omega/dN. (i) Q-balls, complex field: [Battye & Sutcliffe 2000](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-th/0003252) (READ; equations checked on the rendered page 14) write a mechanical model of two equal Q-balls at fixed positions with fixed profile, L = (M/2)(theta1_dot^2 + theta2_dot^2) - epsilon^2 cos(theta1 - theta2) plus a constant (eq. 3.7, M = integral of f^2, epsilon^2 = 4 integral of f f), with equation of motion theta1_ddot - theta2_ddot = (2 epsilon^2/M) sin(theta1 - theta2) (eq. 3.8). They state that for every alpha in (0, pi) the relative phase has an initial positive acceleration (so it moves from 0 toward pi), that the first Q-ball (higher frequency, ahead in phase) loses charge to the second, because charge falls as frequency rises, and that the initial transfer rate is greatest at alpha = pi/2 and falls as alpha falls; the in-phase and anti-phase states are symmetric solutions. This is the project's direction rule (N flows from the phase-leading lump to the lagging one) and its sin(dtheta) dependence, and the sign of its pendulum (unstable at 0; pi is the end point of the motion, implied and not named as stable). They give two limits: the profile is held fixed, and they could not include its frequency dependence (it would give a fourth-order equation of motion). The project's Hamiltonian, with N_i conjugate to theta_i and E_i(N_i) from the family, is the version they say they could not write. They cite an unpublished "effective Hamiltonian for Q-ball dynamics" (their ref. 11, in preparation); no source found that it appeared. (ii) Solitons, conservative: [Malomed 1998](https://arxiv.org/abs/patt-sol/9809009) (READ, concluding remarks) states that the effective mass of the phase degree of freedom is negative, attributing it to Afanasjev, Chu & Malomed, PRE 57, 1088 (not obtained), and uses it to conclude that an orbiting two-soliton state with d^2 E/d psi^2 > 0 is unstable in psi. With a negative mass a minimum of U in psi is unstable and a maximum is stable, so for U proportional to -cos(psi) the in-phase state is unstable and psi = pi is stable (DERIVED here, trivial; the paper states it for the orbiting state and for dissipative bound states, not for a free conservative pair). He gives no pendulum equation and no N exchange. (iii) Bose-Einstein condensates: [Raghavan, Smerzi, Fantoni, Shenoy 1999](https://arxiv.org/abs/cond-mat/9706220) (READ, eqs. 2.6 to 2.8, 4.1, 4.11, 4.12) give the two-mode "non-rigid pendulum", z_dot = -sqrt(1 - z^2) sin(phi), H = Lambda z^2/2 - sqrt(1 - z^2) cos(phi), with pi-phase modes about phi = pi; they treat Lambda > 0 (repulsive). Flipping the sign of Lambda in their linearization, phi_ddot = -[Lambda sin(phi) + sin(2 phi)/2] (eq. 4.11), gives (DERIVED, not in the paper) a stable pi mode for every Lambda < 1 and an unstable zero mode for Lambda < -1. Dropping the non-rigidity term sin(2 phi)/2 (coupling independent of N_i, as in `WPC_DERIVATION.md` section 5, valid for |n| much less than N) leaves the project's pendulum; I did not match constants. [Parker et al. 2008](https://arxiv.org/abs/0712.3002) (READ, 3D Gross-Pitaevskii, bright solitary waves in a waveguide, collisions): the population transferred between two colliding waves varies as sin(delta phi) at the highest speed with its peak at pi/2, and a two-mode Josephson-like model agrees qualitatively and overestimates the amount (context: collisions at fixed speed, not a bound pair). [Burlak & Malomed 2018](https://arxiv.org/abs/1805.09173) (READ, Session 1): N asymmetry up to 0.76 at pi/2 in the 3D cubic-quintic NLS at kappa d = 2.5. (iv) Breathers: [Ahn, MacKay, Sepulchre 2001](https://link.springer.com/chapter/10.1007/978-94-017-2452-4_9) (ABSTRACT, Springer page redirected to a login; abstract read in a search summary) build an effective Hamiltonian on a manifold of periodic orbits that is independent of the overall phase, with critical points at fixed action corresponding to exact periodic solutions. This is the general framework of the project's H(N, theta) for discrete breathers; Battye & Sutcliffe cite it for Q-balls. (v) Not the same mechanism: [Axenides et al. 2000](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-ph/9910388) (READ, sec. III) have the interaction energy oscillating as cos[(omega_+ - omega_-) t] for two Q-balls of different internal frequency (the case studied has opposite rotation), a breathing at the frequency difference and not a libration of the phase about pi; [Copeland, Saffin, Zhou 2014](https://arxiv.org/abs/1409.3232) (READ) give the charge-swapping rate as a slow drift of the relative phase from a small frequency difference. The remark in `WPC_DERIVATION.md` section 9 that the oscillation about pi resembles Axenides et al. can be dropped; I checked and it is a different mechanism. (vi) Real scalar oscillons: no source found for N exchange, a relative-phase pendulum or a stable anti-phase state between two real oscillons in any dimension (searches 47, 59 to 63 in Session 1 and 96 to 98 here). The 1+1D force and phase-dependent outcomes are in Campos et al. 2026 and Xue et al. 2025 above; neither derives N exchange.
  - Verdict: not seen for real fields. The structure (pendulum for the relative phase, N exchange as sin(dtheta), the direction leader to lagger, anti-phase as the end point) is published for Q-balls, solitons and condensates, so it cannot be called new as a structure. What I did not find published is its application to a real scalar field, the use of the exact tail coefficient 2 pi A^2 e^(-kappa d)/d together with d omega/dN taken from the single-frequency family, and any measurement for real oscillons. That is a statement about what these searches returned, not a novelty claim.
- Interpretation notes (scholar): (i) **N18 was incomplete, and wrong on the order of the energy.** It said the theta-shifted start leaves the 3 omega component unshifted (it should be 3 theta), so the mismatch is first order in the 3 omega amplitude ratio eps in the field and second order in the energy. The first half stands. The second half is wrong as a statement about the slice energy: the energy of the slice of the ansatz differs from that of the true quasi-breather slice by the cross term between the fundamental and the omitted harmonics, which is first order, because the quasi-breather is a stationary point of the period-averaged action (equivalently of E - omega N at fixed omega) but not of E on phase space. The analytic form is exact and uses the fundamental alone (N22): E_ans(theta) = E_bar + a2 cos 2theta + a4 cos 4theta + a6 cos 6theta, with E_bar = 2801.683 (the profile's E, 2801.684), a2 = +101.80, a4 = -77.17, a6 = +17.51, where a2 = -(1/4) I3, a4 = (I3 - I5)/8, a6 = I5/12, I_j = integral of Phi R_j, and R3, R5 are the 3 omega and 5 omega parts of the residual of the single-frequency equation (R3 = -g4 Phi^3/24 + g6 Phi^5/384, R5 = g6 Phi^5/1920). Evaluating the slice energy directly from the profile file (`outputs/step2/profiles/profile_beta1.3333_w0.9091.json`) reproduces the five values in D3 to all printed digits (2843.826, 2861.283, 2878.848, 2742.083, 2605.208 for theta = 0, 22.5, 45, 67.5, 90); the amplitudes a_k from the residual agree with the Fourier decomposition of that evaluation to 1e-14 for a4 and a6 and to 1e-5 for a2 (whose linear terms need the profile equation to hold on the finite-difference grid), and E_bar + a_k from the residual give the five energies to within 2e-3. So the 8.4% between theta = 0 and 90 is the 2 omega, 4 omega and 6 omega sloshing of the energy of a truncated solution, generated by the same residual that sources the third harmonic, and it needs no knowledge of Phi_3. It is not a small second-order effect: the 3 omega source at the core is 11% of the linear term (R3(0)/(m^2 Phi0) = 0.111), and the peak-to-peak of E_ans(theta) is 9.8% of E_bar. What was right in N18: the omitted piece is not identical across theta. What I got wrong in S7 row C0b and S8 item 5: the verdict "Consistent" was too weak; the risk was named, the size was not, and the energy of the slice is first order in eps where I wrote second order. (ii) **The pattern of settled omega follows the slice energy over all five phases** (N23, `DERIVED`, `PROJECT DATA`). Take the family's E(omega) at beta = 4/3 (`outputs/family/family_beta1.3333.json`, quartic fit over omega 0.88 to 0.95, dE/domega = -40,741 at omega = 0.9091) and ask which omega has E = E0(theta) with no energy shed. That gives 0.90811, 0.90769, 0.90728, 0.91062, 0.91428 for theta = 0, 22.5, 45, 67.5, 90, against measured settled omega (T = 300) of 0.90952, 0.90923, 0.90875, 0.91215, 0.91611. The differences are +1.41e-3, +1.54e-3, +1.47e-3, +1.53e-3, +1.83e-3. The measured omega spreads over 7.4e-3 across the phases, and the differences spread over 0.4e-3, so about 94% of the phase dependence of settled omega is carried by the slice energy, in sign and size, including the change of sign between 45 and 67.5 (the settled omega is below the profile's 0.90915 at 45 and above it at 67.5 and 90). The constant offset of about +1.5e-3 corresponds to about 60 in energy (2% of E): energy shed while settling, a difference between the single-frequency family's E(omega) and the true quasi-breather's (second order, the sine-Gordon comparison in van Dissel et al. found it small), or excitation of shape modes; this check cannot tell which. It supports cause 1 over a phase-specific instability (a smooth map of five points is not what an instability produces) and extends D2's energy-matching reading from 90 to the other four phases. It does not show that energy is the only variable: the +-22.5 and +-45 pairs differ in omega by 1e-4 for equal slice energy. (iii) **Consequence for the pair runs.** Equal slice energy within a pair (the -/+ assignment) fixes the within-pair mismatch. It does not fix the between-pair spread. Each lump of the batch is a different member of the family: slice energy relative to E_bar is +1.5% at theta = 0 and 180 (the dtheta = 0 pairs use (0, 0) and the dtheta = 180 pairs use (0, 180)), +2.1% at +-22.5, +2.8% at +-45, -2.1% at +-67.5. The dtheta = 135 lumps (+-67.5) are the lightest in the batch and the dtheta = 90 lumps (+-45) the heaviest, so the cosine-law comparison across dtheta carries a known lump-energy spread of -2.1% to +2.8% plus the settled-omega spread of 0.9087 to 0.9123 (N23 predicts each settled omega to about 0.2e-3 after a common offset of 1.5e-3, which is about 1% in e^(-kappa d) at d = 24, against the 18% that the omega spread alone moves it). (iv) **A pass is not "same lump".** The criterion compares frac and omega against theta = 0. At +-67.5 the slice energy is 3.6% below the theta = 0 slice and the lump settles at omega 3.0e-3 higher, yet it passes (8.5e-3 of 1e-2). The control as written catches a gross mismatch (90) and not a few-percent one, and the pair analysis must use each run's measured omega regardless, as it does. (v) **The basis of C0b.** It rested on F-002 D3 (early shedding at beta = 4/3 is 0.03%), which was measured for the theta = 0 start only, and on the premise that a different phase changes little. The shedding is small at every phase; what changes is the energy the lump keeps, which sets which member it becomes. (vi) **Direction of N flow, one check.** My N14 (flow toward the lump with the larger theta in phi = A cos(omega t - theta), the one that peaks later) and the derivation's "from the phase-leading lump to the lagging one" are the same rule. It is also Battye & Sutcliffe's rule (the Q-ball with the higher frequency, ahead in phase, loses charge). (vii) **Mapping of constants not done.** The Battye & Sutcliffe pendulum uses a fixed-profile "moment of inertia" M = integral of f^2 and an overlap coupling, not 1/|d omega/dN| and the tail force; their coupling is an overlap integral of the profiles (the kind of term that dominates the t = 0 energy excess in `WPC_DERIVATION.md` section 2), and the project's U = (E - omega N)_cross is the fixed-omega interaction. The two give the same sign of the acceleration of the relative phase (toward pi) with different magnitudes; I have not matched their normalisations.
- Cross-reference: `PRIOR_ART.md` Addendum C (C1 to C6, derived notes N22 to N24, searches 88 to 98) and S7 row C0b, N18 (correction in C3); `docs/WPC_BUILD_NOTES.md` section 3 and parking lot; `docs/WPC_DERIVATION.md` sections 5, 7 and 9; `docs/SESSION_02_PREDICTIONS.md` Block 1 outcome for the WP-C phase control; `outputs/session2/wpC/phase_control_radial.json`, `phase_control_diagnostics.json`; SESSION_02 report sections 3, 5 and 8; reviewer finding: see the "Reviewer check" line above.
- Status: explained (cause located by D1 to D3 and by the analytic slice energy; the settled-omega pattern over five phases follows it; a phase-specific instability ruled out). The literature has no source for a phase-dependent single-frequency start and constructs real-field relative phase by time shifts of full-harmonic data. Open: (a) the reviewer check; (b) the 3D gate; (c) whether a time-shifted settled lump passes the control at 90 (not run).

### F-010  WP-A prediction A3: the lifetime bands at beta = 0.75, 1.0 and 1.15 are missed by the best-ranked starts

- Date, session, work package, step: 2026-10-02, Session 2, WP-A, first sweep (`outputs/session2/wpA/lifetime_table.json`: 18 long runs, T up to 30,000) and supplement (`lifetime_table_v2.json`: 100 long runs, the 18 plus 82 more, seven continued to T = 300,000).
- Prediction on record: `docs/SESSION_02_PREDICTIONS.md`, Block 1, WP-A table, row A3: "beta = 0.75: T_death between 350 and 600. beta = 1.0: T_death between 3,000 and 12,000. beta = 1.15: T_90 between 3,000 and 15,000". Basis stated there: interpolating Session 1 (344 at 0.70, 576 at 0.80, 1146 at 0.85, alive at 3000 with 0.77 at 1.0). Literature column at the time: "none below 4/3". The row does not say which start the band is for; it sits under A2 ("the best profile") and the first sweep read it that way (the two ranked picks per beta).
- Expected value or outcome, with its tolerance: a T_death or T_90 inside each band (a factor 1.7 wide at 0.75, 4 at 1.0, 5 at 1.15). No other tolerance was written; the general rule of about 3 times is applied to the band edges.
- Measured value or outcome, with parameters and result files: radial solver, dr = 0.05, dt = 0.0125, box 200 with the sponge from 100 at beta of 4/3 or below, single-frequency starts (phi_t = 0), T_death and T_90 as defined in Block 1. First sweep (`lifetime_table.json`, key `rows`; N/N_min in brackets): beta = 0.75: omega0 0.70058 (6.47, best) T_death 868.0, T_90 66.5; 0.70690 (5.92, lowest rate) T_death 784.0, T_90 274.5. beta = 1.0: 0.78780 (6.90, best) T_death 2220.0, T_90 54.5; 0.83836 (2.79, lowest rate) T_death 8867.5, T_90 7966.0. beta = 1.15: 0.79757 (13.33, best) T_90 101.5, no T_death, frac 0.535 at T = 30,000; 0.82027 (6.83, lowest rate) T_90 27,231.0, T_death 28,052.5. Supplement (`lifetime_table_v2.json`, keys `rows`, `per_beta_longest`, `matched_size`): at 1.0 the longest-lived start (omega0 0.77357, N/N_min 9.61) has T_90 28,930.5 and T_death 29,404.5, the next two T_death 27,962.5 (0.77501, 9.27) and 22,161.0 (0.83704, 2.85); at 0.85 the best is T_death 3380.0 (0.75874); at 1.15 a start at alpha^2 = 0.0805 (omega0 0.87560, N/N_min 2.25) is alive at T = 30,000 with frac 0.9941, and omega0 0.87387 and 0.86895 are alive with 0.9735 and 0.9069. Matched N/N_min = 1.3, 1.8, 2.5: T_death 392.0, 435.0, 442.0 at beta = 0.75; 1340.0, 1596.5, 1078.0 at 1.0; 3138.5, 4232.0, alive (frac 0.9069) at 1.15 (T_90 387.5, 480.5, not reached).
- Size of the miss against the tolerance: 0.75: 868 and 784 are 1.45 and 1.31 times the upper edge of 600. 1.0: the lowest-rate pick (8867.5) is inside the band; the best pick (2220) is below it, 1.35 times short of 3000; the supplement's longest (29,404.5) is 2.45 times the upper edge of 12,000, the next two 2.33 and 1.85 times. 1.15: 27,231 is 1.82 times the upper edge of 15,000, and four starts have no T_90 by 30,000 (at least 2.0 times, true values unmeasured). Every measured case is within about 3 times, a pass with a note by the general rule. It is recorded as a miss because A3 was written as an interval, because the best-ranked start falls outside it at 0.75, 1.0 and 1.15, and because the censored values at 1.15 are lower bounds. Census of the long runs against the band: 0.75, 4 of 11 inside (392 to 464.5), 2 above, 5 below (deep starts, 89 to 119); 1.0, 1 of 14 inside, 3 above, 10 below; 1.15, 1 of 10 inside (7416), 5 above or alive, 4 below.
- Ranked causes (orchestrator): (1) The prediction interpolated the lifetimes of moderate-size starts, but the longest-lived starts are much larger lumps deeper on the stable branch (N/N_min of 4 to 13) or sit in rate dips that the Session 1 scan did not sample. (2) Lifetime depends on the start far more than assumed: at beta = 1.15 neighbouring starts 0.8772, 0.8739, 0.8756 give death at 10,712, alive, alive. (3) An artefact: ruled out (doubled box 6.6e-6 at beta = 5 and 5.3e-6 at 4/3; the driver reproduces Session 1).
- Diagnostics run now (orchestrator): the matched-size runs above, which put the 0.75 starts inside the band (392, 435, 442) and show the band was right for the kind of start it was built from there and wrong for "the best profile"; the near-twin starts at 1.15; the doubled-box checks (`box_check_beta5_long.json`: 6.58e-6 at T = 30,000, beta = 5; `box2_beta1.3333_w0.906505_T300k.json`: 5.3e-6 at 4/3, T = 300,000).
- Diagnostics proposed (expensive ones), not run: none supplied. Scholar suggestions (cheap, from the table): (a) quote a distribution per beta (minimum, median, maximum over a fixed grid of starts) instead of one band for one start; at 1.0 the 9 starts with N/N_min up to 10 give 1078 to 29,404. (b) A start grid finer than the dip width: at 1.15 the near-twins differ by 0.0017 in omega0 and by a factor of 30 in first-window rate (9.1e-8 against 2.7e-6), so a grid of 0.002 to 0.005 samples a dip by luck.
- What the miss rules in, and what it rules out: Rules in: at one beta the lifetime of starts of similar size spans more than a decade (1.0: 1078 to 29,404; 1.15: 387 to above 30,000), so an interpolated band for "the best start" has no basis; the best-ranked start of the first sweep was not the longest-lived at 0.85 (T_90), 1.0 and 1.15. Rules out: a solver or box artefact (cause 3).
- Also recorded here (orchestrator): the first sweep's two-pick selection missed the longest-lived start, as the reviewer warned. At 1.0 by a factor of 3.3 in T_death (8867.5 against 29,404.5) and 3.6 in T_90 (7966.0 against 28,930.5); at 0.85 by 1.02 in T_death (3327.0 against 3380.0) and 5.2 in T_90 (471.5 against 2468.5); at 1.15 the longest-lived starts are alive at 30,000 and the picks were not (T_90 27,231.0). At 0.75 the picks found the longest (868.0).
- Reviewer check (done 2026-10-02, the reviewer of the session report, from `lifetime_table.json`, `lifetime_table_v2.json` and the long-run files): agrees. Recomputed: beta 0.75 T_death 868.0 and 784.0; beta 1.0 first-sweep picks 2220.0 and 8867.5, supplement 29,404.5, 27,962.5 and 22,161.0, factors 3.32 (T_death) and 3.63 (T_90); beta 1.15 first-sweep pick T_90 27,231.0 and four supplement starts alive at 30,000, so the true T_90 at 1.15 is above the cap and the miss is larger than the 27,231 figure suggests; matched-size values 392 / 435 / 442, 1340 / 1596.5 / 1078, 3138.5 / 4232 / alive.
- Literature check (scholar): Partly seen. The mechanism (the lifetime of a start depends on which dips it meets, and neighbouring starts land on different attractors) is documented at beta = 4/3 and in other potentials. The size below 4/3, and lifetime against beta in this model, are not: no source found (searches 29, 32 and 70 in Session 1 and 100 here), and no source found for interpolating lifetimes between parameter values. (1) Start dependence at fixed parameters, beta = 4/3. [Mukaida et al. 2017](https://arxiv.org/abs/1612.07750) (READ, sec. 3.1 and Fig. 4, figure read to about 0.1 dex) start Gaussian data at R_ini = 10, 7 and 5: the first two sit at log10 Q of about 2.55 and 2.15 at T = 1e5 with no death, the third dies at about 1e4; the text says a start below an attractor skips it. [Ibe et al. 2019](https://arxiv.org/abs/1901.06130) (READ, sec. IV and Fig. 5, 6) find starts at omega_ini = 0.850, 0.858 and 0.908 following one analytic Gamma(Q) curve and omega_ini = 0.910 dying at about 1e4. [Li, Yamaguchi, Zhang 2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.13276) (READ, sec. 2.3 and Fig. 3) run omega_ini = 0.85, 0.90, 0.91: the later part of one curve joins the start of the next, the 0.91 start dies at mt of about 2.1e4, the 0.90 start is alive at the panel edge (mt of about 5e5), the 0.85 start leaves its platform at about 1.7e5 and joins the 0.90 level (figure readings, about 10%). So the published picture at 4/3 is what the supplement shows: lifetime is set by where on one path a start begins, and a larger lump can outlive a moderate one by orders of magnitude. The 0.85 start has N/N_min of about 6.5 (DERIVED: N = 525 at omega = 0.85 over N_min = 80.3, Li Fig. 1, `PRIOR_ART.md` section 2.4). (2) Lifetime against a start parameter in other potentials: lifetime has fine structure with resonance spikes against the initial radius ([Honda & Choptuik 2002](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-ph/0110065), READ, phi^4 double well); [Gleiser & Krackow 2019](https://arxiv.org/abs/1906.04070) (ABSTRACT; potential not read) report, for 3D Gaussian starts, lifetime spikes conjectured to be infinite and say their results support that; [Zhang et al. 2020](https://arxiv.org/abs/2004.01202) (READ, footnote 16 and sec. 8) say the resonant configurations of the earlier work lived about 10% longer and that their own decay-rate calculation gives no support for infinite lifetimes. (3) Lifetime across a potential parameter: [Cyncynates & Giurgica-Tiron 2021](https://arxiv.org/abs/2104.02069) (READ, `PRIOR_ART.md` S3.1 and S3.3) find islands of longevity with orders-of-magnitude jumps between neighbouring potentials; [Drogosz et al. 2026](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23616) (READ, different potential) a multi-peak lifetime against a sextic coupling. A lifetime interpolated between parameter values therefore has no published basis, and the published landscape is not smooth. (4) Selection of starts: stitching runs from several omega_ini is the published practice at 4/3 (Li 2025, above), and `PRIOR_ART.md` Addendum B3 records that no source supports ranking by an estimate across a gap. Searches 99 to 101 and Addendum D in `PRIOR_ART.md`.
- Interpretation notes (scholar): (i) **The basis of A3 mixed start sizes, and the matched-size check supports the band only at 0.75.** From the family files (`outputs/family/family_beta*.json`, PROJECT DATA, DERIVED N26): the 344 at 0.70 and the 576 at 0.80 are the D3 starts at N/N_min = 2.5; the 1146 at 0.85 is the omega = 0.7793 profile with N/N_min = 3.83; the "alive at 3000 with 0.77" at 1.0 is the E/(mN) = 0.90 profile (omega = 0.8186, `outputs/family/lifetime_scan.json`) with N/N_min = 3.82. So the lower edge of 3000 at 1.0 came from a start of N/N_min 3.8, and the matched starts at N/N_min 1.3 to 2.5 die at 1078 to 1597, below that edge by 1.9 to 2.8 times. At 1.15 the band is for T_90, and the matched T_90 are 387.5 and 480.5 (alive with frac 0.907 at 2.5); the matched T_death of 3138.5 and 4232.0 fall inside the numerical range of the band but are a different measure. So "the band was right for the kind of start it was built from" holds at 0.75 and not at 1.0 or 1.15. (ii) **Size does not order lifetime at fixed beta.** At 1.0, T_death against N/N_min: 29,404.5 (9.61), 27,962.5 (9.27), 2220.0 (6.90), 22,161.0 (2.85), 8867.5 (2.79), 1988.0 (2.69), 1078.0 (2.50), 1596.5 (1.80), 1340.0 (1.30). A start of N/N_min 2.85 outlives one of 6.90 by 10 times and one of 2.69 by 11 times. Large size helps on average (the two longest are 9 to 10) and does not decide. This is the dip picture, with the dip narrower than the start spacing. (iii) **The near-twins at 1.15 repeat F-007.** omega0 0.87560 (alive, frac 0.9941) and 0.87719 (dies at 10,711.5) differ by 0.0017, and their first-window rates by a factor of 30; the F-007 pair at 4/3 differed by three decades in rate over 8% in E. (iv) **Was it anticipated?** Partly. S7 row A3 said "rests on nothing found" and Addendum B3 recorded that starts land on different attractors and that dips carry the lifetime, which makes a band built from one kind of start unreliable. What the briefs did not say is that the published 4/3 runs (Mukaida, Li) already show a factor of 10 or more in lifetime between starts. (v) **The selection miss** is the same failure as F-007 seen from the other side: F-007 found that a map built from short runs has gaps; here the ranked picks, which came from the map, missed the longest-lived start at three betas.
- Cross-reference: `PRIOR_ART.md` Addendum D (D1 to D6, N26 and N27, searches 99 to 101), Addendum B (F-007), S3.1 to S3.4 and S7 rows A2 to A8; F-007 (the path-integral ranking and the map gaps); F-004 and F-002 (the Session 1 points of the basis); `docs/WPA_BUILD_NOTES.md` "Review corrections" (the two-pick warning); SESSION_02 report sections 3, 5 and 8; reviewer finding: see the "Reviewer check" line above.
- Status: explained in outline (the interpolation mixed start sizes; lifetime at one beta spans more than a decade across starts; artefacts excluded). The matched-size diagnostic supports the band at 0.75 only. Open: the reviewer check; a fine omega0 scan at 1.0 and 1.15 to find where the long-lived starts end (not run).

### F-011  WP-A prediction A6: the beta = 4/3 start at omega 0.906505 does not fall below 0.90 by T = 300,000

- Date, session, work package, step: 2026-10-02, Session 2, WP-A supplement, stage S6 (the best starts continued to T = 300,000).
- Prediction on record: `docs/SESSION_02_PREDICTIONS.md`, Block 1b, row A6: "beta = 4/3, start omega 0.9065: T_90 falls between 50,000 and 300,000; the lump is not permanent". Basis stated: measured rate at or below 2e-8 now, but the lump drifts out of its dip (the F-004 mechanism). Literature column at the time: published beta = 4/3 lifetimes are 1e4 to 1e5, and Mukaida 2017 ran to 1e5 without death (READ).
- Expected value or outcome, with its tolerance: T_90 (first time frac = E(r < 3 half-widths)/E0 falls below 0.90) between 50,000 and 300,000; no death.
- Measured value or outcome, with parameters and result files: radial, beta = 4/3, omega0 = 0.906505 (0.9065045), dr = 0.05, dt = 0.0125, box 200 with the sponge from 100, T = 300,000 (43,300 oscillations); `outputs/session2/wpA/long_beta1.3333_w0.906505_T300k.json` and `lifetime_table_v2.json`, key `S6`. frac = 0.99673, 0.99702, 0.99569, 0.99460 at T = 30k, 100k, 200k, 300k (0.9973 at 1000; minimum over the series 0.99395); omega 0.90689, 0.90691, 0.90696, 0.90707 at the same times; windowed Gamma 3.3e-9 (0 to 30k), 4.2e-9 (30k to 100k), 6.9e-9 (100k to 200k), 1.67e-8 (200k to 300k), each with a per-window error of about 1.7e-9. The doubled box (R_box 400, sponge from 200) agrees to 5.3e-6 over the 300,000 (largest at t = 2200, falling to 3.4e-8 by 250k to 300k; `box2_beta1.3333_w0.906505_T300k.json`). The start energy E0 is 2955.9, which is the slice energy of a theta = 0 start (F-009: Ebar x 1.015).
- Size of the miss against the tolerance: no T_90 by T = 300,000, so the measured value is above the upper edge of 300,000; its size is not measured. The lump has lost 0.54% in 43,300 oscillations. Rough extrapolations in the notes (N28, DERIVED, low confidence) put T_90 between 4e5 and 7e5, 1.3 to 2.3 times the edge, if the rate keeps rising as observed (a pass with a note by the general rule if so), and at about 6e6 if it stayed at its last-window value, which the rising rate excludes. The Session 1 primary (omega 0.9091, 0.0026 higher) fell below 0.90 at 10,630.5.
- Ranked causes (orchestrator): (1) The prediction assumed the lump would leave its dip on the F-004 time scale; it leaves at a rate set by its own Gamma, which is 150 to 700 times smaller than the primary's 3e-6, so the drift is that much slower. (2) The rate is rising (fourfold between the two windows), so the miss is in the time scale and not evidence of permanence. Whether the lump dies is not settled by this run.
- Diagnostics run now (orchestrator): the windowed Gamma in four blocks; the doubled box to T = 300,000.
- Diagnostics proposed (expensive ones), not run: continue this start beyond 3e5 (the notes put T_90 between 4e5 and 7e5); a second start at the same dip on a different grid; the same continuation at 1.6 and 2.0, where the rate is rising (3.4e-8, fourfold from 9.6e-9) or flat (5.1e-9). Scholar suggestion (cheap): none beyond reading Gamma(E) of this run against the 4/3 map nodes already on disk, which F-007 left uncovered across 2718 to 2838; this run samples exactly that gap.
- What the miss rules in, and what it rules out: Rules in: a beta = 4/3 lump can lose 0.5% in 43,300 oscillations at a rate of 3e-9 to 2e-8, so the 4/3 lifetime in this model is not capped near 1e5 by radiation. Rules out: that the dip is left on the F-004 time scale (the rate rose fourfold in 200,000 against 20 times in 1000 for F-004 at beta = 0.85), and a box artefact (5.3e-6). It does not rule out a finite lifetime.
- Reviewer check (done 2026-10-02, the reviewer of the session report): agrees. frac 0.99673, 0.99702, 0.99569, 0.99460 at 30k, 100k, 200k, 300k (minimum 0.99395); omega 0.906891 to 0.907066; rate blocks 3.30e-9, 4.16e-9, 6.92e-9, 1.67e-8, each with error about 1.66e-9; doubled box 5.29e-6 at t = 2200. An exponential extrapolation of the rate gives T_90 of about 7e5. The start rate is 3.3e-9, not the 4e-9 quoted in the orchestrator's text.
- Literature check (scholar): Seen in structure, and in magnitude only partly; and the literature column of A6 understated it. No source found for a measured Gamma below about 5e-9 at beta = 4/3, and none for a lifetime measured beyond about 5e5. All figure readings below are `READ (figure)` with the reading error given; the conversions to this project's units are DERIVED (N25: lambda E/m of Li equals E/80 in Set-B-like units, Q of Ibe equals N/20, both fixed by Li's lambda N = 20.07 at the energy minimum and checked against Li's initial energies). (1) Rate at the dips. [Ibe et al. 2019](https://arxiv.org/abs/1901.06130) Fig. 4 (right panel, analytic Gamma against Q0, beta = 4/3): the first dip has its lowest plotted point at about 9e-8 at log10 Q0 = 2.143 (reading error about 30% in Gamma and 0.01 dex in Q0), the second about 5e-7 at 2.58, the third about 1e-6 at 2.94. The text says the rate is not exactly zero at the zeros because of other modes (psi_50, the 5 omega channel) and that Gamma below about 1e-7 cannot be shown by their simulations (t = 1e5). [Li, Yamaguchi, Zhang 2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.13276) Fig. 3 (right panel, beta = 4/3): the semi-analytic curve has its first dip at lambda E/m of about 34.0 with a lowest plotted point of about 4.5e-8, the second near 88 at about 2.5e-7; the numerical curve (the omega_ini = 0.90 run) leaves the bottom of the panel, about 5e-9, at lambda E/m of about 36.9 (reading error about 0.5 within the panel; but the high-energy end of the green numerical curve is at about 36.5 in this panel while the same run starts at about 35.0 in the left panel, a 4% difference I could not reconcile, so I take about 1.5 as the uncertainty in lambda E/m), and the 0.85 run has its numerical second dip at about 96 with about 6e-8. [Mukaida et al. 2017](https://arxiv.org/abs/1612.07750) (READ, sec. 2 text and Fig. 2) say that in their toy calculation the plotted rate (-dQ/dt, lower right panel) is typically of order 1e-2 and as small as 1e-8 at the attractor points, still nonzero because of the six-point interaction; this is a rate of change of Q and not Gamma = |dE/dt|/E, so I do not compare it directly (at Q of about 10^2.2 a -dQ/dt of 1e-8 would be a relative rate of about 6e-11). [Zhang et al. 2020](https://arxiv.org/abs/2004.01202) (READ, secs. 7.5 and 8): for the phi^6 potential the analytic dips are shifted against the numerics and the over-estimate in the dips prevents accurate lifetime estimates; no numbers. So the measured 4.2e-9 and 1.7e-8 are below the lowest analytic dip points plotted (9e-8 in Ibe, 4.5e-8 in Li): 4.2e-9 by factors of 21 and 11, 1.7e-8 by factors of 5 and 3. They are of the size of the floor of Li's numerical panel, and no published number lies below that. The analytic minima are plotted points on a finite grid in Q or E, so they bound the true minima from above and nothing more. (2) Lifetime beyond 3e5. [Li et al. 2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.13276) Fig. 3 (left panel): the omega_ini = 0.90 run is plotted with no death to the right edge, mt of about 5e5, and the 0.85 run joins the same level at about 1.7e5 and also survives to the edge, so the paper's own data show a beta = 4/3 lump alive at 5e5 from a start 0.0065 below ours in omega; their text gives no lifetime number and says they chose different starts instead of running very long. [Mukaida et al.](https://arxiv.org/abs/1612.07750) simulate to 1e5 only (R_ini = 10 and 7 alive); their toy-model time evolution (Fig. 3, g3 = 0, g4 = 1, g6 = 0.4, Gaussian profile, perturbative rate) sits at log10 Q = 2.24 until log10 t of about 8 and decays at log10 t of about 8.8 (reading error about 0.1 dex), a prediction of order 1e8 to 1e9 and not a simulation. [Ibe et al.](https://arxiv.org/abs/1901.06130) simulate to 1e5 and give a lifetime of about 1e4 for Q0 of about 10^2.1 (their omega_ini = 0.910 run dies at about 1e4). Mukaida, Ibe and Li are the same potential (V = phi^2/2 - phi^4/4 + (0.4/6) phi^6 in Mukaida's units, DERIVED mapping to beta = 4/3 from g4 = 6, g6 = 48 in Ibe's). The brief's lifetimes "1e4 to 1e5" (`PRIOR_ART.md` S3.1, S5) took the published lifetimes as upper figures and did not read Li's Fig. 3 to its right edge: that was my omission, and it is why A6's literature column did not warn that a start in the dip survives past 1e5. (3) Where the dip is. Session 1 matched the primary to the Ibe dip at log10 Q = 2.14 (F-002). Mapping through the family at 4/3 (N25): Ibe's analytic dip at log10 Q0 = 2.143 corresponds to omega = 0.9111 and Li's semi-analytic dip at lambda E/m = 34.0 to omega = 0.9112 (two independent readings, same place); Li's numerical dip at 36.9 to omega = 0.9056 (about 8% higher in energy than the analytic one, uncertain by about 4%, N25). The measured energy of this lump (in the 3 half-width ball) is 0.9973 x 2955.9 = 2948, which is lambda E/m = 36.85, the position of Li's numerical dip (36.9). The primary (E0 = 2843.8, lambda E/m = 35.5) lies between the two on the numerical dip's low-energy wall. So a source does place a dip where this start sits (Li Fig. 3, numerical curve), and the analytic dip of Ibe and Li is at omega 0.911, in line with Zhang et al.'s statement that analytic dips shift against numerics. (4) Permanence: no source found for a permanent beta = 4/3 oscillon. [Zhang et al. 2020](https://arxiv.org/abs/2004.01202) (READ, sec. 8) say that even where one radiation channel vanishes the multiples of that frequency do not, so no lifetime is infinite; [Gleiser & Krackow 2019](https://arxiv.org/abs/1906.04070) (ABSTRACT; potential not read) claim support for infinite lifetimes of resonant configurations; the two statements disagree and neither is about this potential. Searches 83 to 87 (Addendum B), 99 to 101 and Addendum D. No novelty is claimed: what the search record shows is that no source reports a measured rate below about 5e-9 or a lifetime beyond about 5e5 for this potential.
- Interpretation notes (scholar): (i) **The prediction failed in time scale, and the literature already showed the time scale.** Li's omega_ini = 0.90 curve (alive at 5e5) and ours (alive at 3e5 with 0.5% lost) are the same behaviour in the same potential. A6's literature column cited only the 1e5 horizon of Mukaida's runs. (ii) **The lump sits at the numerical dip bottom, not on a wall.** Its true energy (lambda E/m = 36.85) matches Li's numerical dip (36.9, uncertain by about 4% in the figure), and this project's own rates fix the bottom near 36.85 independently: Gamma is 3e-9 there and 3e-6 for the primary at 35.5, a factor 1000 over 4% in energy, so the primary sits on the low-energy wall, where energy only falls, and cannot return to the bottom. That is why the primary's Gamma is 3e-6 and this lump's is 4e-9. (iii) **The same slice-energy point as F-009.** E0 = 2955.9 is the theta = 0 slice energy (1.5% above the family's period-averaged 2912.9 at omega = 0.9065); the lump keeps it (frac 0.9973 at 1000) and settles at omega 0.9069 where the family's E is 2896, an offset of about 2%, the same size as the constant offset in the F-009 check (N23). Comparisons of position in omega between a measured lump and Li's or Ibe's analytic curves carry this offset; comparisons in energy do not. (iv) **A crude estimate of T_90** (N28, `DERIVED`, order of magnitude only). Write x = ln(E/E_bottom), so that dx/dt = -Gamma, and read Gamma(x) off this run: the block rates integrate to x - x0 = -1.0e-4, -3.9e-4, -1.08e-3, -2.75e-3 at 30k, 100k, 200k, 300k (sums of Gamma times duration, matching ln frac = -0.0027 at 300k; x0 is the unknown offset of the start from the bottom, taken as 0). Three extrapolations from T = 300,000, with 0.1 more to lose in ln E: (a) a parabolic dip, Gamma = Gamma_floor + a x^2 with Gamma_floor = 3.3e-9, fitted to the last two blocks (a = 6.6e-3 and 3.6e-3) or to the primary's wall (a = 2.0e-3 if the bottom is at lambda E/m = 36.9 and the primary at 35.5), gives T_90 = 3.9e5 to 4.6e5 for a between 4e-3 and 2e-3; (b) a power law fitted to the three blocks, Gamma - Gamma_floor proportional to |x|^1.3, gives 5.9e5, which probably overestimates because it under-predicts the primary's wall rate by about 5 times; (c) an exponential in time with the observed factor of 2.4 per 100,000 gives 7.0e5. Holding Gamma at 1.67e-8 would give 6e6, which the rising rate excludes. So T_90 is probably 4e5 to 7e5, and A6 missed from above by a factor of 1.3 to 2.3 if so. The fits depend on Gamma_floor, which is at the detection floor (2 sigma of 3.3e-9), and on the shape of the dip, which the data fix over only a factor 3 in x; this is a bracket and not a prediction for the record. (v) **What F-007 left open is now sampled**: the 4.3% gap at E = 2718 to 2838 on the 4/3 map holds this lump's Gamma, the 3e-9 to 5e-9 that F-007 read as below the floor at omega0 = 0.9065. (vi) **Gamma rising 4.0 times** over 200,000 with omega rising 0.9069 to 0.9071 is the published pattern of a lump leaving a dip (Zhang 2020b sec. 2.1, READ; Li 2025 sec. 2.3, READ); the numbers have no published counterpart.
- Cross-reference: `PRIOR_ART.md` Addendum D (D1 to D6, N25 and N28, searches 99 to 101), S3.1, Addendum B (F-007, B1 to B6); F-004, F-007, F-009 (slice energy); `outputs/session2/wpA/long_beta1.3333_w0.906505_T300k.json`, `box2_beta1.3333_w0.906505_T300k.json`, `lifetime_table_v2.json`; SESSION_02 report sections 3, 5 and 8; reviewer finding: see the "Reviewer check" line above.
- Status: explained in outline (time scale; the rate is set by the lump's own Gamma, and the lump sits at the dip bottom). Open: whether the lump dies (not settled; the bracket of 4e5 to 7e5 for T_90 is unverified); the reviewer check.

### F-012  WP-A prediction A8: at matched size the time to fall below 0.90 is not monotone in beta

- Date, session, work package, step: 2026-10-02, Session 2, WP-A supplement, matched-size starts (`lifetime_table_v2.json`, key `matched_size`).
- Prediction on record: `docs/SESSION_02_PREDICTIONS.md`, Block 1b, row A8: "At matched N/N_min = 1.3, 1.8 and 2.5 the lifetime rises monotonically with beta". Basis: Session 1 D3 scan, monotone to T = 600. Literature column: no source.
- Expected value or outcome, with its tolerance: at each N/N_min, T_death and T_90 rise with beta over 0.75, 0.85, 1.0, 1.15, 4/3, 1.6, 2.0, 3.0, 5.0 (a start alive at the cap counts as longest).
- Measured value or outcome: radial, T up to 30,000, files `long_beta*_w*.json` and the table above. T_death is monotone in all three curves within the cap. T_90 is not. N/N_min = 1.3: 36, 62, 144, 387.5, 1508, 16,671, then alive (frac 0.9868, 0.9913, 0.9920 at 2.0, 3.0, 5.0): monotone. N/N_min = 1.8: 19, 32, 96, 480.5, 29,395.5 (beta = 4/3), 14,990.5 (1.6), 15,439.5 (2.0), alive (frac 0.9978 at 3.0 and 5.0). N/N_min = 2.5: 18.5, 35, 366, then at beta = 1.15 alive with frac 0.9069, 861 at 4/3, 1412 at 1.6, 24,798 at 2.0, alive at 3.0 and 5.0 (0.9920, 0.9995).
- Size of the miss against the tolerance: at 1.8 the T_90 at beta = 1.6 is 0.51 of that at 4/3 (a fall by 2.0 times) and the value at 2.0 is 1.03 of the 1.6 one; at 2.5 the T_90 at 4/3 is below 861/30,000 = 0.029 of the 1.15 value (a fall of about 35 times or more, the 1.15 value being a lower bound). The monotone-in-T_90 prediction fails for two of the three ratios, and by more than 3 times at 2.5.
- Ranked causes (orchestrator): (1) T_90 at a fixed N/N_min is set by where that start falls relative to the rate dips and peaks, which move with beta, so a fixed size ratio samples a dip at one beta and a peak at the next. (2) T_death integrates over the whole path and so stays monotone. No diagnostic beyond the table was run.
- Diagnostics run now (orchestrator): none beyond the table.
- Diagnostics proposed (expensive ones), not run: none supplied. Scholar suggestion (cheap, from the table; done in the notes): compare T_90 at each matched start with the first-window rate and with the distance to the first dip in N/N_min.
- What the miss rules in, and what it rules out: Rules in: the fixed-size comparison across beta is a comparison of where one line in N/N_min crosses a moving dip, not of the lifetime of a family. Rules out: that T_90 at fixed N/N_min is a usable ordering of beta by lifetime (it is for T_death below the cap and for N/N_min = 1.3).
- Reviewer check (done 2026-10-02, the reviewer of the session report): agrees. All 24 matched-size entries match the table rows. The 1.8 line at beta 4/3 is start 0.907518 (N/N_min 1.80, T_90 29,395.5), next to the surviving 0.906505 (1.83).
- Literature check (scholar): Anticipated in structure, not in this form. [Cyncynates & Giurgica-Tiron 2021](https://arxiv.org/abs/2104.02069) (READ; `PRIOR_ART.md` S3.3) describe a dip in the radiated power that migrates to higher frequency as a potential parameter changes, with lifetime rising while geometric decoupling enhances the dip and then falling at a cliff when the dip passes the frequency of energetic death; [Zhang 2020b](https://arxiv.org/abs/2011.11720) (READ, sec. 2.1) say a lump that starts below the dip frequency spends most of its life in the dip, and one above skips it; [Li et al. 2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.13276) (READ, sec. 2.3) describe the platforms of the path as the dips. A fixed size ratio crossing a migrating dip is what these pictures imply, and non-monotonic lifetime at a fixed non-tuned start is therefore expected. No source found for lifetime at a fixed N/N_min, or at any fixed start prescription, against a potential parameter, and none that states whether it is monotone (Session 1 searches 29, 32, 70; here 100).
- Interpretation notes (scholar): (i) **The table supports cause 1 quantitatively** (N27, `DERIVED`, `PROJECT DATA`). At beta = 1.15, 4/3, 1.6 and 2.0 the lowest-first-window-rate start with N/N_min of 2.5 or less (the first dip as sampled) is at N/N_min = 2.25 (omega0 0.87560, rate 9.1e-8), 1.83 (0.90650, 3.4e-9), 1.48 (0.93498, 9.3e-9) and 1.21 to 1.23 (omega0 0.95844, 0.95902, 0.95960; rates 3.0e-9, 4.9e-11, 4.1e-9), so the first dip moves down in N/N_min as beta rises, while the three lines are fixed. The distance from each line to the first dip, and T_90: beta = 1.15: 0.95 (1.3 line, T_90 387.5), 0.45 (1.8, 480.5), 0.25 (2.5, alive). beta = 4/3: 0.53 (1.3, 1508), 0.03 (1.8, 29,395.5), 0.67 (2.5, 861). beta = 1.6: 0.18 (1.3, 16,671), 0.32 (1.8, 14,990.5), 1.02 (2.5, 1412). beta = 2.0: 0.08 (1.3, alive), 0.58 (1.8, 15,439.5), 1.28 (2.5, 24,798). T_90 falls with the distance in every row except the 2.5 line at 2.0, which sits 0.31 from a second dip at N/N_min = 2.81 (omega0 0.92482, rate 1.4e-9). Positions are the lowest-rate starts of a sampled grid (spacing 0.002 to 0.005 in alpha^2), not a scan, and there are several dips per beta from 1.6 up; this is a consistency check on cause 1, not a measurement of dip positions. (ii) **The first-window rate is itself non-monotone in beta along the 1.8 and 2.5 lines**, and T_90 follows it. N/N_min = 1.8: 2.8e-4 (beta = 1.0), 2.9e-4 (1.15), 5.1e-7 (4/3), 1.5e-5 (1.6), 8.1e-6 (2.0), 4.0e-9 (3.0), 1.9e-10 (5.0). N/N_min = 2.5: 3.2e-5 (1.15), 9.5e-5 (4/3), 7.5e-5 (1.6), 2.5e-6 (2.0), 2.5e-7 (3.0), 6.6e-10 (5.0). N/N_min = 1.3: 1.2e-4 (1.0), 1.4e-4 (1.15), 6.4e-5 (4/3), 4.2e-6 (1.6), 1.9e-7 (2.0), 5.4e-8 (3.0), 8.1e-11 (5.0), monotone, as T_90. The non-monotone ordering is therefore already in the instantaneous rate at the start of the run and does not need a long-time argument. (iii) **T_death is monotone only because it is censored.** At N/N_min = 1.8 and 2.5 every start at beta of 4/3 or above (1.8) and 1.15 or above (2.5) is alive at 30,000, and at 1.3 every start from 1.6 up; T_death is ordered over 3 to 5 values of beta per curve and says nothing above. A8's "lifetime" was not defined as T_90 or T_death in the row. (iv) **The basis of A8 could not discriminate.** In the Session 1 D3 scan (T = 600, F-002) every start at beta of 0.85 and above was still alive, so only the ordering of retained fraction was available, and an ordering of death times was not.
- Cross-reference: `PRIOR_ART.md` Addendum D (D1 to D6, N27), S3.3 and S3.4 (N17); F-002 (D3, the Session 1 matched scan); F-010 (the same dip-spacing problem for the best start); `outputs/session2/wpA/lifetime_table_v2.json`; SESSION_02 report sections 3, 5 and 8; reviewer finding: see the "Reviewer check" line above.
- Status: explained in outline (cause 1, supported by the distance table; cause 2 holds as censoring). Open: a scan of the first dip position against beta (not run); the reviewer check.

**Outcomes of the other WP-A predictions in Block 1 and Block 1b (no miss; one line each, numbers from `lifetime_table.json`, `lifetime_table_v2.json`, key `S6`):**

- **A2 (best-profile lifetime rises with beta, no fall to beta = 5): hit to the cap.** No best start above beta = 4/3 died by T = 30,000; at T = 300,000 frac is 0.9884 (beta 1.6), 0.9882 (2.0), 0.9842 and 0.9694 (3.0, two starts), 0.9984 and 0.9686 (5.0, two starts). The monotone ordering itself cannot be tested above the cap (lifetimes censored); what was tested is "no fall", and a fall in retained energy is not seen (frac at 3e5: 0.9946 at 4/3, then 0.9884, 0.9882, 0.9842, 0.9984).
- **A2-CD ("rises, then falls as the stable branch narrows"): not supported.** No fall to beta = 5 and none to T = 300,000.
- **A4 (beta of 1.6 or more: the best profile alive at 30,000 with frac above 0.90): hit** for the lowest-rate picks (frac at 30,000: 0.9945, 0.9902, 0.9969 and 0.9665, 0.9986 and 0.9687 at 1.6, 2.0, 3.0, 5.0); the path-integral "best" picks fell short at 1.6 and 2.0 (frac 0.7912 and 0.7953 at 30,000, T_90 3656.5 and 11,497.5), which is F-007.
- **A5 (no beta is permanent): untested.** Nothing above 4/3 died, and at beta = 5 the windowed rate is zero within error (1.3e-10 against a per-window 1.3e-10, 300,000 time units).
- **A7 (beta = 3 and 5 alive at 300,000 with frac above 0.95; 1.6 and 2.0 alive with frac above 0.90): hit.** 0.9884 and 0.9882 at 1.6 and 2.0; 0.9842 and 0.9694 at 3.0; 0.9984 and 0.9686 at 5.0. The rate at 1.6 rose 3.5 times over 300,000 (9.6e-9 to 3.4e-8); at 2.0 it is flat (4.2e-9 to 5.1e-9); at 3.0 it falls (5.4e-8 to 4.3e-8, the lump is moving toward a lower-rate state).
- **N17 (a longevity cliff near beta of 1.6 to 2.1, `PRIOR_ART.md` S3.4, low confidence): not seen to T = 300,000.** No start at 1.6 or 2.0 fell. Whether the cliff moved was not tested: there are several dips per beta from 1.6 up, so "the dip" of N17 is not uniquely defined by this table.

### F-013  WP-C pair law: C2 misses on the sliding-force measure (d16_0, d20_0, d16_45) and C4 misses at d = 16

- Date, session, work package, step: 2026-10-02, Session 2, WP-C pair batch (15 pairs, AWS; `outputs/session2/wpC/results/`, analysis `outputs/session2/wpC/analysis/criteria.json` and `summary.json`), Step 3.
- Prediction on record: `docs/SESSION_02_PREDICTIONS.md`, Block 2, rows C2 and C4, as amended in Block 2a. C2: measured force over predicted = 1, within 15% at dtheta = 0 and 180 and 25% at 45 and 135, on two measures, (i) the sliding half-space force on windows centred at t >= 100 with two distinct lumps, against F(d(t), dtheta(t)) at the measured omega, and (ii) the centroid separation d(t) against the point model; at d = 16 the early half-space value (within 20%) is a third item. C4: F/F0 against cos(measured dtheta) has slope 1 within 0.15 and intercept 0 within 0.1. Literature column at the time: the formula is published for the non-relativistic limit (Malomed 1998, READ), no measured value in any source; the cosine law holds only for equal frequencies (Bowcock 2008, READ). Block 2b flagged, before the batch finished, "C2 at d = 16 in phase" (the only windows at t >= 100 are near contact) and "C2 at 45 and 135 degrees (ratios amplified where the predicted force passes near zero)". It did not flag d20_0 or C4.
- Expected value or outcome, with its tolerance: ratio 1 within the tolerances above on every judged window and measure; C4 slope 1 and intercept 0 at each separation.
- Measured value or outcome, with parameters and result files: beta = 4/3, omega = 0.909149 profile, N = 160, L = 88, dx = 0.55, dt = 0.04, d = 16 (T = 300), 20 and 24 (T = 500). C2 measure (i), worst window / median over the valid windows: d16_0 0.148 / 0.241 (2 valid windows, d_min 7.2 and 8.4); d20_0 0.124 / 1.013 (66 valid windows; the worst has d_min 6.8, the next three 8.0, 9.1, 9.9 with ratios 0.27, 0.53, 0.71; worst over windows with d >= 12: 0.901); d16_45 3.71 / 1.38 (48 valid, 14 excluded for |F_pred| < 0.25 F0; the worst is at t_c 246, d_min 18.6, the first window after the law's second zero where |F_pred| passes 0.25 F0). The same three runs on measure (ii): 1.026, 1.059, 0.899; early half-space at d = 16: 0.9999 (d16_0) and 1.040 (d16_45). The other nine C2 items: worst window 0.93 to 1.13 (d16_180 0.931 and d20_45 1.130 are the extremes), medians 1.02 to 1.06, measure (ii) 0.93 to 1.06 (d20_45 0.932 the lowest), early half-space 1.036 (d16_180, d16_135). C4 (least-squares line of F/F0 against cos(dtheta) over all valid windows at one d, 5 runs): d = 16 slope 1.206, intercept 0.152 (196 windows); d = 20 1.003 / -0.022 (492); d = 24 1.035 / -0.004 (535). C3 (not a miss, for reference): kappa 0.4172 (measure i; reviewer 0.4163; on record 0.41647), amplitude 21,137 (reviewer 20,744) against 2 pi A^2 = 20,200.
- Size of the miss against the tolerance: C2 d16_0, deviation from 1 of 0.85 against 0.15, 5.7 times; d20_0, 0.88 against 0.15, 5.8 times; d16_45, 2.71 against 0.25, 10.8 times: all beyond about 3 times. C4 at d = 16: slope 0.206 off against 0.15 (1.4 times), intercept 0.152 against 0.1 (1.5 times), within about 3 times, which the general rule in `CLAUDE.md` scores as a pass with a note; it is recorded as a miss because Block 2a fixed the criterion.
- Ranked causes (orchestrator, with the reviewer's evidence): (1) Close range. The windows that decide d16_0 and d20_0 have d_min of 7.2, 8.4 and 6.8, where the tail law overpredicts (the derivation puts its 10% limit at d = 12: `WPC_DERIVATION.md` section 6 gives F_sup/F_asym = 0.346 at d = 8, 0.736 at 10, 0.907 at 12); against the full superposition force the measured force is 1.00 to 1.04 for d_min >= 10. Block 2b flagged d16_0; d20_0 and C4 were not flagged. (2) A real departure in the 45-degree pair at d = 16: for t = 135 to 195 the force is 1.3 to 1.5 times the law and changes sign at dtheta = 85 degrees, not 90, while one lump has taken 28% of the pair's N (the validity limit of the linear N-exchange model is 15%) and d_min = 11.7. Unequal tails explain 5 to 8%; the cause is unidentified; the leading guess is non-stationary lumps, untested. The orchestrator had filed this run under "ratios amplified near the zero crossing" in Block 2b, which under-flagged it. (3) C4's pooled fit at d = 16 is set by the two near-contact windows and by the d16_45 run; inside single runs the cosine law holds (the 90-degree run alone: slope 1.04, intercept -0.003, over cos from -0.39 to -1; with the centroid-based force the pooled fit gives 0.93 / -0.07). (4) A +2 to 6% systematic excess of force and N flow at d = 20 and 24 (fitted amplitude 20,744 to 21,137 against 20,200).
- Also recorded under this entry (reviewer, `WPC_BUILD_NOTES.md` section 9, point 2): the C6 outcome hit at d = 16 hides a 38% shortfall of the point model's shift for the 45-degree pair (-5.13 measured against -8.3; the criterion applies a shift tolerance only at d = 24).
- Diagnostics run now: the reviewer's independent recomputation from the 24 result JSONs (section 9): quadratic fit of d(t) for the early acceleration, against the derived force at the measured d, dtheta, omega, 0.972 to 1.069 over the 12 runs with a non-zero predicted force; kappa 0.4163 (sub-selections 0.4156 to 0.4199); window counts and d_min for the two close-range misses; the superposition comparison; the 90-degree-only cosine fit. Scholar's cheap checks from `summary.json` and the family file are in the interpretation notes (N29, N30).
- Diagnostics proposed (expensive ones), not run: none supplied. Scholar suggestions (cheap, from files on disk): (a) tabulate measured over law against the N asymmetry Delta_N = |N_0 - N_1|/(N_0 + N_1) and the lump frequency difference for every window of every pair, to see whether the d16_45 excess is a function of Delta_N alone (the d16_90 and d16_135 pairs stay at 0.082 and 0.037 but never come closer than 16, so this batch cannot separate asymmetry from distance except through the in-phase pair); (b) estimate the net radiation momentum through the mid-plane from the two lumps' own loss rates (Gamma(E) from the WP-A map at the lumps' measured omega 0.895 and 0.93) and compare it with the late residual of N30; (c) a pair at d = 16 with a smaller N exchange (for example dtheta = 20 degrees) would put the asymmetry below 15% at the same distance.
- What the miss rules in, and what it rules out: Rules in: the tail law in sign (C1, 12 of 12), decay constant (0.4163 to 0.4172 against 0.4165) and cosine dependence for d >= 12 and N asymmetry below about 15%. Rules out: nothing about the overlap region. Open: the d16_45 excess.
- Reviewer check: done. `WPC_BUILD_NOTES.md` section 9 agrees with every hit and miss of `criteria.json` as written and adds exceptions 1 to 3 and 5 above (close range, the d16_45 excess, the C4 pooled fit, the +2 to 6%).
- Literature check (scholar): Seen in kind, as stated limits of validity, and not as a measured excess. No source found that reports a force excess, or a shifted force zero, for soliton, Q-ball or oscillon pairs once the charge asymmetry is large (searches 102 to 104, Addendum E in `PRIOR_ART.md`). What is documented: (1) The momentum-flux (tail) force needs equal internal frequencies. [Bowcock, Foster, Sutcliffe 2008](https://arxiv.org/abs/0809.3895) (READ, `PRIOR_ART.md` section 4.1; 1+1 dimensions): valid only for equal frequencies, exactly in or out of phase. [Zhou 2024](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.16604) (READ, the review's section on Q-ball scattering): the method needs no net momentum flow out of the interval, "guaranteed" when the two Q-balls have the same internal frequency. In d16_45 the lump frequencies are 0.8985 and 0.9312 at t = 165 and the N asymmetry is 0.26 (N29), so the run leaves the stated domain of the cosine law at the time the excess appears. (2) [Malomed 1998](https://arxiv.org/abs/patt-sol/9809009) (READ): identical solitons, lowest order in the overlap, tails many decay lengths apart. (3) [Battye & Sutcliffe 2000](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-th/0003252) (READ, sec. 3): their mechanical model keeps the profile and positions fixed and, they say, breaks down as the frequencies approach the limits of the allowed band; drift of the positions cuts off the phase dynamics; no force is reported. (4) [Burlak & Malomed 2018](https://arxiv.org/abs/1805.09173) (READ, sec. B): in the 3D cubic-quintic NLS the lowest-order potential vanishes at pi/2 and the simulation shows repulsion with symmetry breaking (Delta = |E_1 - E_2|/(E_1 + E_2) up to 0.76 at kappa d = 2.5), explained by a mismatch between the "amplitude centre" and the "phase centre" when the solitons acquire opposite velocities; no force is compared with a tail law. (5) 1D: [Al Khawaja 2010](https://arxiv.org/abs/1004.3000) (READ, introduction and sec. II, partial) says Gordon's formula holds for small differences in speed and intensity and gives a generalisation with essentially no restriction on the amplitude difference (his Eq. 20); so an amplitude-dependent force correction is known for the 1D cubic NLS, and I found none for 3D or for a Klein-Gordon field. Not read: the full text of Bowcock et al. beyond the Session 1 record, Karpman & Solov'ev, and Zhou's review outside the Q-ball scattering section.
- Interpretation notes (scholar): (i) **Two different things sit under one row.** d16_0 and d20_0 are close-range window selection: the worst window is one or two windows 6.8 to 8.4 apart, where the derivation's own table (written before the batch) puts the tail law 2 to 3 times high; away from contact the same runs read 0.90 to 1.02 (d20_0, d_min >= 12: 59 windows) and measure (ii) 1.03 and 1.06. d16_45 is a force departure at d = 12 to 14 and a late excess (below). The criterion as written could not be hit at d16_0, because the only windows at t >= 100 are those two. (ii) **The d16_45 record, from the sliding windows in `summary.json`** (N30, `DERIVED`). The ratio of measured to law is 0.998 at t = 102.7 (dtheta 70 degrees, d 13.8) and 0.995 at 106; it passes through zero near t = 124 (the law's zero is about 1 time unit later); it is 1.34 at t = 137.5 (d 12.2), 1.42 at 151, 1.47 at 165 (the peak force is 5.43 against 3.70 predicted, d = 12.1, dtheta 145 degrees), 1.38 at 179, 1.18 at 193.5, and 0.68 at 211. The force zero is at dtheta = 86.4 degrees against 87.4 for the law evaluated on the same windows (the law's own zero is shifted from 90 by the 40-unit window average), so the "85 degrees, not 90" is a one-degree shift against the law, not five. The second zero is at dtheta = 254 degrees against 283.5 for the law: a shift of 29 degrees. At t = 232 to 264 (d 19 to 23, dtheta 275 to 335 degrees) the measured force is +0.127 at its maximum (t = 236) against a law of -0.004 and a full-strength F0(d) of 0.18, and +0.063 against 0.025 at t = 264: an excess over the law of +0.15 at t = 232, +0.13 at 236, +0.08 at 246 and +0.04 at 264, larger than the tail law at its maximum phase at the later of these (F0 = 0.096 at d = 20.8 against a measured +0.109 at t = 246); subtracting half the total momentum changes it by under 0.01. That is why the worst ratio, 3.71, sits at t = 246, just after the law's second zero, and why "amplified near the zero crossing" is only part of the story: the residual at t = 246 is 0.08, 0.83 of the full-cosine force F0. (iii) **The size of the unequal-tail effect** (N29, `DERIVED`). With A(omega) and kappa(omega) from the single-frequency family and the lumps' measured omega (0.9006 and 0.9200 at t = 134, 0.8985 and 0.9312 at 165, 0.8946 and 0.9301 at 193.5), the product A_0 A_1 e^(-(kappa_0 + kappa_1) d/2)(kappa/d + 1/d^2) exceeds the equal-lump value at the mean omega by 2.7%, 4.9%, 7.7%, 9.1%, 9.3% at t = 134, 151, 165, 179, 193.5 (t = 165, 7.7%, against the excess of 47%). This reproduces the reviewer's 5 to 8% and leaves about 35 to 40 points at the peak. (iv) **The excess starts at the model's stated limit.** Delta_N = |N_0 - N_1|/(N_0 + N_1) is 0.068 at t = 99 (ratio 1.00), 0.109 at 120, 0.150 at 134, 0.216 at 151, 0.277 at 179 (each lump's N has moved by 27.7% of its start value; the larger holds 64% of the pair's N); the derivation puts the validity of the linear N-exchange model at 10 to 15%. The 90-degree and 135-degree pairs at d = 16 stay at Delta_N 0.082 and 0.037 and the cosine law holds to 5.5% in the 90-degree pair (C5a), but they never come closer than 16, so this batch separates asymmetry from distance only through the in-phase pair (Delta_N = 0, agrees with the superposition force at d_min >= 10). The close-range correction goes the wrong way for the 45-degree excess: F_sup/F_asym is 0.907 at d = 12, so against the superposition force the peak excess is about 1.6 times, not 1.47. (v) **A candidate not on the list: radiation momentum through the plane.** The two lumps sit at different omega (0.895 and 0.928 at t = 240) and so at different points of Gamma(omega), which at beta = 4/3 runs from 3e-6 to 4e-4 (F-002); a loss rate of 1e-4 on E of about 2900 is a power of about 0.3, and a quarter of it crossing the plane is about 0.07, the size of the late residual. The estimate is rough and untested (suggestion (b)). Block 2a and the notes of `WPC_BUILD_NOTES.md` section 8 already record that the coherent-radiation momentum was about 10% of F at d = 16 early. (vi) **The +2 to 6% at d = 20 and 24 is unexplained.** The derivation estimates the coherent cross-radiation force at about 1e-4, 0.6% of F(24) = 0.0176; the superposition correction goes the other way (-0.14% at d = 20, -0.02% at 24).
- Cross-reference: `PRIOR_ART.md` Addendum E (E1 to E5, N29 and N30, searches 102 to 104), section 4.2 (the derivation), S6.1 and S6.2; F-009 (single-lump starts, N exchange and the pendulum); F-014; `docs/WPC_DERIVATION.md` sections 5 and 6; `WPC_BUILD_NOTES.md` sections 8 and 9; `docs/SESSION_02_PREDICTIONS.md` Block 2, 2a, 2b; `outputs/session2/wpC/analysis/criteria.json`; SESSION_02 report section 8 (WP-C); reviewer finding: section 9 (line above).
- Status: explained in part. The two close-range misses and the C4 pooled fit are explained (windows at d_min 6.8 to 8.4 where the law's own limit is on record; the cosine law holds inside runs). The d16_45 excess (1.3 to 1.5 times at d = 12, and a late attractive residual of +0.06 to +0.13) is open: unequal tails explain 5 to 8%, the asymmetry and frequency mismatch coincide with it, the cause is unidentified. The +2 to 6% at d = 20 and 24 is open.

### F-014  WP-C prediction C5b: at d = 24 the early N flow reads the wrong direction (and C5-CD with it)

- Date, session, work package, step: 2026-10-02, Session 2, WP-C pair batch, the dtheta = 90 pairs (`pair_d{16,20,24}_p090`) and the +-45 single-lump runs (`single_x12_t45.0`, `single_x12_tm45.0`; `outputs/session2/wpC/analysis/single_dNdt.json`), Step 3.
- Prediction on record: Block 2, row C5b: "dtheta = 90: N flows from the leading lump to the lagging one at 1.61, 0.244, 0.038 per unit time at d = 16, 20, 24", tolerance "direction; rate within 30% over the early window" (the antisymmetric rate (dN_0/dt - dN_1/dt)/2 over t_c in [15, 60], Block 2a); and C5-CD, the plan's statement "at 90 degrees the leading force vanishes and N flows from one lump to the other". Literature column at the time: direction and rate, no source (my N14 gives the same result); Q-ball charge transfer at intermediate phase, Battye & Sutcliffe 2000 (READ). Block 2b flagged this row before the batch finished: "C5b at d = 24 (the +theta and -theta lumps settle at different rates, larger than the interaction rate of 0.038; needs single-lump runs at -theta to subtract)".
- Expected value or outcome, with its tolerance: leader to lagger at 1.61, 0.244, 0.038 within 30%, direction included.
- Measured value or outcome: antisymmetric rate over [15, 60] (`criteria.json`, row C5b): d = 16, +1.571 (leader to lagger, 0.976 of the record; 0.978 of the 1.6057 predicted at the measured inputs); d = 20, +0.1765 (0.723 of 0.244, 27.7% short: a hit at the edge of the 30%); **d = 24, -0.0335, the wrong direction** (dN_0/dt = +0.0022, dN_1/dt = +0.0691; the on-record rate is 0.038). Plan-level C5-CD is scored a miss with it.
- Size of the miss against the tolerance: the sign is wrong, and the distance from the prediction is 0.0715 against a tolerance of 0.0114 (30% of 0.038), 6.3 times, beyond about 3 times.
- Cause, tested by a diagnostic run after the miss (orchestrator): the +45 and -45 single lumps drift in N in opposite directions while they settle, +0.0853 and -0.0362 per unit time on [15, 60] (`single_dNdt.json`, each on its own N-sample grid), comparable to the 0.038 signal. With both singles subtracted the d = 24 rate is +0.0412 (predicted 0.0382, 8% high) and the direction is as predicted; d = 16 and 20 become 1.645 (predicted 1.606) and 0.251 (predicted 0.243). On later windows (from t = 60) the reviewer finds 0.99 to 1.08 of the prediction at d = 24 without subtraction. The judged value stays a miss: the criterion is not changed. The subtracted value depends on the time grid of the N samples (13 samples in the window): the +45 single is +0.0853 on its own grid and +0.1207 interpolated onto the pair grid.
- Diagnostics run now: the singles subtraction above (reported, not judged, in `criteria.json`); the reviewer's later-window rates (0.99 to 1.08 at d = 24, 1.04 to 1.12 at 20, 1.02 to 1.09 at 16); the reviewer's check that with the +45 single subtracted lump 1 loses N at the predicted rate (-1.57 against -1.53 at t = 60; -17.1 against -16.0 at t = 450).
- Diagnostics proposed (expensive ones), not run: none supplied. Scholar suggestion (cheap): fix one N estimator for the early window (the slope of N on the sample times of one lump, with both lumps interpolated onto it, or the pair-specific least squares) and recompute the raw and subtracted rates with it; see the interpretation notes for the spread of the present ones.
- What the miss rules in, and what it rules out: Rules in: the derived N-flow law in direction and rate (within about 10%) at all three separations once the settling of the starts is removed; that the +theta and -theta single-frequency starts are not mirror images (the F-009 class). Rules out: a wrong sign in the derivation.
- Reviewer check: done. `WPC_BUILD_NOTES.md` section 9 agrees with the miss as written and calls it mechanical (point 4: the early window holds 1.7 units of N exchange against about 1.6 of settling difference between the +45 and -45 lumps).
- Literature check (scholar): Seen in kind; not for a real field. The direction rule (the lump that leads in phase loses N to the lagging one) and the sin(dtheta) dependence of the initial rate are those of [Battye & Sutcliffe 2000](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-th/0003252) (READ, sec. 3, eqs. 3.7 and 3.8; F-009 (b)), who find the initial transfer greatest at alpha = pi/2; the 1D and 3D rates they report are qualitative. No source found for a measured N-flow rate between real oscillons, and none for the start-up drift of single-frequency starts at +theta and -theta; the latter is the F-009 point (the slice energy depends on theta and the +-22.5 and +-45 starts settle 8e-5 to 1.2e-4 apart in omega). No new search.
- Interpretation notes (scholar): (i) **The raw bias is the same at all three separations, as a settling drift must be** (`DERIVED`, from `criteria.json`). Raw minus subtracted antisymmetric rate: 1.5708 - 1.6453 = -0.0745 (d = 16), 0.1765 - 0.2511 = -0.0746 (d = 20), -0.0335 - 0.0412 = -0.0747 (d = 24). A bias of -0.0746 independent of separation is the antisymmetric part of the singles' drift, (dN_0 - dN_1)/2 for the -45 and +45 starts, which is -0.061 on the singles' own grids and -0.082 on the grid of `pair_d24_p045` lump 0 (`single_dNdt.json`); the script subtracts on the pair's own lump-0 grid, and -0.0746 lies between the two. The signal at d = 24 is 0.038, half of the bias, and at d = 20 the 27.7% "hit" carries the same bias of -0.075 on 0.244: the raw d = 20 value is a marginal hit by luck of the bias, not a better measurement. (ii) **The early-window N rate is an estimator-dependent quantity at this size.** For the 90-degree d = 24 pair, lump 1 over [15, 60]: 0.0408 on its own N-sample grid, 0.0691 interpolated onto lump 0's grid (the judged value), 0.0767 on the grid of `pair_d24_p045` lump 0 (`single_dNdt.json`, key `pairs_d24_lumps`); lump 0 gives +0.0022 and -0.0055. The raw antisymmetric rate is then -0.0193, -0.0335 or -0.0411: the direction is wrong in all three and the size varies by a factor 2. The N estimator carries a ripple of the order of the signal (13 samples over 45 time units, period-averaged N from windows that are centred differently for the two lumps). The script's subtracted value is +0.0412, and +0.0415 follows from the own-grid values in `single_dNdt.json` (lump rates +0.0022 and +0.0408, singles -0.0362 and +0.0853); the raw pair rates differ between the two files, so they should be reconciled before either is quoted as the early rate. (iii) **Consistent with F-009 and Block 2a.** An N drift of 0.1215 per unit time between the +45 and -45 singles over 45 time units is 5.5 in N, which at dN/domega = -44,878 (the 4/3 family, F-002 and `PRIOR_ART.md` N23) is 1.2e-4 in omega: the 8e-5 to 1.2e-4 that Block 2a recorded for lumps started at +theta and -theta. (iv) **Was it anticipated?** Yes, in Block 2b, with the mechanism and the repair (the -theta singles). The miss is therefore not a surprise of the physics; the entry records that the repaired value agrees with the derivation to 8% at d = 24, 2.5% at 16 and 3.2% at 20. The criterion was not changed after the data, so the judged result stays a miss. (v) **What the plan's 90-degree statement did and did not get right.** The leading force vanishes (C5a: worst |F - F0 cos(dtheta)|/F0 0.055, 0.040, 0.028 at d = 16, 20, 24 against 0.15); N flows from the leader to the lagger (from t = 60 at 0.99 to 1.12 of the prediction at all three separations); the pair then repels as the phase runs toward 180 (C5c, C5d). The row C5-CD is scored a miss only because C5b is.
- Cross-reference: `PRIOR_ART.md` S6.2 (N14), Addendum C (N23, F-009), Addendum E (searches 102 to 104); F-009; F-013; `docs/WPC_BUILD_NOTES.md` section 8 and section 9 point 4; `outputs/session2/wpC/analysis/criteria.json`, `single_dNdt.json`; `docs/SESSION_02_PREDICTIONS.md` Block 2a and 2b; SESSION_02 report section 8; reviewer finding: section 9 (line above).
- Status: explained (the settling of the +-45 starts, flagged before the batch, tested with the singles; the derived law holds in direction and in rate to 8% once it is removed). Open: one N estimator for the early window; the reconciliation of the pair rates between `criteria.json` and `single_dNdt.json`.
- Update (scholar, 2026-10-02, after the WP-E review): the reviewer's last paragraph in `WPE_BUILD_NOTES.md` resolves the open reconciliation. The three early d = 24 rates on file (0.0691, 0.0408, 0.0767) are the same slope read on three time grids across a start-up transient, and `dNdt_on_pair_grid` in `single_dNdt.json` uses the grid of a different run than its label says; with both singles subtracted on their own grids the rate is 0.038 +- 0.007 against 0.038 predicted, leader to lagger. The miss as scored stands; the cause (settling of the +-45 starts) is confirmed, and the open item about one N estimator for the early window is reduced to using each single on its own grid.

### F-015  WP-C prediction C11: the linear twin's "no centroid attraction" cannot be measured by ball centroids

- Date, session, work package, step: 2026-10-02, Session 2, WP-C linear twins (`twin_d16_p000`, `twin_d20_p000`, `twin_d24_p000` on AWS; `twin_d16_p180` on the Mac, `outputs/session2/wpC/results_mac/`), Step 3, control (standard 5 of `CLAUDE.md`).
- Prediction on record: Block 2, row C11: "Linear twins: energy in each ball below 0.05 of its start by T = 100; no centroid attraction". Literature column at the time: free packets disperse (Copeland 1995); Session 1 twins 0.018 at T = 100. Block 2b flagged, before the batch finished, "C11 'no centroid attraction' (ball centroids of a dispersing linear twin are not lump positions)". The evaluator (Block 2a and `criteria.json`): ball energy at the raw sample nearest t = 100 over its t = 0 value below 0.05 for each lump, and attraction if the 3D centroid separation d(100) - d(0) < -0.05.
- Expected value or outcome, with its tolerance: ball energy ratio below 0.05 at T = 100 for every twin and no attraction of the centroids.
- Measured value or outcome, with parameters and result files: g4 = g6 = 0, same grid as the pairs, in-phase twins at d = 16, 20, 24 and one anti-phase twin at d = 16. Ball energy ratio at t = 100: 0.0452 (d = 16, in phase), 0.0220 (d = 20), 0.0246 (d = 24), 0.0367 (d = 16, anti-phase, Mac): this half hits, the first within 10% of the limit. Centroids: the 3D separation of the in-phase d = 16 twin goes 15.963 to 14.861 over 0 to 100, a change of -1.10: a miss as written (the criterion is -0.05). The d = 20 and 24 twins change by +37.2 and +25.1; the anti-phase d = 16 twin by +18.6.
- Size of the miss against the tolerance: -1.10 against -0.05, 22 times the threshold; the threshold was an arbitrary small number, and the quantity is not a measure of interaction (below).
- Ranked causes (orchestrator): the criterion cannot measure what it names: ball centroids of a dispersing linear field are not lump positions (at t = 100 both centroids sit at x = 0; the anti-phase twin closes by the same amount, -0.70 against -0.72 at t = 20).
- Diagnostics run now: the centroid tracks of the four twins (below); the reviewer's check (`WPC_BUILD_NOTES.md` section 9, "Other points").
- Diagnostics proposed (expensive ones), not run: a clean no-interaction test of the linear twin is still missing. Scholar suggestions (cheap): (a) the half-space momentum of the twin, P(x < 0), which for two linear packets must equal the superposition of the two single-lump twins' half-space momenta; (b) the exact superposition of two single-lump twins against the pair twin, field by field, at a few times (the equation is linear, so they must agree to rounding, and it tests the engine and the diagnostics rather than the physics); (c) a statement of what standard 5 is meant to establish for a pair run: that the nonlinear signal is absent in the linear twin, which the ball-energy ratio does test (dispersal), and nothing more.
- What the miss rules in, and what it rules out: Rules in: the twin disperses as a free field, to the extent the ball energy measures (0.022 to 0.045 at T = 100, in line with F-005's 0.0198 and the t^-3 tail law). Rules out: nothing about interaction; the criterion's second half has no content.
- Reviewer check: done. `WPC_BUILD_NOTES.md` section 9, "Other points": the ball-energy half hits (0.022 to 0.045 at T = 100 against 0.05), the "no centroid attraction" half cannot be measured by ball centroids of a dispersing field (the anti-phase twin closes by the same amount as the in-phase one), and a clean no-interaction test is still missing.
- Literature check (scholar): not needed beyond F-005: a free 3D Klein-Gordon field decays as t^-3/2 at fixed position ([Komech & Kopylova 2010](https://arxiv.org/abs/1003.3799), READ, introduction), so the ball energy falls as about t^-3, and the radial twin's 0.0198 at T = 100 (F-005) is within about 25% of the d = 20 and 24 twins' 0.022 and 0.025 (same time, different geometry). No source found for a no-interaction test of a linear twin; it is a design matter. No new search.
- Interpretation notes (scholar): (i) **What the centroid does** (`DERIVED`, from the raw series in the result files). For the in-phase d = 16 twin the x coordinates of the two ball centroids go -7.98, -7.62 (t = 20), -3.06 (40), -0.07 (60), and are 0 from t = 70 on: the two dispersing packets overlap on the mid-plane and each ball is centred on a field that is symmetric about it. The 3D separation then reads 12.75 (t = 60), 14.86 (100), 15.89 (120) because each centroid moves off the axis, to (y, z) = (-4.8, +5.7) and (+4.8, -5.7) at t = 100 (probably the cubic symmetry of the dispersing field; it is not a lump position).
