So the -1.10 is a difference between two geometric accidents (15.96 on the axis and 14.86 off it), and "closing by 1.10" is not attraction of anything. The anti-phase twin's centroids stay on the axis and separate, 16.04 to 34.68, as the antisymmetric overlap pushes the weight outward. At t = 20, before the packets overlap, the changes are -0.716 (in phase) and -0.700 (anti-phase), the same: the small early motion of the centroid is the dispersal of each packet, independent of the partner. (ii) **The energy ratio depends on the phase at d = 16** (0.0452 in phase, 0.0367 anti-phase) because the cross terms of the superposition differ between the in-phase and anti-phase twins; at d = 20 and 24 the ratio, 0.022 and 0.025, is near the single-lump twin value (0.0198 for the radial twin, F-005), within about 25%. (iii) **Standard 5 is met for dispersal only.** The twin shows that the nonlinear lumps' retention of their ball energy is not a free-field effect (the lumps keep 0.76 to 0.99 of it over the same times against 0.02 to 0.05 for the twin), and nothing about the pair force or N flow; the forces in F-013 are checked against the derived law and the singles, not against the twin.
- Cross-reference: F-005 (twin tails, t^-3); `docs/SESSION_02_PREDICTIONS.md` Block 2 row C11, Block 2b; `docs/WPC_BUILD_NOTES.md` section 8 ("Found while testing postprocess v2") and section 9; `outputs/session2/wpC/analysis/criteria.json`; `outputs/session2/wpC/results*/twin_*.json`; SESSION_02 report section 8; reviewer finding: section 9 (line above).
- Status: explained (the criterion measures geometry, and was flagged before the batch). Open: a clean no-interaction test of the linear twin; standard 5 is met only for dispersal.

(ii) **B2 hit, with a caveat that it could not have failed at this level.** The start is on the lattice with exact cubic symmetry, so a2 = 0 and a3 of the order of rounding (6e-14) are what any run from that start would give while the symmetry is not broken by noise; Session 1's run 5 (generic perturbations, no growth to T = 1000) remains the only test of non-spherical growth, and the checkpoint is at t = 4950, not 5000. ### F-017  WP-E prediction E11: the ring of six around an anti-phase centre does not merge by T = 700, and the criterion labels it "bonded"

- Date, session, work package, step: 2026-10-02, Session 2, WP-E arrangement batch, run `E11_ring6_c_R15.77` (AWS), Step 4 arrangement search. Result `outputs/session2/wpE/results/E11_ring6_c_R15.77.json`; analysis `outputs/session2/wpE/analysis/E11_ring6_c_R15.77.json` and `criteria_E.json`.
- Prediction on record: `docs/SESSION_02_PREDICTIONS.md`, Block 3, row E11: "6-ring around an anti-phase centre, R = 15.77 (4.71 hw; Model B balance resting on second-order terms; a probe, not a candidate)", T = 700; point model (B) "merger at 463"; prediction "merged; low confidence"; tolerance "contact 320 to 650". Block 3b (written after the batch, before the second run) adds E11-long (T = 1200, same grid: contact between 700 and 850) and E11-fine (dx = 0.393 to T = 1000: d(t) agrees with E11-long to 0.1 up to t = 600, contact within 10%) and says the point model gives 440 to 463 for this start. Literature column at the time: none for this row.
- Expected value or outcome, with its tolerance: merged (distinct-lump count below 7 or a pair within 2 hw = 6.69), first contact between t = 320 and 650.
- Measured value or outcome, with parameters: beta = 4/3, K = 7 (six lumps on a ring of radius 15.767 about a centre lump; ring lumps at phase 180 degrees, centre at 0), snapshot starts, dx = 0.55, dt = 0.04, T = 700, run completed; omega/m (median) 0.9096, nonlinear share at t = 0 0.350. The lump finder counts 7 distinct lumps in all 26 samples (t = 60.4 to 685.4); at the last sample the nearest-neighbour ratios (peaks) are 0.655 to 0.849 and the tracked minimum at the last valid window is 0.593. Ring radius about the centre lump (my recomputation from the stored positions, one-period averages): 15.770, 15.655, 15.361, 14.939, 14.477, 13.841, 13.128 at t = 10, 200, 300, 400, 500, 600, 690; inward speed averaged over 100 to 200, 300 to 400, 500 to 600 and 600 to 690: 0.0013, 0.0042, 0.0064, 0.0079 (the reviewer's instantaneous value at the end is 0.0092). The spread of the six ring radii (standard deviation) is 0 to t = 400, 0.0005 at 500, 0.008 at 600 and 0.206 at 690, an e-fold time of 27 to 36. Centre-to-ring phase and N: the reviewer finds the phase librating about 180 degrees by about +-17 degrees with a period of about 350 and N passing between centre and ring at about 9% with the same period (the stored maximum deviation of the phase steps from their set values is 17.5 degrees by t = 100). Contact: none in the record; the reviewer's extrapolation puts the first pair at 2 hw at 699 to 709 and the count drop at 720 to 770; the symmetric ring alone would reach contact at 1100 to 1270. By the Block 3 rule the outcome is "bonded" (held fraction 1.0, 26 of 26); `criteria_E.json` scores the outcome "merged" a miss and the contact time "not evaluable".
- Size of the miss against the tolerance: the outcome class is missed outright (no merger in the record). The contact time was not measured; the extrapolated 699 to 709 is 1.08 to 1.09 times the upper edge of 650, inside the 3-times rule, so the timing is a miss by the written band and a pass with a note by the general rule if the extrapolation holds. The point-model value 463 is a factor of about 1.5 below the extrapolated contact.
- Ranked causes (orchestrator): (1) the point model's merger time depends on its seed (displacements of 1e-3 there against about 1e-10 from round-off in 3D), so 463 was never a quantitative target; (2) the balance rests on second-order terms (the WP-D review), and the contraction is set by the libration of the centre-to-ring phase, which the model contains only in part. Cause of the label "bonded": my criterion (a band of 0.5 to 1.5 times the starting distance, no trend test, no link between the run length and the interaction time, no comparison with a far-apart control); the far-apart control E14 is labelled bonded for the same reason.
- Diagnostics run now: the reviewer's recomputation (all 18 runs; `docs/WPE_BUILD_NOTES.md`, last section, points 2 and 4); my own recomputation of the ring radius, the speed and the hexagon asymmetry from the stored positions (above), which agrees with the reviewer.
- Diagnostics proposed (expensive ones), not run at the time of writing: the second run (Block 3b; since done, see the "Second run" line below): E11-long to T = 1200 at dx = 0.55 and E11-fine at dx = 0.393 to T = 1000. Scholar suggestion (cheap, a point-model run of about 100 s, not a simulation of the PDE): rerun the E11 start in the point model with a seed of 1e-10 as well as 1e-3 (`WPD_NOTES.md` line 254 gives sigma = 1e-3 for the scan). If the model's merger time moves from 463 toward 700 or later, cause 1 is confirmed and the model's number was a seed artefact; if it stays near 463, the model's second-order terms are what differ from 3D.
- What the miss rules in, and what it rules out: Rules in: at R = 4.71 hw the ring of six around an anti-phase centre is not stationary: it contracts at a growing rate with no turning point, and the phase libration of about +-17 degrees leaves a net inward force of the measured size (reviewer: 1.7e-5 against 1.6e-5 in acceleration). Rules out: "bonded" as an outcome of this run (the label measures a run that stopped before contact) and the point-model 463 as a quantitative target. It does not rule out a merger later; none is observed in the record.
- Reviewer check: done (`WPE_BUILD_NOTES.md`, last section, "E11 is not bonded in any meaningful sense" and "The criterion").
- Literature check (scholar): Seen, as practice; the specific rule is not published. Published studies of rotating and static clusters do not use a band on the neighbour distance; each defines "robust" by a monitored time series, a pre-computed equilibrium and a perturbation test. [Desyatnikov & Kivshar 2002](https://arxiv.org/abs/nlin/0112039) (READ, PRL 88, 053901, p. 3): the equilibrium radius of a ring cluster is the minimum of an effective potential U(R), found for given step and number; the clusters are described as generally metastable and subject to a symmetry-breaking instability, yet able to propagate for many tenths of a diffraction length (their Eq. 4, the condition of zero energy exchange, is an existence condition and not a stability statement). [Li, Malomed, Mihalache 2020](https://arxiv.org/abs/2010.12748) (READ, sec. IV, Fig. 7): the cluster's mean radius, mean angular velocity and depth of azimuthal modulation are tracked, "quasi-periodically oscillate with a small amplitude", under a random perturbation factor 1 + 0.1(rho_1 + i rho_2), for clusters started at the radius R_min of the effective-potential minimum; propagation distances are those of the figures (I did not extract a number of revolutions). [Dong, Liu, Malomed 2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.22231) (READ, sec. 2, 3D quantum-droplet necklaces): the energy of the ansatz against R has a minimum R_min for step phases up to 90 degrees; clusters at R of about R_min "persist up to t > 1500", which they put at about 10 diffraction times (t_D of order R^2), under random perturbations of up to 5%; with R above R_min they "undergo gradual fusion or exhibit nearly periodic oscillations" (radial oscillation period about 196 in one case), and without angular momentum (in phase) they fuse. In these papers a cluster that contracts without a turning point is, by construction, not a bound cluster; E11's radius falls with accelerating speed and no turning point after t = 85 (reviewer), so it would not qualify under any of them. The duration used is a number of the cluster's own interaction (diffraction) times: for E11 the in-phase control's contact time is 150 to 160, and the reviewer's "T of at least five interaction times" is about 800, which E11-long (T = 1200) meets and E11 (T = 700) does not. No source found for a fixed distance band as a bound criterion, or for a far-apart-control comparison as part of a bond criterion; the control is ordinary practice and not a literature point. Not read: Crasovan et al. 2003 beyond the abstract-level record in `PRIOR_ART.md` S1; Mihalache et al. 2004 (abstract only).
- Interpretation notes (scholar): (i) **The two "bonded" runs are bonded for opposite reasons.** E14 (far apart) moves by 5.3e-4 in the ratio and 2.0e-4 in N spread over T = 500; E11 moves by 17% in the ratio and has an inward speed rising from 0.0004 to 0.0092. Any rule that required a response different from the control, or a last-third trend within a few times the control's, would have separated them (reviewer's proposed rule). (ii) **What "second run" must show.** The literature definition of a hold is a turning point or an oscillation about a radius; E11-long needs to be read for the sign of the radial acceleration after the hexagon breaks (t of about 500 on), not for the count alone. (iii) **E11 is the only arrangement where the first-order forces cancel by design** (centre anti-phase to a ring that is itself in phase), and its failure is a second-order effect (libration, seeded asymmetry): the same structure as the pair result that a stable phase repels (Battye & Sutcliffe 2000, F-013) and not a new kind of failure.
- Cross-reference: `PRIOR_ART.md` Addendum F (Session 2 novelty check, items 5 and 6), S1.1 and S1.6, Addendum C (C1); F-019; `docs/WPE_BUILD_NOTES.md` last section; `docs/WPD_NOTES.md` line 254; `outputs/session2/wpE/analysis/E11_ring6_c_R15.77.json`; `docs/SESSION_02_PREDICTIONS.md` Block 3 and 3b; SESSION_02 report section 7; reviewer finding: above.
- Second run (done 2026-10-02; `outputs/session2/wpE/results/E11L_ring6_c_R15.77_T1200.json`, `E11F_ring6_c_R15.77_dx0.393_T1000.json`; prediction Block 3b): merged on both grids. Same grid to T = 1200: reproduces the first run to round-off up to T = 700; first pair within 2 hw at t = 699.4; distinct-lump count 7 to 5 at the window centred 710; the five remaining lumps fly apart (at t = 1185 one is 8.6 from the centre, four are 34 to 37 out); 35% of the energy absorbed by T = 1200. Finer grid (dx 0.393, N = 256, L = 100.57) to T = 1000: contact at 720.1 (+3.0%), count 7 to 5 at 735; ring radius agrees with the base grid to 1.8e-4 up to t = 600. Against Block 3b: outcome hit on both; E11-long's contact window (700 to 850) missed by 0.6 as written (699.4), inside it by the count reading (710); E11-fine's three items hit. Diagnostic after the miss (point-model seed scan, `outputs/session2/wpD_measured/e11_seed_scan.json`): the model's contact time for this start is 436 / 454 (Model A / B) at seed 1e-3, 641 / 682 at 1e-5, 824 / 906 at 1e-7, 1053 / 1235 at 1e-10, and 1264 / 1630 with no seed; its symmetric ring is at radius 15.05 (A) or 15.75 (B) at t = 700 against 13.13 in 3D, and its centre-to-ring phase stays at 180 to a fraction of a degree against a 17 degree swing in 3D (the 13.13 is the 3D radius at t = 690). So cause (1) is confirmed (the 463 was set by the model's seed). A model without the phase swing does not contract, which is consistent with the swing driving the 3D contraction; that the swing starts from a frequency difference between centre and ring lumps (0.9155 against 0.9085 in the first window, 0.9132 against 0.9088 by t = 30) is a hypothesis, untested. E11 is not a bonded group.
- Status: explained in outline. First run: contact later than the band, label a criterion flaw. Second run and seed scan done (see the "Second run" line): merged on both grids; the point-model time is set by its seed. Open: what starts the centre-to-ring phase swing (the frequency-offset origin is a hypothesis, untested).

### F-018  WP-E scoring and reading errors in the arrangement table (E3, E7, E15; the twin; the retained-energy reading; the box and resolution checks)

- Date, session, work package, step: 2026-10-02, Session 2, WP-E, analysis (`outputs/session2/wpE/analysis/criteria_E.json`, `summary.json`) and the orchestrator's reading of the batch; reviewer recomputation in `docs/WPE_BUILD_NOTES.md`, last section.
- Prediction on record: Block 3 rows E3 ("merged by T = 300; not bonded"), E7 (same), E15 ("twin: no lumps left, group energy below 0.1 by T = 100. Checks: same outcome class as E3, contact time within 15%"); the Block 3 definition of "group energy" is not given there, and the orchestrator's later statement to Aaron that the merged five-rings "disperse".
- Expected value or outcome, with its tolerance: E3 and E7: count below K by T = 300; E15 twin: group energy below 0.1 at t = 100; E15 checks: same class as E3 and contact within 15%.
- Measured value or outcome (all from `criteria_E.json` and the `counts` in the analysis files; run lengths E3 500, E7 400, E15 checks 500, twin 300): (a) E3 and E7. The script scored "outcome merged" a miss for both: it read the end of the run (E3: "flew apart", with 5 distinct lumps at the last window, t = 485; E7: "not distinct at the start"). The item that was written, "count below K by T = 300", is a hit in both (first count below K at t_mid = 35.4 for E3 and 10.4 for E7). On the stored counts, E3 has 2 distinct lumps at t = 285 and 310 and E7 has 2 at t = 285 and 6 at t = 310 (debris: the count is of peaks above a threshold relative to the maximum, on a field that has emptied; the reviewer's figure for E3 is a peak energy density of 0.08 against 17 for the lumps). The reviewer's "hits at t = 285 and 310" therefore holds for E3 at both windows and for E7 at t = 285 only. The E15 class items (res and box checks "same class as E3") are misses as scored because the three runs are read at T = 500: E3 ends "flew apart" (counts 6, 6, 6, 5 from t = 410), the resolution check "merged" (2, 3, 6, 3) and the box check "merged" (2 in every window); all three have count 2 at t = 285 and 310. (b) Twin. Group energy 0.2363 at t = 100 against 0.1 (2.4 times, inside the 3-times rule; 0.098 at t = 150); by the ball measure 0.058. The group sphere has radius R_ext + 3 hw + 2 (reviewer's definition); taking R_ext as the ring radius, 13.61 for spacing 16, that is 13.61 + 10.03 + 2 = 25.6 (my arithmetic), so a ring dispersing freely from radius 13.6 is mostly still inside it at t = 100, which the limit of 0.1 ignored. "No lumps left" is not evaluable: the finder's threshold is relative to the maximum, so the count never reaches 0 (18 at the end; |phi|max at t = 100 is 0.063 of its t = 0 value). (c) The reading error. The orchestrator told Aaron that the merged five-rings "disperse" because `E_retained_end` was about 2%. That number is residence in the group sphere. In E1 two lumps of 0.36 E0 each (omega about 0.87) leave back to back and reach the sponge at about t = 500; in E2 two lumps of 0.38 E0 leave along the ring axis; they are alive until the sponge absorbs them (reviewer, table of section 1). (d) The checks. The resolution and box checks of E3 are identical to E3 up to contact (contact at 22.78 in all three by `criteria_E.json`, relative differences 2e-5 and 8e-9; the reviewer and the report quote 22.69 and 22.7 from the reviewer's own recomputation, a difference of 0.1 against a tracked resolution of half a period, about 3.5; counts identical to t = 385) and differ after about t = 235, when the recoiling lumps meet the sponge at different radii (27.6, 30.4, 47.4); ball energy at T = 500: 0.017 (E3), 0.031 (resolution), 0.159 (box). The resolution check also changed the box (84.86 against 79.2), so it is not a check at fixed box, and the box check is 1.5 times, not the 2 times of standard 4 (deviation recorded in the build notes). The two "contact within 15%" items cannot fail, because the runs are identical before contact.
- Size of the miss against the tolerance: (a) scoring: E3 and E7 are hits as written on the stored counts at t = 285; the three class items are misses only on the T = 500 reading, hits at T = 300. (b) 2.4 times, a pass with a note. (c) a reading error with no data consequence. (d) the standard met only up to contact for this family.
- Ranked causes (orchestrator): (a) the script evaluated the end of the run where Block 3 says "by T = 300", and the lump finder counts debris on an emptied field; (b) the 0.1 limit ignored the size of the group sphere; (c) `E_retained_end` is a residence measure, not a survival measure; (d) recoiling lumps reach the sponge at different radii, so late behaviour depends on the box, and the checks were not run at fixed box.
- Diagnostics run now: the reviewer's recomputation from the result files with its own code; my reading of the stored counts for E3, E7 and the two checks (above).
- Diagnostics proposed (expensive ones), not run: none. Scholar suggestion (cheap): quote group energy only with its sphere radius and, for the lump count, a threshold in absolute energy density as well as one relative to the maximum, so that an emptied field counts as zero.
- What the miss rules in, and what it rules out: Rules in: for the deep-overlap starts (E3, E7, E4) the process is fast (contact 22.7, about 10, about 10), the outcome is two recoiling lumps (E3: 0.41 E0 each; E7: 0.45 E0 each) and the late record depends on the box. Rules out: nothing about the physics; the "miss" lines in `criteria_E.json` for E3, E7 and the three E15 class items are scoring artefacts, not results; the twin is a pass with a note.
- Reviewer check: done (`WPE_BUILD_NOTES.md`, last section, points 1 and 4 and the verdicts paragraph).
- Literature check (scholar): not applicable to (a), (b) and (c) (scoring and reading). For (d): no source found on box dependence of late-time outcomes of lumps that recoil into an absorbing layer; the linear case (reflection and damping of slow massive waves in a sponge, imperfect near the mass gap) is in `PRIOR_ART.md` A1 (F-001). The standard of energy inside a radius over time is the project's own (CLAUDE.md standard 2); a radius that scales with the ring is needed once the group is larger than the lump.
- Interpretation notes (scholar): the "merged" entries of the table for E3 and E7 carry two different kinds of statement: the pair of numbers "contact at 22.7 and 10" says the structure collapsed immediately, and the later "flew apart" label (E3) belongs to the debris. Where the report says "hit on the counts at T = 300", the count it rests on is t = 285 (E7) or t = 285 and 310 (E3).
- Cross-reference: F-017 (the criterion), F-019; `docs/WPE_BUILD_NOTES.md` last section, sections 6 to 8; `outputs/session2/wpE/analysis/criteria_E.json`, `E03_ring5_m1_a8.11.json`, `E07_ring6_m1_a7.32.json`, `E15_*`; SESSION_02 report sections 7 and 8; reviewer finding: above.
- Status: explained (scoring and reading); the two box and resolution items stay as written (not met after contact).

### F-019  WP-E and WP-D: point-model statements contradicted in 3D (the stepped ring as a fixed point of the N flow; the wording on 90-degree rings; the timing)

- Date, session, work package, step: 2026-10-02, Session 2, WP-E against WP-D (`docs/WPD_NOTES.md` with its review corrections; `outputs/session2/wpD/predictions_point_model.json`), Step 4; runs `E06_ring4_m1_a12`, `E10_ring8_m2_a12`, with the controls `E14` and `E05`, `E13`.
- Prediction on record: (a) WPD notes (review corrections) and the report: a uniform stepped ring is a fixed point of the N flow, its phase sector neutral at steps of 90 degrees (unstable below, stable above); (b) Block 3 row E-all, as worded by the orchestrator: "rings with steps of 90 degrees or more and patterns with a phase-exchanging mirror plane expand"; (c) point-model times in Block 3 (not scored there): E1 276, E2 149, E3 135 (B), E5 230, E6 318 (B 292), E7 174 (B), E10 248, E11 463 (B), E12 117.
- Expected value or outcome, with its tolerance: (a) N equal across the lumps and phase steps at their set values while the ring holds its shape; (b) E10 expands (Block 3 row E10 itself predicts "merged, contact 170 to 370"); (c) no tolerance written; judged by the report as a guide for well-separated lumps.
- Measured value or outcome (analysis files; relative N spread is max over lumps of the spread over the mean as defined by the post-processing): (a) E06 (4-ring, 90-degree steps, a = 12): 0.0145 at t = 50, 0.027 at 98, 0.079 at 150, 0.342 at 199, 0.448 at 251, 0.499 at 300; phase steps off their set values by 5.2 degrees at t = 98, 2.7 at 150, 35.8 at 199, 117.6 at 251. E10 (8-ring, 90 degrees, two turns, a = 12): 0.026 at 50, 0.0016 at 98, 0.523 at 150, 0.834 at 189; phase steps off by 6.3 degrees at 98, 42.4 at 150, 140 at 197. The far-apart control E14: maximum 2.0e-4 over T = 500 (first window 3.5e-5), phase steps within 0.04 degrees. The 72-degree ring E01 (not a 90-degree case): 0.0025 at 98, 0.0063 at 150, 0.011 at 199, 0.081 at 251. E13 (144 degrees) and E05 (alternating): 2.1e-3 and 1.2e-4 maxima. (b) E10: contact at 193.0 (tracked pair within 2 hw; count 8 to 4 by t_mid = 210), as its own row predicted. (c) Contact or fly-apart times against the point model: E1 295.7 against 276 (+7%), E2 160.4 (count) or about 150 (tracked) against 149 (+1 to +8%), E5 229.5 against 230 (0%; peaks 235.4, +2%), E12 116.1 against 117 (-1%); E6 416.1 (peaks 435.4) against 318 (A) or 292 (B), +31 to +49%; E10 193.0 against 248, -22%; E11 about 700 (extrapolated) against 463, a factor 1.5; E3 22.7 against 135 (a factor 6.0); E7 within the first count window (t of about 10 to 20) against 174 (a factor of 8 or more).
- Size of the miss against the tolerance: (a) the N spread of E06 is about 1,700 times the control's maximum (0.342 against 2.0e-4) by t = 199 and the phase steps are 36 degrees off; this is a contradiction of the "fixed point" as a description of the 3D ring, not a tolerance miss; (b) a wording error, not a measurement miss; (c) none scored; E6 (+31 to +49%) and E10 (-22%) are outside the 7% of the well-separated rings, E11 a factor 1.5 and E3 and E7 beyond 3 times.
- Ranked causes (orchestrator): (a) at 90 degrees the first-order phase stiffness between neighbours is zero, so second-order terms and next-nearest neighbours decide; the fixed point is neutral, not stable, so any seed grows algebraically; the snapshot lumps differ by up to 6e-5 in N. (b) a wording error in the orchestrator's summary row. (c) the point model uses the tail law and period averaging, which fail inside about 3.6 hw (the WP-D scan found 27 of 28 balances there) and which treat the 90-degree ring at first order.
- Diagnostics run now: none beyond the N-spread and phase-step histories above (from the stored series); my arithmetic on them. Scholar observation (not a diagnostic): E06's spread has already risen from 2.1e-4 in the first window to 1.45% by t = 50, which is 70 times the first-window value and about 70 times the control's maximum, and E10's is 2.6% at t = 50 and 0.16% at 98 before the jump to 52% at 150. The early exchange is therefore not the slow growth of a seed at the round-off level (the snapshot differences of 6e-5 are far below 1.45%); it is driven by the pair interactions at the spacing of 3.6 hw, which supports the "second order decides" cause and the kinship with the pair result that N flows at every phase except 0 and 180.
- Diagnostics proposed (expensive ones), not run: none supplied. Scholar suggestion (cheap, point model, about 100 s each): rerun E06 and E10 in the point model with the second-order terms of the WP-D "full-superposition" variant (the variant that adds 23 balances) and compare the N spread at t = 50 and 150 with the numbers above; if neither variant gives a spread of order 1% by t = 50, the model lacks the exchange term that matters at 3.6 hw.
- What the miss rules in, and what it rules out: Rules in: the point model as a guide to merger and fly-apart times of well-separated lumps (4.8 hw), where it is within 7% in four of four cases. Rules out: its use inside about 2.5 hw (E3, E7: factors of about 6 and 8 or more) and for 90-degree rings (E6, E10: +31 to +49% and -22%, and the N exchange); and the reading of "fixed point" as "holds". The structural result (attraction between neighbours and stability of their relative phase exclude each other at first order) is not contradicted: no stepped ring held.
- Reviewer check: done (`WPE_BUILD_NOTES.md`, last section, points 3 and the timing paragraph).
- Literature check (scholar): Seen, and my brief is where the stability caveat was left out. The "fixed point" is the zero-energy-exchange condition of [Desyatnikov & Kivshar 2002](https://arxiv.org/abs/nlin/0112039) (READ, Eq. 4: theta = 2 pi m/N, "the condition of the vanishing energy flow"), which my N14 reproduced for the real-field pair law; it is an existence condition for a stationary state. The same paper says, in the sentences that follow the existence result, that such clusters are generally metastable and undergo a symmetry-breaking instability (p. 3). I wrote in S6 and N14 that the uniform stepped ring is a fixed point of the N flow, and the WP-D review carried that, without saying that a fixed point of this kind is not a stable one; the published treatments say so. For 90-degree steps the case is special in the published models too: DK 2002 find that for m = 2 bound states exist only for theta = 4 pi/N of at most pi/2 (N of at least 8), and show an exact rotating stationary cluster for N = 8, m = 2 (theta = pi/2), equilibrium radius R_0 = 5.5, angular velocity 0.092, in their 2D saturable model with a complex field (READ, Fig. 4c); [Dong, Liu, Malomed 2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.22231) (READ) state that steps up to 90 degrees attract and steps between 90 and 270 degrees repel, find a minimum R_min of the ansatz energy for steps up to 90 degrees, and find for steps between 90 and 270 degrees a global minimum only at infinite radius (expansion). So a 90-degree ring is the edge case in both the point model and the published models. What those clusters have, and the project's rings do not, is rotation at the equilibrium: the published balance is attraction against centrifugal motion (angular velocity J/I to 2% in DK), whereas the reviewer finds that none of the seven stepped rings rotates at J/I before contact (E1 turns by 0.7 degrees by t = 100, the other way). No source found for a real-field point-lump model of N flow and phase for rings, or for its test against 3D; no source found for the timing comparison in (c).
- Interpretation notes (scholar): (i) **Wording to repair.** The point-model summary should read "a uniform stepped ring is a fixed point of the N flow; at 90 degrees its phase sector is neutral at first order, below 90 unstable and above 90 stable", and nothing more; the 3D result (E06, E10) is that the neutral case does not hold. (ii) **Why the 72-degree ring holds longer.** E01's spread stays at 1.1% to t = 200 and its steps within 3 degrees, as the point model's unstable-but-slow growth would give: the first-order restoring or growth rate is nonzero at 72 degrees, so the ring's pattern is set by first-order terms until contact; the 90-degree rings have no first-order term to hold or to grow, so the exchange starts early. This is consistent with the data above and is not tested. (iii) **E-all clause (b).** In the row E-all, "rings with steps of 90 degrees or more ... expand" is wrong for E10 (merges at 193, as its own row said) and holds for E13 (144 degrees: five intact lumps, radius 13.6 to 26) and E06 (flew apart at ratio 1.67 at T).
- Cross-reference: `PRIOR_ART.md` S6 (N14, N16), S7, Addendum F (item 5), Addendum E; `docs/WPD_NOTES.md` (review corrections, section 11); `docs/WPE_BUILD_NOTES.md` last section; `outputs/session2/wpE/analysis/E06_*.json`, `E10_*.json`, `E14_*.json`; `docs/SESSION_02_PREDICTIONS.md` Block 3; SESSION_02 report sections 6 and 7; reviewer finding: above.
- Status: open (the N exchange at 90 degrees is observed and its cause is a candidate, not tested; the wording repair is to be made in the report and the notes).

**Outcomes of the other WP-E predictions in Block 3 (no miss; one line each; numbers from `outputs/session2/wpE/analysis/criteria_E.json` and the analysis files; reviewer recomputation in `WPE_BUILD_NOTES.md`, last section):**

- **E1 (5-ring, 72-degree steps, a = 16): hit, all five items.** Merged; contact 295.7 (window 190 to 420); delay against the in-phase control E2 1.84 (criteria: 295.7 over 160.4; the orchestrator's 1.96 is 294 over 150 from the tracked contact, which I could not find in the analysis file; window 1.3 to 2.5; the point model said 1.85); per-lump N spread 2.6e-3 until t = 100 (limit 0.02); phase steps within 0.42 degrees of 72 (limit 10).
- **E2 (in phase): hit.** Merged, contact 160.4 by the count (the reviewer's recomputation gives 150; the analysis file has no tracked-pair time for E2; window 105 to 200).
- **E4 (5-ring, CD's balance, a = 3.02): hit.** First-window count 2 of 5 (window t = 0 to 20.7; count drop at t_mid 10.4), then 2 to 3 distinct and recoiling fragments; not bonded; reviewer: 0.98 absorbed.
- **E5 (4-ring, alternating 0/180, a = 16, mirror control): hit.** Four intact lumps; nearest-neighbour distance passes 1.5 times its start at 229.5 (tracked; peaks 235.4; window 160 to 320); no merger; per-lump N spread 1.2e-4 (no tolerance written; control 2.0e-4).
- **E6 (4-ring, 90 degrees, a = 12): hit, at the top of the window.** Flew apart; passes 1.5 at 416.1 (peaks 435.4; window 200 to 450). N exchange and drifting steps are F-019.
- **E8 (octahedron around an anti-phase centre, CD's balance, 1.14 hw): hit.** First-window count 8 (more peaks than K = 7: the finder counts the collapse's debris), one lump from the window at t_mid 35.4; a pair within 2 hw at t = 5.0; one lump with 0.47 E0 at T = 300, omega 0.83, losing 0.23% of itself per unit time (reviewer).
- **E9 (cube around a centre, 2.54 hw): hit.** Nine distinct lumps to t_mid 85; one lump from 110.4; the ratio passes 1.5 at 50.9 (tracked); one lump of 0.43 to 0.46 E0 (reviewer).

- **E12 (5-ring 0,0,180,180,0): hit.** Merged; contact 116.1 (window 80 to 160); the in-phase pair closes first (the analysis file marks this "not evaluable" because pair identity is not stored; the reviewer evaluated it from the result files and finds a hit).

- Date, session, work package, step: 2026-10-02, Session 3, WP-P2 point-model scan (1,822 runs, 0 errors, 1,059 s on 2 workers; `swirl/arrange/p2scan.py`; results in `outputs/session3/wpP/p2/` of the integration worktree `.claude/worktrees/integration`: `summary.json`, `p2_0230.json`, `p2_0234.json`, `p2_0242.json`), Step 4 (arrangements). - Size of the miss against the tolerance: categorical, 3 where 0 was predicted: 3 of the 134 static-pair runs that stayed outside 3.6 hw, 3 of the 45 unequal pairs at 7.2 hw, 3 of the 20 at ratio 1.75 and above. Each passes its clauses narrowly (trend at 0.67 to 0.79 of the limit, N exchange up to 0.83, the response clause by 1.08 to 1.63 times its floor).
- Ranked causes (the orchestrator's record in `SESSION_03_STATUS.md` and `WPP_BUILD_NOTES.md` section 11 does not number them; the order is my reading, my additions are marked [scholar]): (1) A threshold on a continuum, not a distinct state: the label marks pairs whose slow drift falls under a limit that is fixed by the run length (3 times 5e-4 over the run, so the limit scales as 1/T) after a horizon of exactly 5 contact units; the status calls them "marginal on trend and N clauses". [scholar] The control's own trend in the point model is 6.7e-14, so the limit is set by the 3D noise floor of Session 2 (E14), which the point model does not have. (2) The point model omits the phase-independent mean force of unequal lumps (`PRIOR_ART.md` N34: the quartic term, less the sextic term that cancels 13 to 19% of it at 7.2 hw), which after the review fix is 8.6e-4, 8.1e-4 and 8.4e-4 of the in-phase first-order force F0 at 7.2 hw for N ratios 1.1, 1.5 and 2.0 (`n34_check.json`, `Fnet_mean_over_F0`; 0.005 at 6.0 hw), as large as or larger than the model's own beat-averaged force at ratio 1.5 and above (net over the model's beat-averaged force: 0.045, 1.39, -7.7 at ratios 1.1, 1.5, 2.0; the model's beat average is +5.8e-4 F0 at 1.5 and -1.1e-4 F0 at 2.0), so the model's sign and size of the drift at these ratios is not meaningful beyond first order. (3) The basis of P2 was unsound (WP-P1 reviewer): the held-position beat-averaged force is not a sound basis for "drift together", because the free dynamics of a pair released at rest can push it apart; `WPP_BUILD_NOTES.md` section 5.3 now records the reviewer's counterexample (ratio 1.5, 6.0 hw: held-position average attractive at 0.0056 F0, yet p2_0175 to p2_0179 end with the mean distance up 1.3 to 7.5%). [scholar] Interpretation note (ii) below gives a measured pattern at 7.2 hw that fits this. (4) A design choice, not an error: counting "beating" as a candidate label (Block P) makes the label reachable by any pair that stays under the trend limit for five units.
- Diagnostics run now: D1, D2, D3 and the reviewer's long runs (all point model, orchestrator and reviewer; numbers read from `p2_diagnostics.json`, `WPP_BUILD_NOTES.md` section 11.1 and Block P3).
  - D1 (the quartic N34 force added as a diagnostic variant, not the model; same T = 4603.3): the pairs close by 0.679, 0.652, 0.868% (0.128, 0.105, 0.093% in the scan); trend 1.47e-6, 1.43e-6, 1.90e-6 against 3.29e-7 (4.5, 4.3, 5.8 times); all three read "not bonded" on the trend clause; N exchange (8.6e-4, 8.6e-4, 3.7e-4) and the distance clause still pass. The review fix notes that the sextic term cancels 13 to 19% of the added force at 7.2 hw, so the closing would be about 0.55 to 0.75% and the trend about 1.2 to 1.6e-6 (3.6 to 4.9 times the limit): the conclusion is unchanged. Rules in: the omitted mean force, at the size `n34_check.json` gives, removes the label. Rules out: the label as a property that survives the correction the model is known to lack. [scholar] A free-fall estimate with the net force 8.3e-4 F0 (F0 = 0.0193 at ratio 1.5 and 0.0241 at 2.0, about 0.0217 at 1.75) and a reduced mass of about 1,760 gives a closing of about 0.45% over 4603 units, within 1.7 times of the 0.55 to 0.75% above (DERIVED, rough). Not shown: that the quartic and sextic terms are the whole of the omitted second-order force (the reviewer sizes the net omitted terms at about 0.005 of F0 at 6 hw, Block P3).
  - D2 (three times longer, T = 13,809.9, model without N34): p2_0230 and p2_0234 close by 1.12% and 0.96% and fail trend (7.8e-7 and 7.1e-7 against 1.09e-7: 7.1 and 6.5 times); p2_0242 closes by 10.01% to d = 21.66 (closest 5.43 hw) and fails trend (6.5e-6, 60 times), N exchange (1.0086e-3 against 1e-3) and distance (0.10012 against 0.1). Rules in: a slow closing that continues and, for p2_0242, speeds up; the label does not survive a longer window even in the model without N34. Rules out: a constant separation.
  - D3 (contact time against beat period): see F-021; for the candidates the ratio goes the other way: the equal in-phase contact time at 7.2 hw (920.7) is 4.4 beats at ratio 1.75 and 6.6 beats at 2.5 (my arithmetic from the omega values), a regime where first-order averaging is well justified and the force that remains is small.
  - Reviewer's runs to T = 60,000 (known only from Block P3; the scripts `rv/longrun.py` and `rv/long242.py` in the session scratchpad print and do not store their output, so I could not check the figures): p2_0230 and p2_0234 stay between d = 22.2 and 24.1 (the start is 24.08, so at most 7.8% closing), p2_0242 closes to 19.4 and leaves the family's range. Rules in: no merger within 65 contact units for the first two in the model without N34, and an exit from the family's range for the third. Rules out: nothing about 3D.
- Diagnostics proposed (expensive ones), not run: P3 (3D, running; Block P3 has the in-phase control, p2_0230, p2_0242 at 7.2 hw, isolated single-lump controls and a linear twin). Scholar suggestions (point model, about 1.4 s a run): (a) scan the start phase finely at 7.2 hw for ratios 1.75 and 2.5 and plot the trend against sin of the start phase, to test the pattern in note (ii); (b) release the pair with the mean drift velocity of note (ii) removed and see whether the neighbours of the candidates then also pass the trend clause. Both separate "the candidates are special" from "the clause is crossed at a zero of a start-phase term"; neither rescues the label.
- What the miss rules in, and what it rules out: Rules in: in the point model at 7.2 hw there are unequal static pairs whose closing in five contact units (0.09 to 0.13%) is below the clause limit, while their later behaviour without the omitted mean force is a slow continued closing and with it a closing 5 to 9 times faster; the label is a statement about a five-unit window. Rules out: the prediction "none bounded, beating"; the held-position beat-averaged force as a predictor of drift or merger; a reading of the label as evidence of binding. It does not rule in a bonded state (none occurred) and does not rule out that the real 3D pair is held by a second-order force; P3 decides that.
- Literature check (scholar), question: has published work seen weakly interacting unequal solitons or oscillons stay near each other for long times without binding? Seen in neighbouring systems in forms that differ from this outcome; no source found for real 3D lumps. (1) Breathing at the difference frequency with a slow net drift: [Axenides et al. 2000](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-ph/9910388) (READ, secs. III and V; complex-field Q-balls, 1D, ansatz with internal frequencies +omega_int and -omega_int, omega_int^2 = 0.9, initial separation 10, box 30, their units): the pair oscillates in position with period about 3.5 against 3.2 from the interaction energy (a term at the frequency difference plus a much smaller constant term); the amplitude is not constant and the pair drifted slowly to larger separations, which they tie to the constant term; their conclusion says pairs tend to breathe for times long compared with the internal period (2D slow collisions of equal charge end bounded). Context: complex field, opposite-sign internal frequencies so the difference is 2 omega_int and not a small detuning, a net drift outward where the model's mean force is attractive, no run length given in contact times. (2) No bond at separation: [Battye & Sutcliffe 2000](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-th/0003252) (READ, sec. 3.4; 1D Q-balls, omega_1 = 1.8 and omega_2 = 1.5, a = 3 in their parameter, no boost, t up to 450): they repel with virtually no charge transfer because they never get close enough, and a non-zero start phase changes the rate of separation only slightly. Opposite sign to the model, complex 1D. (3) Bound by a second-order attractive part: [Al Khawaja 2010](https://arxiv.org/abs/1004.3000) (READ, secs. II and III; exact 1D NLS two-soliton solution, arbitrary amplitude difference): the force has a part that oscillates between attractive and repulsive with the relative phase and an attractive nonlinear part, and at short separation the pair forms a molecule oscillating about an equilibrium bond length; the paper also says that the acceleration of solitons that are moving apart decays with time (its Fig. 4) and that any non-trivial effect of the oscillating force is expected only at short separation, which I read as no long-lived near-stationary pair at large separation in that model (my reading). [Buryak et al. 1998](https://arxiv.org/abs/patt-sol/9812005) (READ, `PRIOR_ART.md` T4): an incoherent attraction wins in 3D saturable NLS once phase exchange suppresses the coherent term (stable spiraling, not a static pair). (4) Real fields: [Campos, Manton, Mohammadi 2026](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12633) (READ, sec. IV, now read; earlier recorded as ABSTRACT): for two unequal small-amplitude 1+1D oscillons the force at large separation is a term at the sum frequency, which averages away, plus a slowly varying term proportional to cos(delta - eps_bar Delta-eps t) exp(-2 a eps_bar) (eqs. IV.11 to IV.14): the first-order beat law, with no static unequal-pair run in the sections read. [Xue et al. 2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.01597) (READ, secs. V and VI): mismatched real 3D oscillons were only collided. [Murai, Ogawa, Takahashi 2026](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.04494) (READ earlier) and [Blaschke et al. 2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.09136) (ABSTRACT): long-lived bound states of two co-located oscillons, not separated lumps. (5) The "beating" reading: for weakly coupled oscillators of different frequency the relative phase locks only if the coupling exceeds the detuning and otherwise drifts ([Arenas et al. 2008](https://arxiv.org/abs/0805.2976), READ, sec. 3.1.1 on the Kuramoto model: oscillators with |omega_i| at most Kr lock, the rest drift around the circle), so "beating" is the generic unlocked state and not a form of binding, which is also how Block P defines the label (DERIVED reading). (6) Driven-dissipative analogues, context only: [Turaev, Vladimirov, Zelik 2012](https://cris.bgu.ac.il/en/publications/long-range-interaction-and-synchronization-of-oscillating-dissipa-2/) (ABSTRACT; PRL; preprint [WIAS 1659](https://www.wias-berlin.de/preprint/1659/wias_preprints_1659.pdf), ABSTRACT): oscillating localized structures bind through weakly decaying waves by synchronization. No source found for an unequal pair of real-field lumps in 3D held near constant separation over several interaction times, or for a measured mean (second-order) force between unequal real lumps. Searches 156 to 165 (`PRIOR_ART.md` Addendum G).
- Interpretation notes (scholar): (i) **The label is where first-order effects cancel, which the model cannot resolve.** At 7.2 hw the model's own beat-averaged force changes sign between ratio 1.5 (+5.8e-4 F0) and ratio 2.0 (-1.1e-4 F0) (`n34_check.json`, three ratios sampled, so the zero is interpolated), and the ratio-1.75 candidates sit between; the omitted mean force (8e-4 F0) is larger than both. (ii) **The trend clause at 7.2 hw follows the start phase, which fits the reviewer's caveat.** Trend over limit by start phase 0, 45, 90, 135, 180 degrees (my tabulation of the `p2_*.json` files): ratio 1.75: 0.79, 16.5, 23.1, 16.6, 0.72; ratio 2.0: 3.73, 10.99, 14.01, 11.03, 3.68; ratio 2.5: 8.10, 2.85, 0.67, 2.77, 8.06; ratio 2.95: 12.03, 2.90, 9.10, 3.08, 12.07. The values are nearly symmetric about 90 degrees (0 against 180 within 1.4% for ratios 2.0 to 2.95 and 9% for ratio 1.75, where both are below 0.8; 45 against 135 within 6%), as a term proportional to sin of the start phase would be. A two-term form |A + B sin(start phase)| with A from the 0-degree run and B from the 45-degree run predicts the 90-degree value to 2% or better for ratios 1.75 (A = 0.75, B = 22.2: 23.0 predicted, 23.05 measured), 2.0 (A = 3.7, B = 10.3: 14.0 and 14.0) and 2.5 (A = 8.1, B = -7.4: 0.68 and 0.67), and fails for 2.95 (predicted 0.9, measured 9.1). With two parameters and three distinct phases this is one test point per ratio. The pattern is specific to 7.2 hw: at 6.0 hw and ratio 1.5 the same five values are 54, 19, 21, 10 and 47, where the pair moves enough for the force to change during the run. The mechanism that gives such a term (DERIVED, untested): a pair released at rest into a force F0 cos(D0 + Delta-omega t) acquires, by integrating the force from zero momentum, a mean velocity -F0 sin(D0)/(mu Delta-omega) that the beat does not average away; with F0 = 0.0217, mu about 1,760 and Delta-omega = 0.0302 this is 4e-4 per unit time against the 1.8e-4 implied by the ratio-1.75, 90-degree run (a factor 2.2), and it exceeds the beat-averaged term by up to 30 times at ratio 1.75. On this reading the candidates are the runs where that term vanishes (ratio 1.75, start phase 0 and 180, where sin is zero) or nearly cancels the mean term (ratio 2.5, 90 degrees), which is why they sit at the weak end of a continuum and not at a bound state. This is the sense in which free dynamics can push a pair apart and the held-position average is no basis for "drift together". (iii) **Where P3 bears on this.** P3 runs p2_0230 and p2_0242 at 7.2 hw in 3D and so tests the first-order beat picture with all second-order terms present; it does not run p2_0234, and a 3D start at rest has the same start-phase term, so the 3D candidates, if any, will sit near the same zero. (iv) **Bookkeeping.** F-022 reads the same three runs against P4. Count F-020 and F-022 as one set of runs, two failed statements.
- Cross-reference: `PRIOR_ART.md` T4, T5, T7, N34 (Session 3 brief) and Addendum G; F-021 (the other P2 clause), F-022 (P4 on the same runs); `docs/WPP_BUILD_NOTES.md` sections 2.2, 5.3, 11 and 11.1; `docs/SESSION_03_PREDICTIONS.md` Blocks P and P3; `docs/SESSION_03_STATUS.md` (WP-P row; reviewer's corrections); reviewer finding: the held-position beat average is not a sound basis for "drift together".
- Status: open. Explained in the point model as a threshold crossing near a zero of the first-order terms that the model cannot resolve (D1, D2, reviewer's runs), not as evidence of binding. P3 result (filled in 2026-10-02): **not run for the candidates; the 3D premise fails.** The 3D sources for the two candidate partners (the 1.75 N0 lump of p2_0230 and the 2.5 N0 lump of p2_0242; N/N_min about 3.1 and 4.4 at beta = 4/3; N = 160, L = 88, dt = 0.04) failed the engine's settling guard (omega drift at most 1e-4 per 100 time units, crop loss at most 1e-4) twice. Settled to t_s = 300: drift 1.42e-3 and 8.35e-4, crop loss 7.5e-3 and 4.8e-3. Settled to t_s = 1,500 on a dedicated instance (fix attempt 1): src_p3_N175 omega 0.8955 against the profile's 0.8790, drift 4.70e-4, crop loss 2.1e-3; src_p3_N250 omega 0.8855 against 0.8639, drift 1.49e-3, crop loss 7.4e-3. The guard refused both sources, as designed, so no candidate run (P3_c0230, P3_c0242), isolated-lump run (N175, N250) or candidate linear twin (P3_c0230_lin) was made. The second fix attempt allowed by CLAUDE.md (relaxing the guard) was not taken: it would have cost about 11 USD for a test whose premise does not hold (orchestrator's decision, 2026-10-02 16:30Z; `SESSION_03_STATUS.md`). This is evidence about the candidates' physical meaning, not a test of them: static unequal lumps of these sizes were not obtained in 3D, and with the drift still running at t_s = 1,500 (the omega of the 1.75 N0 lump had risen 0.0165 over the profile's, that of the 2.5 N0 lump 0.0216) they would not stay the fixed-N unequal pair the point model assumes over T = 7,500. So the 3D question of this entry (does a second-order force hold a real unequal pair?) is unanswered, and "bounded, beating" stays a point-model label (D1, D2, the reviewer's runs). Scholar's checks: (i) the guard's omega_drift is the absolute difference between the whole-period frequency at t_s and the same estimator 100 time units earlier (`swirl/engine/snapshot.py`, `make_source`, `drift_lag` = 100), so the figures are per 100 time units; (ii) between the two attempts N175's drift fell by a factor 3 and N250's rose by a factor 1.8, so N250 was not relaxing; (iii) Session 2's radial runs at beta = 4/3 (`outputs/session2/wpA/long_beta1.3333_w*.json`) show a drifting lump at N/N_min 2.50 (omega 0.8950, 0.8985, 0.9004, 0.9015 over its first four 1,000-unit windows; T_90 = 861) and a steady one at 5.21 (omega changing by about 2.4e-7 per 100 time units; frac 0.996 at 30,000), but none at 3.1 or 4.4, so the 3D drift is not cross-checked in the radial solver. Cheap diagnostic proposed, not run: radial runs at omega 0.8790 and 0.8639 to T = 1,500 (minutes each) would say whether the drift belongs to the family at those sizes or to the 3D source preparation. **Update 2026-10-05: run.** Radial runs at omega 0.878988 and 0.863859 to T = 2,000 (integration worktree `outputs/session3/wpP/radial_p3_check/`) reproduce the 3D drift (5.1e-4 and 1.5e-3 at t = 1,500 against 4.70e-4 and 1.49e-3) and the settled omega (0.8954 against 0.8955); frac 0.72 and 0.58 at 2,000, T_90 330 and 558. The drift belongs to the lumps, not to the 3D preparation. The "P3 (3D, running)" in the proposed-diagnostics line above is superseded. See `FINDINGS.md` FND-23 and `PRIOR_ART.md` Addendum I (I1 row (h), I3 item 8).

### F-021  WP-P2: at 3.6 hw unequal pairs with N ratio 1.5 and above make contact no later than equal in-phase pairs (Block P, row P2, second clause)

- Date, session, work package, step: 2026-10-02, Session 3, WP-P2 point-model scan, Step 4 (arrangements); same files as F-020 (`summary.json`, key `unequal_static_pairs_contact_over_equal_inphase`, and the `p2_*.json` runs; `p2_diagnostics.json` D3).
- Prediction on record: `docs/SESSION_03_PREDICTIONS.md`, Block P, row P2, second part: "At N ratio 1.5 and above, the pair drifts together on the beat-averaged attraction, and contact comes at least 3 times later than for an equal in-phase pair at the same spacing, or not within the cap." Basis: phase runs at omega_1 - omega_2 and the beat-averaged force is attractive and small; Block P's own note that the model's second-order terms are missing.
- Expected value or outcome, with its tolerance: for N ratio 1.5, 1.75, 2.0, 2.5, 2.95, contact time over the equal in-phase contact time at the same spacing of at least 3, or no merger within the cap (five equal-pair contact units, at most T = 10,000).
- Measured value or outcome: at 3.6 hw, start phase 0 (the starts the orchestrator scored), the merger times are 39.8, 39.7, 39.5, 35.9 and 31.2 against 41.03 for the equal pair: ratios 0.970, 0.968, 0.962, 0.875, 0.759 for N ratio 1.5, 1.75, 2.0, 2.5, 2.95. All five merge, all five are flagged as having come inside 3.6 hw (closest approach 1.96 to 2.02 hw, which is the merger). Beat periods 275.9, 208.3, 173.9, 138.7, 121.9; D3 (equal in-phase contact over beat period): 0.071 (ratio 1.2), 0.149 (1.5), 0.236 (2.0) at 3.6 hw, and 0.129, 0.268, 0.425 at 4.2 hw, 0.222, 0.462, 0.732 at 4.8 hw for the same ratios. Scholar's tabulation of the other start phases at 3.6 hw, N ratio 1.5 and above (25 runs, from the `p2_*.json` files): start phases 0, 45 and 90 degrees merge in 15 of 15 at 0.70 to 1.40 times the equal in-phase time; 135 degrees merges in 4 of 5 (1.28 to 2.95 times; ratio 1.5 stays bounded); 180 degrees flies apart in 5 of 5 (event at 1.76 to 4.55 times the equal contact time). So at 3.6 hw the outcome follows the start phase for every ratio up to 2.95, which is what Block P predicts only for ratio 1.1.
- Size of the miss against the tolerance: the target is at least 3; the measured values are 0.76 to 0.97, a shortfall of 3.1 to 4.0 times (beyond about 3 times). Scholar's extension to the other spacings, start phase 0, from the same files: at 4.2 hw ratios 2.5 and 2.95 merge at 2.04 and 1.66 times the equal time (below 3); at 4.8 hw ratios 2.5 and 2.95 merge at 4.20 (hit) and 2.84 (below 3, within about 3 times). Ratios 1.5 to 2.0 at 4.2 to 7.2 hw and ratios 2.5 and 2.95 at 6.0 and 7.2 hw do not merge within the cap (the second alternative of the prediction), but three of those are not "drift together": at 4.2 hw (ratios 1.5 and 1.75) and 4.8 hw (ratio 1.5) the pair flew apart, at 220, 289 and 465.
- Ranked causes (as for F-020 the ranking is my reading of the orchestrator's D3 and the status; additions marked [scholar]): (1) The phase has barely turned at contact (D3): at 3.6 hw the contact time is 0.07 to 0.24 of a beat, so the pair meets at a relative phase close to its start phase and acts as an equal pair of that phase; [scholar] with phase at contact about 2 pi times contact over beat, the start-phase-0 runs reach contact at about 52, 69, 82, 93 and 92 degrees for ratios 1.5 to 2.95 (DERIVED from the merger times and beat periods). The P2 argument (average over the beat) needs contact times of several beats. [scholar] The scan agrees: where merger takes at least one beat the delay is 1.66 times (ratio 2.95, 4.2 hw, 1.0 beats), 2.04 (ratio 2.5, 4.2 hw, 1.1 beats), 2.84 (ratio 2.95, 4.8 hw, 3.0 beats) and 4.20 (ratio 2.5, 4.8 hw, 3.9 beats), and where it would take more the pairs do not merge in the cap. (2) The edge of the model's validity: first order in the overlap is good to about 10% at 3.6 hw (`WPP_BUILD_NOTES.md` section 2.2), the Session 2 tail law and period averaging failed inside about 3.6 hw (F-019 (c)), and a contact at t = 41 is about six oscillation periods (2 pi/0.909 = 6.9; DERIVED), so the "contact time" itself is a model extrapolation; the omitted mean force is also largest here (net 0.13 to 0.16 of F0 at 3.6 hw, `WPP_BUILD_NOTES.md` section 2.2). (3) [scholar] The reference is not matched in N: the contact-time ratio falls monotonically with the N ratio at 3.6 hw (0.970, 0.968, 0.962, 0.875, 0.759), so the larger lump's stronger coupling outweighs any beat effect; the cause of that trend is untested.
- Diagnostics run now: D3 (above). It separates cause (1) from "the beat-averaged force is weaker than expected": the pairs merge at 0.14 to 0.26 of a beat, before any averaging can act. It does not separate (1) from (2).
- Diagnostics proposed (expensive ones), not run: a 3D pair at 3.6 hw, N ratio 1.5 to 2, start phase 0, against the equal in-phase pair: P3 does not include it (Block P3 runs 7.2 hw only), so P3 will not decide this item. Scholar suggestion (cheap, point model): the contact time against start phase at 3.6 hw and 4.2 hw for ratio 2.0 on a 15-degree grid, and against the beat period at fixed spacing by varying the N ratio from 1.2 to 2.95, to see whether the delay is a function of contact over beat alone.
- What the miss rules in, and what it rules out: Rules in: at 3.6 hw (contact within 0.15 to 0.26 of a beat) unequal pairs up to N ratio 2.95 behave as equal pairs of the same start phase: merge from phases 0 to 90 degrees at 0.70 to 1.40 of the equal in-phase time, fly apart at 180 degrees. Rules out: beat averaging as a predictor of contact time when the contact time is a fraction of a beat; Claude Desktop's "neither merge quickly" for N ratio 1.5 and above at 3.6 hw (Block P-CD, whose reading rests on this row). Does not rule out the delayed contact at 4.8 hw (2.8 to 4.2 times for ratios 2.5 and 2.95, after 3 to 4 beats).
- Literature check (scholar), question: has published work seen contact times governed by the ratio of the interaction time to the beat period? The quantity is published as the phase accumulated before contact; no source states a criterion in terms of the ratio. (1) [Battye & Sutcliffe 2000](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-th/0003252) (READ, sec. 3.4; 1D complex Q-balls, omega_1 = 1.8, omega_2 = 1.5, boosted together at v = 0.2 from a = 6): the charge transferred depends on the relative phase at the collision, and changing the starting separation is equivalent to changing the start phase because the phase at collision shifts by the change in collision time times the frequency difference; they add that for unequal charges the start phase is not preserved even without interaction. In the project's variables the phase at contact is 2 pi times (contact time over beat period) plus the start phase (DERIVED), the ratio of this entry in another form. Moving lumps, 1D, complex field, no merger-time statistic. (2) [Xue et al. 2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.01597) (READ, secs. V and VI; 3D real oscillons, 0.01 to 0.02 c): the outcome for different oscillons depends on closing speed and initial separation because they set the relative phase at contact, and mismatched lumps move in and out of phase, so the outcome is effectively stochastic; no beat period or ratio is given. (3) [Campos et al. 2026](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12633) (READ, sec. IV): the force on an unequal pair has a slow factor at the frequency difference (eq. IV.14); the sum-frequency term is dropped on the ground that it oscillates quickly, a statement about fast against slow and not about the contact time. (4) [Buryak et al. 1998](https://arxiv.org/abs/patt-sol/9812005) (READ): the stability of spiraling is lost when the spiraling and power-exchange frequencies become commensurable (resonance windows): a ratio of two time scales deciding an outcome, for orbits and not for contact. (5) [Arenas et al. 2008](https://arxiv.org/abs/0805.2976) (READ, sec. 3.1.1): locked against drifting phase is decided by coupling against detuning; the general averaging argument (the fast phase averages out when the slow time is many periods) was not looked up in a primary source. No source found for a contact or merger time of unequal real-field lumps in units of the beat period, or for a measured delay against that ratio. Searches 156 to 166 (`PRIOR_ART.md` Addendum G).
- Interpretation notes (scholar): (i) The P2 wording "contact at least 3 times later, or not within the cap" has two failure routes in the data: contact that is not later (3.6 hw, a fraction of a beat) and non-contact that is not a drift together (flew apart at 4.2 and 4.8 hw for the in-phase starts at ratio 1.5 and 1.75, because the phase turns over before contact). Only the second alternative of the prediction, "not within the cap", is satisfied there, and not by the stated mechanism; this fits the reviewer's caveat that the held-position beat average is not a basis for "drift together". (ii) All 25 runs at 3.6 hw with ratio 1.5 and above are flagged (they come inside 3.6 hw by definition once they merge), so under Block P's rule none could have been counted as a candidate; the prediction concerns contact times, which are flagged by their nature. (iii) The miss is in the model's weakest regime; the better test of the averaging argument is at 4.2 to 4.8 hw, where contact takes one to four beats and the delay does appear (1.7 to 4.2 times).
- Cross-reference: F-020 (D1 to D3 and the label); F-019 (c) (point-model times against 3D, validity inside 3.6 hw); `PRIOR_ART.md` T3, T4, T7 (WP-P2 rows) and Addendum G; `docs/WPP_BUILD_NOTES.md` sections 2.2, 5.3, 11; `docs/SESSION_03_PREDICTIONS.md` Block P (rows P2, P-CD); reviewer finding: as in F-020.
- Status: explained in the point model (the phase is frozen at contact, D3), with the caveat that the regime is the model's edge; not testable by P3 as planned. P3 line: none expected (P3 has no 3.6 hw run); fill in only if one is added.

### F-022  WP-P2 and P4: the three candidates never came inside 6.0 hw, against "any candidate would be one that spent time inside 3.6 hw" (Block P, row P4)

- Date, session, work package, step: 2026-10-02, Session 3, WP-P2 point-model scan, Step 4 (arrangements); same files as F-020. These are the same three runs as F-020, read against P4. Count them once when summarising misses.
- Prediction on record: `docs/SESSION_03_PREDICTIONS.md`, Block P, row P4: "Overall: zero bonded and zero 'bounded, beating' groups at spacings of 3.6 hw and more over the whole scan. If the scan returns any candidate, it is one that spends time inside 3.6 hw." Basis: P1 to P3.
- Expected value or outcome, with its tolerance: zero bonded and zero "bounded, beating" among runs that stay at 3.6 hw and more; any candidate that does appear is flagged as having come inside 3.6 hw (`flag_below_3p6hw` true). No tolerance.
- Measured value or outcome: zero bonded in 1,822 runs (this half hits). Three "bounded, beating" candidates (p2_0230, p2_0234, p2_0242; F-020), all with `flag_below_3p6hw` false and closest approach 6.48, 6.48 and 6.03 hw (from dense output after the review fix). Of the 1,822 runs 798 are flagged (after the review fix reflagged 5; static pairs 116 of 250, rings 239 of 312, equal orbits 292 of 672, unequal orbiting pairs 51 of 252, alternating-size rings 100 of 336), so 1,024 stayed at 3.6 hw or more; the three candidates are 3 of those 1,024 (0.3%), all static pairs at 7.2 hw.
- Size of the miss against the tolerance: categorical. The first half of P4 is met; the second half (the rider about where a candidate would come from) is wrong for all three candidates, which are 1.7 to 1.8 times as far out as 3.6 hw (6.03 to 6.48 hw).
- Ranked causes (orchestrator and scholar, as in F-020): (1) P4 inherits P2's error: it is the same statement counted over the whole scan, and the rider has no separate basis in Block P ("P1 to P3"). (2) At 7.2 hw the forces are weak, so "did not merge within five contact units" is the default for unequal pairs; no close approach is needed to be "bounded for the run", and the label then rests on the clause margins (F-020, cause 1). (3) The flag records the closest approach in the run, not how near a run is to merging, so it cannot screen out a slow drifter that has not yet acted.
- Diagnostics run now: those of F-020 (D1, D2, D3 and the reviewer's runs to T = 60,000); none specific to P4. D1 and D2 say that the candidates close slowly (0.1 to 0.9% in five units, 1 to 10% in three times that, and, from Block P3, up to 7.8% for two of them and 19% for p2_0242 in the reviewer's runs): even the fastest closing in the record leaves them outside 3.6 hw (closest approach 5.43 hw at three times the window for p2_0242; d = 19.4, which is 5.8 hw, in the reviewer's run).
- Diagnostics proposed (expensive ones), not run: P3 (3D, running). Nothing else specific.
- What the miss rules in, and what it rules out: Rules out: the rider, and the use of the 3.6 hw flag as a screen for what counts as a candidate: a point-model "bounded, beating" label does not need a close approach. Rules in: that the candidate set is at 7.2 hw only, among static pairs only, and that the screen on 3.6 hw (798 of 1,822 runs flagged) did not remove them. Zero bonded stands.
- Literature check (scholar): see F-020; no further source. No source found for a rule that long-lived pairs of lumps arise only after a close approach. The idea behind the rider has a published counterpart in the Q-ball literature: bound, breathing systems come from slow collisions ([Axenides et al. 2000](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-ph/9910388), READ, 2D, equal charge: right-angle scattering and then a bound system that breathes) and a molecule in the exact 1D NLS forms at short separation ([Al Khawaja 2010](https://arxiv.org/abs/1004.3000), READ); neither is a pair that stays at large separation without merging, which is what the candidates are.
- Interpretation notes (scholar): (i) The rider conflates "bounded" with "came close". The runs the rider describes would be mergers and near-mergers, which the scan classifies as merged, flew apart or bounded with a flag; the candidates are the opposite, runs in which nothing happened. (ii) The bookkeeping in the status ("P2 miss, P4 miss") is correct as two failed statements and should be reported as one set of three runs.
- Cross-reference: F-020 (same runs; full diagnostics and literature check); F-021; `docs/SESSION_03_PREDICTIONS.md` Block P (row P4) and Block P3; `outputs/session3/wpP/p2/summary.json` (keys `label_counts_all`, `label_counts_not_flagged`, `flagged_below_3p6hw`, `candidates`, `reflag`).
- Status: open, as F-020. P3 result (filled in 2026-10-02): **not run for the candidates; the 3D premise fails** (the 3D sources for the two candidate partners, N/N_min about 3.1 and 4.4 at beta = 4/3, failed the settling guard at t_s = 300 and at t_s = 1,500, with omega drifts of 4.70e-4 and 1.49e-3 per 100 time units at t_s = 1,500 against a guard of 1e-4; the figures, the orchestrator's reason for not taking the second fix attempt and the scholar's checks are in F-020). For P4 specifically: there is no 3D candidate to set against the prediction that any candidate would have spent time inside 3.6 hw, so P3 gives no evidence on the rider; the miss stands as scored, and the three point-model runs never came inside 6.0 hw. This is evidence about the physical meaning of the candidates, not a test of them. The "P3 (3D, running)" in the proposed-diagnostics line above is superseded.

### F-023  WP-L1: the outright-break threshold of both 4/3 starts lies 0.017 to 0.025 beyond the predicted range for field kicks and core removal (Block L1, row L1-1)

- Date, session, work package, step: 2026-10-02, Session 3, WP-L1 (radial break and degradation scan; plan WP-L, lone-lump break test). 384 runs, 0 failed, 2 h 53 m (ac53521, merged bdd8b6c). Scored by the orchestrator in `SESSION_03_STATUS.md` ("Block L1 scored") after the reviewer recomputed every number from the raw runs; the reviewer's corrections are used throughout F-023 to F-026: the primary's N/N_min is 1.754 (the summary and Block L1 carry 1.73, taken from `WPL_BUILD_NOTES.md` section 4; `scan_summary.json` key `N_min` notes that it is not in the lifetime table at omega 0.9091, the nearest family start is at omega 0.9075 and the run's profile has omega 0.909149), the T = 2000 settled values were missing from the summary rows (`dN_over_N_settled` is null), the family comparator used the family's last window, and the slow-packet early window included the passage. Starts: dip (beta = 4/3, omega 0.906505, N/N_min 1.83), primary (beta = 4/3, omega 0.909149, N/N_min 1.754), b5 (beta = 5, omega 0.975598, N/N_min 1.97).

Basis given there: a lump at N_min at 4/3 dies at about 1,830 (Session 2 table); the N31 ceiling 1 - N_min/N of 0.45 and 0.42; "relaxation sheds a little more N". The row's literature cell: no published threshold for a real oscillon; death at the critical charge (Mukaida 2017; Ibe 2019).
- Expected value or outcome, with its tolerance: negative field kicks, negative velocity kicks and core removal break the two 4/3 starts outright (within 2,000, E inside 3 half-widths below half its pre-kick value) only beyond an imposed t = 0 dN/N of -0.30. Threshold (midpoint of the bracketing scan values) between -0.30 and -0.45 at the dip, between -0.28 and -0.42 at the primary; for field kicks in epsilon between -0.15 and -0.25. The tolerance is the band itself.
- Measured value or outcome (t = 0 dN/N, bisected, last unbroken / first broken; `bisection.json`, `scan_summary.json` rows): dip field -0.4621 / -0.4711 (midpoint -0.4666), velocity -0.4064 / -0.4159 (-0.4112), removal -0.4657 / -0.4719 (-0.4688); primary field -0.4340 / -0.4433 (-0.4386), velocity -0.3771 / -0.3868 (-0.3819), removal -0.4415 / -0.4479 (-0.4447). Field epsilon midpoints -0.2719 (dip) and -0.2531 (primary). Every break is dynamic: first below half at 106.4 (dip field), 120.7 (dip velocity), 1346.4 (dip removal), 118.9, 204.1 and 211.9 (the three primary) time units after the kick; instantaneous breaks start only at t = 0 dN/N of about -0.51 to -0.59. Settled N/N_min of the last unbroken run (reviewer): 1.00 to 1.01 at the dip, 1.01 to 1.02 at the primary; the t = 0 value of the field and removal edges is 0.966 to 0.984 and rises by 2 to 4% on relaxation. Context, b5 (L1-2, a hit): edges field -0.4804 / -0.4893, velocity -0.4624 / -0.4715, removal -0.4833 / -0.4895 (midpoints -0.4848, -0.4670, -0.4864, my arithmetic).
- Size of the miss against the tolerance: field and removal lie outside the band by 0.017 and 0.019 at the dip, 0.019 and 0.025 at the primary; the field epsilon is 0.022 outside at the dip and 0.003 outside at the primary. Velocity kicks are inside both bands. [scholar] In ratio terms the measured dN/N values are 1.04 to 1.06 times the band edge (1.09 and 1.01 in epsilon), so on `CLAUDE.md`'s "within about 3 times is a pass with a note" the numbers alone would pass; the orchestrator scored a miss because the stated mechanism (relaxation sheds N, threshold inside the N31 ceiling) is contradicted by two of the three kick kinds. The scoring call stays with the orchestrator.
- Ranked causes (orchestrator first, my additions marked [scholar]): (1) The basis assumed that relaxation sheds a little more N, so the threshold would sit inside the N31 ceiling. At the 4/3 starts field and removal kicks recover N instead, and the threshold is where the settled N reaches N_min. [scholar] The sign was already in the record when the block was written: Block L1's "what was known" paragraph gives t = 0 dN/N = -0.0198 at epsilon = -0.01 and, after relaxation, 1.908 epsilon, and `linearity.json` (field kicks) gives slopes 1.9856 epsilon at t = 0 and 1.908 epsilon relaxed, so the relaxed |dN| is 3.9% smaller than the imposed one. The basis sentence holds for velocity kicks only (-0.0198 becomes -0.0217 at epsilon = -0.01). (2) [scholar] The wrong coordinate. N at the kick phase is not conserved through relaxation, while E is conserved up to what is radiated. In t = 0 dE/E the thresholds of the three kick kinds agree to 4.5% or better (spread over the smallest): dip -0.3976 (removal), -0.4112 (velocity), -0.4155 (field); primary -0.3766, -0.3819, -0.3897; b5 -0.4670, -0.4683, -0.4716 (midpoints of the edge rows, `scan_summary.json` `t0_dE_over_E`). In t = 0 dN/N they spread over 14% (dip), 16% (primary) and 4% (b5). For a given dE a field kick at phase 0 removes 12% and a removal 18% more instantaneous N than a velocity kick at phase 90, where phi = 0 and dE/E = dN/N, so the field-against-velocity gap in N is a coordinate effect. (3) [scholar] N is only an adiabatic invariant: Ibe et al. minimise the time-averaged energy at fixed I and say I is well conserved only where the quadratic potential dominates (see the literature check). The two 4/3 starts (omega 0.9065 and 0.9091, E/N 0.975 and 0.978) are further from that limit than b5 (omega 0.9756, E/N 0.9925), and the data fit: from the last unbroken run, relaxation changes dN/N by +0.017 (dip field), +0.020 (dip removal), +0.016 (primary field), +0.021 (primary removal) and -0.036 and -0.034 (velocity) at the 4/3 starts, against -0.002, -0.002 and -0.013 (field, removal, velocity) at b5, where the N31 ceiling (0.4924) held for all three kinds. (4) [scholar] Input defect for the primary: the ceiling uses N/N_min = 1.73; with 1.754 the ceiling is 0.430, and the primary field and removal midpoints are 0.009 and 0.015 beyond it, not 0.017 and 0.023.
- Diagnostics run now: the reviewer's recomputation (settled values, bracket midpoints, dynamic times). Scholar's tabulation of rows already in `scan_summary.json` (T = 2000, window 250 to 750, no new run, not reviewed): (a) settled E/(mN) of the last unbroken run, from (E_profile/N_profile) times (1 + `EN_over_control`)/(1 + dN/N): dip 1.0153 (field), 1.0144 (velocity), 1.0144 (removal; 1.0155 at the broken edge), primary 1.0145, 1.0133, 1.0143, b5 1.0046, 1.0038, 1.0041. (b) settled omega of the same runs: dip 0.9606 to 0.9660, primary 0.9581 to 0.9637, b5 0.9894 to 0.9919, against omega at the N-minimum of 0.9700 (4/3) and 0.9921 (b5) from sqrt(1 - 0.0788/beta) (DERIVED, `PRIOR_ART.md` N8 and N17, from the universal N-minimum position of Li et al.; N17 prints 0.9692 in one line and 0.970 in the next, and the formula gives 0.9700). (c) The t = 0 to settled N/N_min of the last unbroken run: dip field 0.984 to 1.015, velocity 1.086 to 1.020, removal 0.978 to 1.015; primary 0.993 to 1.022, 1.093 to 1.032, 0.980 to 1.017. This is 1.004 to 1.020 (dip) and 1.017 to 1.032 (primary) in my window, against the reviewer's 1.00 to 1.01 and 1.01 to 1.02; the difference is the window. (d) Break time against distance from the threshold, delta in the kick amplitude from the bracket midpoint: primary field (negative), four points delta = 0.003 to 0.047, times 118.9, 92.9, 67.9, 41.9, -dt/d ln delta = 28.3 (largest residual 2.9); positive field kicks near +0.8, dip five points over delta = 0.001 to 0.19 and primary six points, slopes 24.0 and 26.0 (residuals at most 2.1). The three-point series (dip removal 1346, 136, 70; primary velocity; b5) do not fit as cleanly: the dip removal times rise by a factor of 10 for a factor of 3 in delta. The first point of each series sits at half a bracket width by construction, so the fit is indicative only.
- Diagnostics proposed (expensive ones), not run: none. Cheap, one: bisect the dip's break threshold for a field kick at start phase 45 degrees (the runner's specs carry a `phase` parameter), about 10 radial runs of T = 2000 at about 8 s each (`wall_s` in the run files). A phase-45 kick has dE/dN between the field and velocity values. The energy reading predicts a threshold at t = 0 dE/E = -0.41 +- 0.01, whatever the phase; the N reading predicts nothing universal in dN/N.
- What the miss rules in, and what it rules out: Rules in: the break is the settled lump reaching the end of its family (settled E/(mN) of 1.013 to 1.016 at 4/3 and 1.004 to 1.005 at b5, omega within 0.4 to 1.2% of the N-minimum value at 4/3 and within 0.3% at b5, for all three kick kinds), so N_min is the break criterion as the basis said; the break time rising as a logarithm of the distance from threshold where five or more points exist (24 to 28 time units per e-fold), which is what a threshold solution with one growing mode gives; kick kinds that relax differently at 4/3. Rules out: the t = 0 dN/N as the break coordinate (field and removal pass beyond the N31 ceiling by 0.009 to 0.015 with the corrected N/N_min, velocity does not); "relaxation sheds a little more N" as a general statement (true for velocity kicks and for field and removal kicks below about |dN/N| 0.35 at the dip, false above, and nearly neutral at b5); the N31 ceiling as an upper bound in imposed N. Does not rule out N31 in energy form: with E/(mN) = 1.0 at N_min (Li et al. Fig. 2, READ (figure), `PRIOR_ART.md` N35) the ceilings in dE/E are -0.439 (dip), -0.417 (primary) and -0.489 (b5), and all nine thresholds lie inside them, by 0.024 to 0.042, 0.027 to 0.040 and 0.018 to 0.022 (DERIVED; with the survivors' E/(mN) of 1.0145 instead the dip and primary ceilings are -0.431 and -0.408, inside by 0.015 to 0.033 and 0.018 to 0.031).
- Literature check (scholar): Seen in the slow-path form, not as a kick threshold. (1) Death at the end of the family after a start far from it: [Saffin & Tranberg 2006](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-th/0610191) (READ, secs. 1, 3.3, 4.1; symmetric double-well phi^4, D = 3, Gaussian start A = 2, sigma = 3.3, not fine-tuned): the quasi-breather energy has a minimum at omega_crit (about 0.967 there), oscillons move toward it as they radiate and decay on reaching it because no quasi-breather of lower energy exists; the Gaussian start sheds energy, approaches the quasi-breather and decays like it, about 600 time units after crossing omega_crit; quasi-breather starts above omega_crit showed no oscillon phase and, except at D = 3, dissipated rapidly into radiation (sec. 4.4, Fig. 12). [Salmi & Hindmarsh 2012](https://arxiv.org/abs/1201.1934) (READ, introduction and conclusion): an oscillon demises on reaching its minimum energy state, shown in 3D phi^4. [Ibe et al. 2019](https://arxiv.org/abs/1901.06130) (READ): the lump minimises the time-averaged energy at fixed I; I approximately conserved where the quadratic potential dominates; the decay comes from the deviation from it. Mukaida 2017, Zhang 2020 and Li 2025 (READ, T1 and T9 of `PRIOR_ART.md`): a lump driven to the critical charge or frequency decays fast. These put the break at the family's end, in energy as well as charge, as the data do. (2) A published threshold in kick amplitude of a settled real lump: no source found (searches 120 to 122, 142, 146, 147, 167 to 175). Neighbours: the unstable branch of the 3D cubic-quintic NLS separates dispersal from relaxation to a stable lump with about 3% mass radiated ([Carles, Klein, Sparber 2021](https://arxiv.org/abs/2012.11637), READ in T1); complex Q-balls near the cusp have a vibrational mode that continues the unstable branch's decay mode ([Kovtun, Nugaev, Shkerin 2018](https://arxiv.org/abs/1805.03518), READ in the abstract, sec. 3 and the conclusion; piecewise-parabolic and thin-wall sextic potentials, omega_c about 0.960 in their units; context). (3) Logarithmic divergence of the lifetime near a threshold: [Honda & Choptuik 2002](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-ph/0110065) (READ, results on resonances and time scaling; 3+1D symmetric double-well phi^4): 125 resonances in the lifetime against the Gaussian bubble radius for 2.0 to 5.0; near each, lifetime of the form gamma ln|r0 - r0*| on both sides, gamma from resonance to resonance, read as the reciprocal Lyapunov exponent of one unstable shape mode (Type I critical solution, their field period 4.6, their units). [Ikeda, Yoo, Cardoso 2017](https://arxiv.org/abs/1708.01344) (READ) report the same scaling with gravity and attribute the flat-space result to Honda and Choptuik and to Fodor et al. The control parameter there is the initial bubble radius, not a kick of a settled lump, and the numbers are not comparable with 24 to 28 time units in this potential. Searches 167 to 175 (`PRIOR_ART.md` Addendum H).
- Interpretation notes (scholar): (i) **The block's variable was the problem, not its mechanism.** The same N31 argument in energy gives a ceiling that every threshold respects, and the thresholds in dE/E collapse to 4.5% or better across kick kinds. A sharper prediction would have been written as "break when E_lump(settled) = E(N_min)", with E_lump = E_0 + dE - E_radiated. (ii) **The data make the N-recovery at 4/3 a property of the starts.** b5 changes dN/N by at most 0.003 (field, removal) or 0.013 (velocity) between t = 0 and settled, the two 4/3 starts by 0.016 to 0.036; this is the ordering one expects if I is better conserved nearer the non-relativistic limit. Not tested. (iii) **L1-6 covers |dN/N| up to 0.1 and so not this regime**; the edge survivors' omega and E/(mN) give an independent check of the settled state (see the hits paragraph after F-026). (iv) **The log fit is a three-series result.** It should be reported as "consistent with one growing mode of e-folding time 24 to 28 at these starts", not as a measured exponent.
