(v) **Horizon dependence.** A break time that rises as a logarithm of the distance from threshold (24 to 28 time units per e-fold) makes the T = 2,000 threshold essentially the long-time threshold for the series that fit. The dip removal series does not fit and has a break at 1346.4, within 650 time units of the horizon; for it a longer horizon could move the edge toward smaller |dN/N|, that is toward the predicted band, not away from it.
- Cross-reference: `PRIOR_ART.md` T1, T7, T9, N31, N32, N35, N17 and Addendum H; `docs/SESSION_03_PREDICTIONS.md` Block L1 (rows L1-1, L1-2); `docs/SESSION_03_STATUS.md` ("Block L1 scored"); `docs/WPL_BUILD_NOTES.md` (t = 0 table, linearity); F-023 to F-026 share the scan and the reviewer's corrections; reviewer finding: the primary N/N_min.
- Status: explained (the break is at the family's end; the prediction's coordinate was wrong; partly tested by rows already on file). One cheap diagnostic proposed. Rerun not needed.
- Diagnostic run (2026-10-05, `outputs/session3/diagnostics/`, commit 400d1ec): phase 45 needs a code change and was not run. Substituted: the kick shape varied, at (1,2), (2,3) and (3,4) hw, for field and velocity kicks at the dip, bisected to 0.004 in dE/E. The t = 0 dE/E at threshold is -0.4077 to -0.4138 for all six (spread 1.5%); dN/N spreads 13%. Supports the energy reading; cause 1 (N shed on relaxation) not separately tested. The narrow-shape breaks come at 1,794 to 1,955, near the horizon.

### F-024  WP-L1: the family rule for the loss rate holds for 8 of 15 comparable runs early and 7 of 15 late, against at least 80% (Block L1, row L1-5)

- Date, session, work package, step: 2026-10-02, Session 3, WP-L1; same files and corrections as F-023. T = 30,000 grid (78 runs) with the T = 2,000 grid for the early window.
- Prediction on record: `docs/SESSION_03_PREDICTIONS.md`, Block L1, row L1-5: for spherical disturbances with |dN/N| up to 0.2, the loss rate after relaxation (window t_d + 500 to t_d + 2,000, and the late windows of the 30,000 runs) equals the undisturbed family's rate at the same settled N within a factor of 3, wherever a family start lies within 1% in N/N_min; hit if at least 80% of the comparable runs pass. Basis: one adiabatic curve per beta (Session 2, F-007 diagnostics). Literature cell: published lifetime estimates assume this path (Ibe 2019 eq. 55; Li 2025 eq. 2.30).
- Expected value or outcome, with its tolerance: at least 80% of the comparable runs within a factor of 3 of the family rate at the same N.
- Measured value or outcome (reviewer's recount): 8 of 15 comparable runs in the early window, 7 of 15 late. Kicks alone: 7 of 9 early, 5 of 9 late. The 6 packet runs at almost unchanged N have 4 to 31 times the family rate in both windows, a lasting excess; the dip k3 packet at 0.05 E is 16 times the family rate at 30,000. The family rate swings by more than 100 times between N/N_min 1.80 and 1.83. [scholar] The counts do not add up as stated: 8 = 7 + 1 puts one packet run inside 3x early, and 7 = 5 + 2 puts two inside late, so "4 to 31 times in both windows" can hold for at most 5 and 4 of the 6 packet runs. Please confirm which.
- Size of the miss against the tolerance: 53% early and 47% late against 80%, a shortfall of 1.5 to 1.7 times in the pass fraction; the individual packet runs miss by up to 31 times in rate (beyond 3 times).
- Ranked causes (orchestrator first, additions marked [scholar]): (1) The packet runs leave a lasting excess at unchanged N, a state variable the family rule does not carry. (2) The family rate at the dip is so steep in N that a 1% match gives only about 2 times of slack in the rate (orchestrator). [scholar] My arithmetic from the Block L1 basis values (1.7e-5 at N/N_min 1.80, 3.5e-9 at 1.83, 1.1e-7 at 1.857; the summary's family column reproduces them and the reviewer found it read from the wrong window, so treat them as the basis' own numbers) gives a factor 2 per 0.13% in N between 1.80 and 1.83 and about 190 per 1% (DERIVED, log-linear between two points; the shape between them is not known). The "2 times of slack" fits a mismatch of about 0.13%, not 1%. The packets' settled shifts are dN/N = -0.0032 (dip k3 0.05), -0.0016, -0.0045 and -0.0007 (dip slow 0.05, 0.2, 0.5): on that flank these move the family rate by 5.3, 2.3, 10.5 and 1.4 times. Against the reviewer's 16 times for the dip k3 packet at 0.05 E this leaves about 3 times (still crude). The tolerance in the prediction itself (1% in N/N_min) is far looser than the dip needs. (3) [scholar] The late clause compares unlike things. A lump with a rate of 1e-5 to 3e-5 loses of order 10 to 30% of its N over 1e4 time units, so the late rate (mean of the 500-wide windows after t_d + 2000 while alive) is an average along the path from the settled N down the family, not the rate at the settled N. Ibe's and Li's lifetime formulas integrate the family rate along the path for this reason. This is a design flaw in the late clause and fits late being worse than early for kicks (5 of 9 against 7 of 9). (4) [scholar] The early window for kicks measures a transient excitation: see the diagnostics. (5) [scholar] b5 comparisons sit at the measurement floor: 7 of the 26 b5 early rates in the T = 30,000 table of `scan_summary.md` are negative, as is the b5 velocity control (-1.7e-10), and the per-window sigma of the rate in `gamma_windows` is about 5e-8 against family rates of 1e-11 to 1e-10.
- Diagnostics run now: the reviewer's recount. Scholar's tabulation from the T = 30,000 rows (not reviewed): the early-window rate of dip kicks scales as epsilon squared with no dependence on sign beyond a constant 1.4 to 1.5: Gamma over epsilon squared is 7.5e-3, 7.4e-3, 7.7e-3 (field, epsilon -0.01, -0.02, -0.05), 5.2e-3, 5.2e-3, 4.3e-3 (field +0.01, +0.02, +0.05), 9.4e-3, 9.5e-3, 9.9e-3 (velocity -), 6.6e-3, 6.4e-3, 5.0e-3 (velocity +). The late rates are not quadratic: 1.24e-5 and 2.42e-5 at field -0.01 and -0.02 (a factor 1.95 for a factor 2 in epsilon), 2.6e-7 and 6.7e-7 at +0.01 and +0.02. That is an excitation radiating in the early window (energy in the excitation proportional to epsilon squared) and the family flank in the late window. For packets (summary values, slow-packet early window includes the passage and is superseded by the reviewer): dip late rates 4.2e-8 (k3 0.05), 1.5e-8, 7.7e-8, 7.0e-9 (slow 0.05, 0.2, 0.5) against the family 3.5e-9 and a control of 3.2e-9.
- Diagnostics proposed (expensive ones), not run: none. Cheap, one: three dip family starts at the packet runs' settled N/N_min (1.821, 1.824, 1.827, from the settled dN/N above and N/N_min 1.8295) run radially to T = 30,000, and the packet runs compared with the family rate at the same omega (the omega shift follows N to 2% in these runs and is measured far more finely than N: a dip width of 1.7% in N is 0.001 in omega). If the excess falls to 3 times or less, the lasting excitation is an N-resolution artifact at the dip; if it stays, it is real.
- What the miss rules in, and what it rules out: Rules in: a transient excitation after kicks, with energy in it proportional to epsilon squared and a lifetime of order the early window; a possible lasting excitation after packets, not yet separated from the dip's N resolution. Rules out: the rule "same settled N, same rate within 3 times" as a test with a 1% N tolerance at the dip; the late clause as written. Does not rule out the family rate as a function of omega or N for lumps without extra excitation: for the four dip kicks at +-0.01 the late rates (1.24e-5, 1.75e-5, 2.6e-7, 3.1e-7) lie within 3 times of the Block L1 basis values at the nearest start (1.7e-5 and 1.1e-7; same caveat on the comparator).
- Literature check (scholar), questions: has published work seen a lump keep a long-lived excitation at unchanged charge, and does the adiabatic path hold for kicked lumps? (1) The family rule for lumps started on the family is published: [Ibe et al. 2019](https://arxiv.org/abs/1901.06130) (READ, `PRIOR_ART.md` line 450): the rate is a function of the instantaneous charge and runs from three different starting omega follow one curve. Applied to a kicked or pulsed lump with a measured rate: no source found. (2) Excitations at fixed charge that decay slowly, in 1+1D: [Ciurla et al. 2024](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.06591) (READ, sec. 3.5; complex phi^6 Q-ball, beta = 1/4, omega = sqrt(3)/2, squash lambda = 1.05, "these deformations do not affect the charge"): the perturbation decays with a rate that slows and a pattern like A(t) of order t^(-1/2) (the Manton-Merabet law for the phi^4 kink shape mode); relaxation is dominated by the decay of a normal mode through nonlinear coupling to scattering modes and a long-lasting quasi-normal mode (abstract). [Barashenkov & Oxtoby 2009](https://arxiv.org/abs/0907.3611) (READ, abstract and decay-law section; 1+1D phi^4 kink): the wobbling amplitude decays as t^(-1/2), appreciably only after times of order |a(0)|^(-2), so a weaker excitation lasts longer. [Romanczukiewicz & Shnir 2017](https://arxiv.org/abs/1706.09234) (READ, abstract, secs. 2.3, A.1; 1+1D): an oscillon and the oscillational mode of the phi^4 kink are hard to tell apart over moderately long evolution and the mode decays by the same power law. (3) Excited oscillons and lifetimes that depend on more than the family variable: [Honda & Choptuik 2002](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-ph/0110065) (READ; 3+1D phi^4): a large-period amplitude modulation is present in all typical oscillons from Gaussian data and disappears late in their life near a resonance, and the lifetime has 125 resonances against the initial radius. [Blaschke et al. 2024](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.00443) (READ; 1+1D quasi-quadratic potential, m = 5, Gaussian data A_0 exp(-x^2/10)): strongly excited oscillons are multi-oscillon quasi-bound states whose decay is chaotic and fractal in A_0 through resonant energy transfer from amplitude modulation. [Saffin & Tranberg 2006](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-th/0610191) (READ, sec. 4.1; 3D phi^4): the frequency of a Gaussian-start oscillon has a large modulation until it reaches the quasi-breather attractor; the quasi-breather reading is good to a delay of about 10% of the lifetime there. Not found: a spherical wave packet leaving such an excitation in a real 3D oscillon, or a measured rate excess against the family at the same N. Searches 167 to 175 (`PRIOR_ART.md` Addendum H).
- Interpretation notes (scholar): (i) **Two different things are scored together.** The kick excess is transient and quadratic in epsilon (and the family rule works late for the four dip kicks at +-0.01); the packet excess may be lasting. A rate test needs the family rate at the lump's own omega, not at the nearest start. (ii) **The window clause cannot test "same N" for runs that die.** A test in integrated form (death time against the family's remaining lifetime from the Session 2 table at the settled N) uses data already on file (27 of the 30,000-unit runs at scale 1 die; deaths 9,510 to 28,938 time units after the kick). Not run. (iii) **The 1% tolerance came from the build's convenience, not from the dip's width.** (iv) **b5 comparisons need an absolute floor**; a ratio test below about 1e-8 tests noise.
- Cross-reference: F-023; `PRIOR_ART.md` T1, T7, N32 and Addendum H; `docs/SESSION_03_PREDICTIONS.md` Block L1 (rows L1-5, L1-6, L1-7); reviewer finding: the family comparator and the slow-packet window.
- Status: open (the packet excess is not separated from the N-resolution at the dip; the kick excess is explained as a transient, not tested). One cheap diagnostic proposed.
- Diagnostic run (2026-10-05, 400d1ec): family starts made at N/N_min 1.821, 1.824 and 1.827 (T = 30,000). The family rate changes 5 times over 0.33% in N. Against them the packet runs are 0.88 to 1.15 of the family rate (slow 0.2 E; k3 0.05 E; slow 0.05 E), while 4 to 24 times their control. So the packet excess is the family flank at the packets' settled N, not a lasting excitation, in these three cases: the miss is in the test's 1% N matching (cause 1), not in the family rule.

### F-025  WP-L1: degradation ordering holds, but the dip's upper edge, the primary's lower edge and one b5 clause miss their bands (Block L1, row L1-8)

- Date, session, work package, step: 2026-10-02, Session 3, WP-L1; same files and corrections as F-023.
- Prediction on record: `docs/SESSION_03_PREDICTIONS.md`, Block L1, row L1-8: rate after the disturbance over the control's rate at matched |dN/N| of 2% to 20%: dip 100 to 1e4 (most degraded); primary 0.3 to 30 (its control already loses 4.2e-6); b5 keeps an absolute rate below 1e-7 for every |dN/N| up to 0.2. Basis: Session 2 family rates. Literature cell: the dip start is the most fragile in lifetime terms (Claude Desktop, no source); the effect depends on the sign (T7).
- Expected value or outcome, with its tolerance: the three bands above.
- Measured value or outcome (reviewer): dip early 316 to 1.63e4; the runs named are field -0.05 at 4.8e3 (inside), velocity -0.05 at 1.19e4, removal 0.1 at 1.06e4 and velocity +0.1 at 1.63e4 (the last three above 1e4). Primary 0.0066 to 13.9; the +0.02 kicks land in the dip and lower the rate by up to 150 times. b5 kicks at most 5.4e-9 (hit); the b5 slow packet at 0.5 E after the passage 1.5e-7 (above 1e-7).
- Size of the miss against the tolerance: dip upper edge 1.63e4 against 1e4, 1.6 times (within about 3 times); primary lower edge 0.0066 against 0.3, 45 times below (beyond); b5 slow packet 1.5e-7 against 1e-7, 1.5 times (within about 3 times). The ordering (dip most degraded, b5 least) holds.
- Ranked causes: (1) Primary: N32 was applied to the dip start only. With the corrected N/N_min of 1.754 the primary sits 4.1% below the dip start (1.8295), so +0.04 in dN/N (field +0.02: settled +0.0439; velocity +0.02: settled +0.0487) puts it at N/N_min 1.831 and 1.839, the dip's own centre, where the family rate is about 3.5e-9. The measured rates of the two runs are 1.2e-7 and 2.7e-8 early and 2.2e-7 and 3.5e-8 late, against the primary control's 4.07e-6 early and 2.63e-5 late. [scholar] This is the same sign argument as L1-7, applied in the direction that helps the primary. The 1.73 in Block L1 hid the 4% distance. (2) Dip upper edge: the early window includes the excitation transient of F-024 (rate proportional to epsilon squared), which the family-based band did not include; the +0.1 kicks have early rates (3.1e-5 field, 3.4e-5 velocity) about 80 times the family rate at the nearest family start (N/N_min 2.006, 3% from their settled value, 4.0e-7) and late rates 7 times it. (3) [scholar] The dip's control rate is itself uncertain by a factor 1.9: the summary has 3.97e-9 for field, removal and packet runs and 2.07e-9 for velocity runs (same lump, different kick phase and window; late 3.21e-9 for both), at the 1e-9 level of the fit. Against the field control the two velocity runs above 1e4 become 6.2e3 and 8.5e3, inside the band; only removal 0.1 (1.06e4, 6% above) stays out. (4) b5: the slow packet is absorbed, see below; the 1e-7 bound was written for changes of N and rests on Session 2 family rates (1e-11 to 4.5e-10 for N/N_min 1.8 to 2.5), with no packet case.
- Diagnostics run now: the reviewer's numbers. Scholar's tabulation (summary rows, not reviewed): b5 slow packet settled dN/N +0.0013, +0.0210, +0.1040 at 0.05, 0.2, 0.5 E, with frac at 30,000 of 1.00, 1.02, 1.10: the lump gains N and energy from the packet, dN/N rising as efrac to the power 2.0 (0.05 to 0.2) and 1.75 (0.2 to 0.5), and the absorbed N is 2.6%, 10.5% and 21% of the packet's energy fraction. At the dip and primary the slow packets change N by under 1% (-0.0016, -0.0045, -0.0007 and -0.0010, -0.0021, +0.0069). The k3 packets lower N at b5 at every energy (-0.006 to -0.18 for 0.05 to 2 E).
- Diagnostics proposed (expensive ones), not run: none. Cheap, one (analysis of existing data): re-tabulate all dip ratios against a single control rate from the same window for every kick kind, with the control's rate over three window starts, so that the band edge is scored against the control's own scatter.
- What the miss rules in, and what it rules out: Rules in: the ordering; b5 robustness to kicks (at most 5.4e-9 out to |dN/N| of 0.2); absorption of a strong slow packet by b5; the primary's +dN route into the dip. Rules out: the primary lower edge of 0.3, the single bound of 1e-7 for b5 across disturbance kinds, and the dip upper edge of 1e4 as a hard limit for an early window with a transient. Whether the dip excess is more than a window effect is not settled (F-024).
- Literature check (scholar): Ordering half supported: lifetime changes by large factors with a small change in N near a dip (Li et al. 2025 Fig. 3: initial energies 35 and 41 give a death near 2.1e4 and a lump alive at 5e5, READ (figure), T1; [Zhang 2020b](https://arxiv.org/abs/2011.11720) sec. 2.1, READ: a lump starting below the dip rises into it and spends most of its life there). Nothing for a degradation ratio or for the primary's flank. Nonlinear absorption of a strong wave by an oscillon or Q-ball: no source found. Closest: linear scattering of a monochromatic wave by a Q-ball with no charge change at linear order and radiation pressure of either sign ([Ciurla et al. 2024](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.06591), READ, sec. 4, T2); a plane wave of a few percent of the peak pushes a 3D real oscillon and does not destroy or absorb it ([Xue et al. 2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.01597), READ, T2); resonant excitation by incoming waves in 1+1D with two vacua ([Romanczukiewicz & Shnir 2010](https://arxiv.org/abs/1002.4484), READ, T2); the radiation losses of a wobbling kink compensated by resonant driving with multistability ([Oxtoby & Barashenkov 2009](https://arxiv.org/abs/0907.3615), READ in the abstract and introduction; external driving, 1+1D); quasi-breathers as strictly periodic solutions that need ingoing radiation, not expected to be physical ([Saffin & Tranberg 2006](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-th/0610191), READ). Wave-turbulence condensation of NLS solitons that grow in contact with incoherent waves came only from search summaries (SECONDHAND, search 177; pointers arXiv:0904.2552, 2003.05558; not opened). The "Q-balls absorb incoming particles in a thermal bath" statement in a search summary (176) could not be traced to a paper I opened. Searches 170, 174, 176 to 178 (`PRIOR_ART.md` Addendum H).
- Interpretation notes (scholar): (i) The three clauses are three different failures: a mis-sized input (1.73), an unmodelled transient plus a control scatter, and a wrong assumption of what packets do at b5. Only the primary clause is beyond 3 times. (ii) The b5 absorption is a measured fact worth keeping for the report (N and E gained from a 0.5 E slow packet), with no published comparison found; novelty wording: "no source found", not "new" (rule 10).
- Cross-reference: F-023, F-024, F-026; `PRIOR_ART.md` T2, T7, N32 and Addendum H; `docs/SESSION_03_PREDICTIONS.md` Block L1 (rows L1-7, L1-8).
- Status: explained for the primary clause (input and sign), partly explained for the dip and b5 clauses; rerun not needed.

### F-026  WP-L1: the primary-to-dip ratio of |dN/N| after a k3 packet is at least 2 only up to 0.05 E (Block L1, row L1-9)

- Date, session, work package, step: 2026-10-02, Session 3, WP-L1; same files and corrections as F-023. k3 packets (k = 2.5302 dip, 2.5392 primary, 2.7507 b5; sigma 5.0), efrac 0.01 to 2.
- Prediction on record: `docs/SESSION_03_PREDICTIONS.md`, Block L1, row L1-9: at equal packet energy |dN/N| after the packet is at least 2 times larger for the primary than for the dip, because at a dip the lump's 3 omega source nearly vanishes. Basis and literature cell: N33 (derived; Zhang 2020; Ibe 2019).
- Expected value or outcome, with its tolerance: ratio of at least 2 at equal packet energy (no energy range stated).
- Measured value or outcome (reviewer): ratio primary over dip by efrac 0.01: 36; 0.02: 7.1; 0.05: 2.58; 0.1: 1.73; 0.2: 1.38; 0.5: 1.17; 1: 1.10; 2: 1.07.
- Size of the miss against the tolerance: hit for efrac up to 0.05 (3 of 8), miss for 0.1 to 2 (5 of 8); the largest shortfall is 2 against 1.07, 1.9 times (within about 3 times). [scholar] The ratio depends on the window: from `scan_summary.json` I get 2.2 (window t_pass to t_pass + 200) and 2.3 (250 to 750) at 0.05, 1.5 and 1.6 at 0.1, against the reviewer's 2.58 and 1.73, so the 0.05 pass is marginal.
- Ranked causes: (1) The N33 argument holds at small amplitude only: while the packet's own dN stays inside the dip's width the lump stays decoupled, and once it passes about a third to a half of the width (1.7% in N, Block L1 basis) the dip's coupling is that of the primary. (2) [scholar] The dip's response is steeply superlinear at small amplitude. From the (250 to 750) windows of `scan_summary.json` the dip's |dN/N| is 6e-5, 4.8e-4, 3.2e-3, 1.1e-2, 3.4e-2, 0.110 at efrac 0.01, 0.02, 0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.5, exponents about 3.0, 2.0, 1.8, 1.6 and 1.3 between neighbours; the primary's is 1.8e-3, 3.0e-3, 7.3e-3, 1.7e-2, 4.3e-2, 0.122, exponents 0.7, 1.0, 1.2, 1.3, 1.15. The ratio falls toward 1 where the dip's exponent meets the primary's. (3) [scholar] The ratio of 36 at 0.01 rests on a dip |dN/N| of about 5e-5, which may be near the floor of the period-averaged estimator (no floor is stated in the files I read).
- Diagnostics run now: the reviewer's ratios; scholar's windows above. At b5 the same packets give dN/N = -0.0006, -0.0017, -0.0057, -0.0118, -0.0195, -0.043, -0.095, -0.183, roughly proportional to efrac up to 0.1, and the sign is negative at every energy and start (one packet phase only).
- Diagnostics proposed (expensive ones), not run: none. Cheap, one: a finer efrac grid at the dip (0.03, 0.04, 0.07, 0.15, 0.3), six radial runs of T = 2000 at about 8 s each, to place the crossover where the ratio passes 2 and to see whether it sits at a fixed |dN/N| of the dip (the width reading) or at a fixed efrac.
- What the miss rules in, and what it rules out: Rules in: the 3 omega argument at amplitudes where the packet leaves the lump inside the dip (ratios 36, 7.1, 2.6). Rules out: the unqualified "at equal packet energy" statement over 0.1 to 2 E; a fixed-ratio reading of the dip's insensitivity.
- Literature check (scholar): the vanishing 3 omega source at rate dips is published for the lump's own emission ([Zhang et al. 2020](https://arxiv.org/abs/2004.01202), [Ibe et al. 2019](https://arxiv.org/abs/1901.06130), READ, `PRIOR_ART.md` line 179); that an incoming wave at 3 omega couples weakly at a dip follows from the same source by reciprocity (DERIVED, N33), with no source found for a tuned packet on an oscillon (searches 146, 170, 174, 178; `PRIOR_ART.md` T2, Addendum H). Resonant excitation of a lump by waves in 1+1D: [Romanczukiewicz & Shnir 2010](https://arxiv.org/abs/1002.4484) (READ, T2). A superlinear response that saturates as the lump leaves a narrow window: no source found.
- Interpretation notes (scholar): (i) The prediction's physics is intact in its regime; the written form had no amplitude limit. (ii) k3 packets drain the lump at every efrac and start in this scan, from one packet phase; N33 said the sign depends on the phase at arrival (feeds or drains), which the scan does not vary.
- Cross-reference: F-024, F-025; `PRIOR_ART.md` T2, N33 and Addendum H; `docs/SESSION_03_PREDICTIONS.md` Block L1 (row L1-9).
- Status: explained (self-detuning out of the dip, from the windows on file), untested by a new run; one cheap diagnostic proposed.
- Diagnostic run (2026-10-05, 400d1ec): k3 packets at 0.03, 0.04, 0.07, 0.15 and 0.3 E at the dip and the primary. Primary/dip ratio 4.14, 3.10, 2.58 (0.05, existing), 2.06, 1.50, 1.27; it crosses 2 at about 0.075 E, where the dip lump's |dN/N| is 0.0067, about 40% of the dip's 1.7% width. Consistent with cause 1 (self-detuning out of the dip); fixed efrac and fixed |dN/N| are not separated.

**Hits of Block L1 that bear on F-023 to F-026 (no miss; two items).**

- **L1-6 (omega shift follows the family's d omega/dN, 84 of 85 runs within 20%): hit.** It bears on the misses in two ways. First, at the amplitudes it covers (|dN/N| up to 0.1) the settled omega agrees with the settled N, so the settled N is a state of the lump on the family curve, and the dip k3 packet at 0.05 E sits on the family in (omega, N): its d omega over dN/N is -0.0625 against the family's -0.0613 (-0.117 over 1.908), within 2%, the rounding of the two-digit table values. If a rate excess is real there, it lies in a variable outside (omega, N). Second, the N recovery of F-023 appears at |dN/N| of about 0.35 to 0.47, outside the 0.1 range, so L1-6 neither confirms nor contradicts it; the edge survivors' omega and E/(mN) in F-023 are an independent check. I did not find which of the 85 runs is the exception.
- **L1-7 (sign asymmetry at the dip, late window): hit as written; the early window shows none (1.4 to 1.5).** Late rates at epsilon = -0.01, +0.01, -0.02, +0.02: field 1.24e-5, 2.6e-7, 2.4e-5, 6.7e-7 (ratios 47 and 36); velocity 1.75e-5, 3.1e-7, 2.6e-5, 7.6e-7 (57 and 34), against a late control of 3.2e-9. Early, the rate follows epsilon squared with the sign factor 1.4 to 1.5 (F-024). So the asymmetry predicted from the family flank appears when the transient has gone, which is also why the early-window ratios of F-025 and the early counts of F-024 carry the excitation and the late ones carry the path.

### F-027  WP-L3: stretching the dip lump at fixed N costs 4.4e-3 in frac at eps 0.1 and 1.75e-2 at eps 0.2, against 1e-3 and 1e-2, because the lump sheds N (Block L3, row L3-2)

- Date, session, work package, step: 2026-10-05, Session 3, WP-L3 (3D distortions, collisions and spot checks; plan WP-L, 3D part). Mac set only: the cloud batch (four instances, and the P3 runs) is lost with AWS closed, so 21 result files exist: the primary gate and gate check, six primary collisions, the dip source, the dip (d) runs (control, stretch 0.1 and 0.2, random multiplier 0.1 and 0.15 with seed 1) and two linear twins. Results in the integration worktree `.claude/worktrees/integration/outputs/session3/wpL3/`: `summary.json`, `results/d_dip_control.json`, `d_dip_q0.1.json`, `d_dip_q0.2.json`, `twin_d_dip_q0.1.json`. Production grid (N = 160, L = 88, dx 0.55, dt 0.04), T = 2000, dip start (the 3D settled omega is 0.906883; the radial dip start is 0.906505). Scored in `SESSION_03_STATUS.md` ("Block L3 scored") after the reviewer recomputed every number from the saved series; the reviewer's numbers are used below. Missing, and bearing on the reading: the stretch at eps 0.05 and the primary and b5 (d) runs (cloud jobs), the 3D spot checks (L3-8), and the box and resolution checks (L3-11). The Mac collisions used the primary start (the dip collisions were lost); the (d) runs here are the dip start.
- Prediction on record: `docs/SESSION_03_PREDICTIONS.md`, Block L3 (written 2026-10-02 14:25 +07, before any L3 run), row L3-2, "Stretch costs little". Basis given there: Delta N = 0 at t = 0, so only the energy delivered (Delta E / E 1.8e-3 at eps 0.1 and 6.4e-3 at 0.2; at most 0.64%) is shed as radiation. Literature cell: as row L3-1, elliptical and generic kicks relax (Hindmarsh & Salmi 2006; Kinach & Choptuik 2025), no source for the sextic family in 3D (T1, T7).
- Expected value or outcome, with its tolerance: frac(2000) minus the control's within 1e-3 for eps up to 0.1, and within 1e-2 for eps 0.2.
- Measured value or outcome (reviewer): frac(2000) minus control -4.4e-3 at eps 0.1 (0.99611 against 1.00054) and -1.75e-2 at eps 0.2 (0.98309). The stretched lumps shed N by t of about 60: settled Delta N -14.9 and -54.5 (-0.49% and -1.81%). Their omega shifts, +3.0e-4 and +1.12e-3, follow the family d omega/dN (-2.07e-5) to 4%. The frac shift is explained to about 1e-3 by dE = omega x dN_settled. The shed energy by t = 300 is 3.3 times the delivered energy: the deficit forms in the first 300 time units. Late loss rate over [1000, 2000]: 6.5e-8 (eps 0.1) and 6.1e-7 (eps 0.2), against 6e-9 for the control (11 and 100 times). No outright break (L3-1 hit at the dip).
- Size of the miss against the tolerance: eps 0.1: 4.4 times the tolerance (beyond about 3 times: a miss). eps 0.2: 1.75 times (within about 3 times: a pass with a note). eps 0.05: no run. [scholar] If the shed N scales as the delivered energy (cause 2), eps 0.05 would settle at about -0.13% in N and shift frac by about -1.2e-3, at the tolerance; untested.
- Ranked causes: (1) [scholar] The basis counted only the delivered energy and took "Delta N = 0 at t = 0" to mean that no N is lost. A lump pushed off its family sheds N while it radiates the excitation, and along the family N and energy are tied by dE = omega dN, so the lump ends below the control's energy by omega times the N it shed, not just back at it. The numbers: delivered 1.8e-3 and 6.4e-3 of E, settled N shed 0.49% and 1.81% of N, that is 2.7 and 2.8 times the fractional energy delivered; frac shift = omega dN / E, with omega N / E = 0.93 at the dip. A tolerance of 1e-3 then required a shed of N below 0.11%. (2) [scholar] The shed is second order in eps, like the delivered energy: the settled dN grows 3.66 times for a doubling of eps (exponent 1.9), the delivered energy 3.56 times. A first-order argument ("a distortion at fixed N costs nothing") gives no warning of that. (3) [scholar] The candidate channel is the lump's own quadrupole ring-down. From the saved `series.shape` (not reviewed, no run made): the signed quadrupole of the energy density, (M_zz - (M_xx + M_yy)/2) over the trace, oscillates at omega about 0.16 to 0.19 (period 35 to 40); its rms falls from 0.055 (t 0 to 40) to 0.022 (40 to 80), 0.006 (80 to 120) and 0.0028 (120 to 200) at eps 0.1 (0.102, 0.042, 0.011, 0.005 at eps 0.2), with a revival to 0.008 and 0.015 in 200 to 400 and 0.0014 and 0.0026 in 400 to 800. The sphere energy (r < 10.18, 3 hw) crosses below the control at t = 23 to 25 and has made 96% (eps 0.1) and 92% (eps 0.2) of its t = 2000 deficit by t = 100 (-13.0 against -13.6; -45.8 against -50.0). The energy drop follows the cumulative squared quadrupole with a lag of about 17 time units (50% of the drop at t = 33 against 50% of the cumulative quadrupole power at t = 16). So the deficit forms while the mode rings down, within about 100 time units, not over 300. (4) Not the cause, from the reviewer's numbers: numerics. The energy bookkeeping closes to 5.5e-6, the twin disperses (0.018 at T = 500, L3-10 hit), the omega shift follows the family to 4%.
- Diagnostics run now: the reviewer's numbers; the [scholar] tabulation above. The energy delivered inside the sphere is 4.77 and 16.8 (1.62e-3 and 5.7e-3 of the sphere's energy), against 1.8e-3 and 6.4e-3 for the whole box; I did not find why they differ (probably tail energy outside the sphere). [scholar] A bookkeeping check, DERIVED (N36, `PRIOR_ART.md` J2.1): if radiation of frequency omega_r carries N = E / omega_r (the non-relativistic limit; only approximate for a real field), the energy shed is delta E / (1 - omega / omega_r); the observed 3.3 to 3.5 times corresponds to omega_r of 1.2 to 1.3 m, above the mass gap and below the 2.72 of the lump's ordinary 3 omega emission (which would give 1.5 times). Waves near the gap are slow (group speed 0.4 to 0.6 for 1.08 to 1.25 m, against 0.93 at 3 omega), which fits a lag of this size between the quadrupole power and the energy leaving the sphere, but the lag is a rough reading of two smoothed curves and is not a test.
- Diagnostics proposed (expensive ones), not run: the stretch at eps 0.05 and the primary and b5 stretch runs of the lost cloud set (about 4 to 10 job-hours each at N = 160 to 216). Cheap, one: repeat the eps 0.1 stretch on the Mac to T = 150 with the field recorded at a few hundred points on a shell of radius 25 (about 12 to 15 minutes at N = 160, going by the 556 s of the v = 0.2 gate at T = 120) and take the spectrum of the outgoing wave over t = 30 to 150. A peak near 1.2 to 1.3 m supports the N bookkeeping with near-gap radiation as the channel; a peak at 3 omega = 2.72 means the shed N has another source (for instance an l = 0 part of the stretch).
- What the miss rules in, and what it rules out: Rules in: the family picture for a distorted lump (the omega shift follows d omega/dN to 4% and the frac shift equals omega dN / E to 1e-3, so the settled lump is a family lump of lower N); a second-order shed with the quadrupole ring-down as the candidate channel; no break and no shape instability at the dip start. Rules out: "stretch costs little" at the 1e-3 level in frac at eps 0.1 for the dip start; the step from "Delta N = 0 at t = 0" to "no N lost"; the delivered energy as the whole cost. Not ruled out, and not in the prediction: the cost in rate. The late rate rises 11 and 100 times because the lump moved down the dip's flank (-0.49% is 29% of the dip's 1.7% width, and -1.81% is beyond it, Block L1 basis). [scholar] The 3D control's omega (0.906883) is 3.8e-4 above the radial dip start's (0.906505), which by the family slope is about 0.6% in N, so its N/N_min is not 1.83 and the Block L1 rates (1.7e-5 at N/N_min 1.80) cannot be applied to these runs without the 3D control's own N/N_min (the late rate at eps 0.2 is 6.1e-7).
- Literature check (scholar): has published work already seen and explained this? Partly, the bookkeeping but not the amount. (1) dE/dQ = omega for a Q-ball ([Smolyakov 2018](https://arxiv.org/abs/1711.05730), eq. 111, READ) and dE/dQ0 = omega for the I-ball/oscillon in the non-relativistic limit ([Ibe et al. 2019](https://arxiv.org/abs/1901.06130), eq. 21, READ), so the frac deficit omega dN / E needs no new physics. (2) Smolyakov (sec. 6, eqs. 151 to 153, READ; complex field, linear theory, any dimension, no run, no radiation) reads an excitation made at fixed total charge as a Q-ball of charge Q0 - Qp plus a mode that carries Qp and the energy Ep = delta E + omega Qp. If the mode radiates, the lump keeps the lower charge and the energy shed exceeds the energy delivered: that is the shape of this result, as a DERIVED extension (N36), not a statement of the paper. (3) Deformed lumps relax by radiating, with no charge reported: [Hindmarsh & Salmi 2006](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-th/0606016) (READ, sec. IV; 2D real phi^4 and sine-Gordon, 3:2 Gaussian start): ellipticity falls by four orders, exponentially in phi^4 and as a power law in sine-Gordon, the core energy by just over 10%, with the difference tied to the first radiative frequency (twice the basic frequency against three times); a Gaussian start sheds its excess anyway. The potential here is even in phi, so its first radiative frequency is 3 omega like sine-Gordon's, and the reviewer reads the late decay of a2 in L3-1 as a power law (`SESSION_03_STATUS.md`); whether the early N shed is 3 omega radiation is what the cheap diagnostic tests, and the bookkeeping above points to near-gap waves instead. [Amin & Mou 2020](https://arxiv.org/abs/2009.11337) (READ, sec. 4; 3+1D real, tanh potential): the merged lump starts with an oscillating quadrupole density pattern, radiates, and then has falling amplitude and rising frequency; no invariant. [Ciurla et al. 2024](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.06591) (READ, sec. 3.5; 1+1D complex phi^6): a squash or stretch does not change the charge at t = 0 and decays through a bound mode and a quasi-normal mode with amplitude about t^(-1/2); no later charge in the passage. [Ibe et al. 2019b](https://arxiv.org/abs/1908.11103) (READ, sec. IV; log potential, radial only): 1% random fluctuations grow in the Floquet bands, the energy falls and the lump ends with smaller I. [Multamaki 2001](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-ph/0102339) and [Kinach & Choptuik 2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.07561) (T1, READ in the Session 3 brief, not re-opened): random kicks adding up to 40% of the energy of a 2+1D complex lump change the charge by under 1%; aspherical pulses on stable gauged Q-balls are followed by a return to near the start; neither is a quadrupole at fixed N, so context only. (4) No source found for a measured loss of N or of the adiabatic invariant after a quadrupolar deformation of a settled lump, for its scaling with the amplitude, or for the sextic family. Almumin et al. 2021 ([arXiv:2112.00657](https://arxiv.org/abs/2112.00657), READ in part) say the excitation spectrum of Q-balls at fixed charge was not established. Searches 215, 216, 220, 221, 225 (`PRIOR_ART.md` Addendum J).
- Interpretation notes (scholar): (i) The miss is in the prediction's basis, not in the family picture: the omega clause of L3-3 is a hit at 4% for the same bookkeeping. (ii) The scored miss is one run (eps 0.1, 4.4 times); eps 0.2 is within 3 times and eps 0.05 is untested. (iii) The reviewer's "3.3 times by t = 300" and my "96% by t = 100" are the same deficit: it is formed by t = 100. (iv) The more important cost for the dip start is in rate (11 and 100 times), which the prediction, written in frac, did not cover.
- Cross-reference: F-028, F-029 (same Mac set); F-024 (family rule); `PRIOR_ART.md` T1, T7, N31, N32 and Addendum J (J1, J2.1, J3); `docs/SESSION_03_PREDICTIONS.md` Block L3 (rows L3-1, L3-2); `SESSION_03_STATUS.md` ("Block L3 scored").
- Status: partly explained (the bookkeeping and the timing, from the saved series); which channel carries the N is open; one cheap diagnostic proposed; rerun not needed for the score.
- Diagnostic run (2026-10-05, 400d1ec): a shell record needs a code change; substituted single points at r = 25 on the stretch axis and a transverse axis, stretch 0.1, T = 150, with an unstretched control. The extra radiation peaks at omega 1.07 (flux-weighted 1.08), just above the mass gap; the 3 omega band holds 0.3% of the extra flux (21% of the control's). Radiation near the gap, not at 3 omega. One point per axis; frequency resolution 0.05.

### F-028  WP-L3: the random multiplier at the dip leaves frac(2000) 2.9e-3 and 7.3e-3 above the control, against 2e-3, because the lump keeps 36 to 39% of the added N (Block L3, row L3-3, frac clause)

- Date, session, work package, step: 2026-10-05, Session 3, WP-L3; same Mac set, files and corrections as F-027. Results `results/d_dip_r0.1_s1.json` and `results/d_dip_r0.15_s1.json` against `results/d_dip_control.json`. Only seed 1 ran: seeds 2 and 3 at a = 0.1 and 0.15 were cloud jobs and are lost. Dip start. Missing as in F-027: the box and resolution checks (L3-11) and the spot checks (L3-8); the Mac collisions used the primary start and the dip collisions were lost (F-029).
- Prediction on record: `docs/SESSION_03_PREDICTIONS.md`, Block L3, row L3-3, "Random multiplier at the dip": the multiplier adds N (+a^2, up to +2.25%), so by N32 it leaves the dip start on the high-N side: frac(2000) within 2e-3 of the control for every a up to 0.15 and every seed; the omega shift matches the family's d omega/dN times the settled Delta N within 30%. Basis: N32 and L1-6. The block's t = 0 inputs: Delta N / N = +a^2 exactly, Delta E / E from 4e-4 to 1.2e-2, its sign varying with the seed. Literature cell: N32 (derived).
- Expected value or outcome, with its tolerance: frac(2000) minus control within 2e-3 for a up to 0.15 and every seed; omega shift within 30% of (family d omega/dN) x (settled Delta N).
- Measured value or outcome (reviewer): frac minus control +2.9e-3 (a = 0.1) and +7.3e-3 (a = 0.15), seed 1. The omega shift matches to 4% (a hit on that clause). The lumps keep 36% and 39% of the imposed +a^2 in N (settled +0.36% and +0.88%, against +1.0% and +2.25% imposed). The frac excess is explained by omega x dN to about 1e-3. Late rates over [1000, 2000] are 4.5e-8 and about 2.3e-7 against a control of 6e-9 (7.5 and 38 times). The block's stated range of Delta E / E (to 1.2e-2) was exceeded at a = 0.15: 2.4e-2.
- Size of the miss against the tolerance: a = 0.1: 1.45 times (within about 3 times: a pass with a note); a = 0.15: 3.7 times (beyond: a miss). The omega clause is a hit (4% against 30%). "Every seed" is untested.
- Ranked causes: (1) [scholar] The prediction carried an unstated claim about the fraction of the added N that stays. Since the frac excess is omega dN / E (0.93 dN/N at the dip), a tolerance of 2e-3 allows a settled dN/N of at most 0.22%, so at a = 0.1 (imposed +1.0%) at least 78% and at a = 0.15 (imposed +2.25%) at least 90% of the added N had to be shed. The lump shed 64% and 61%. N32 says where the lump lands in rate (the high-N side), not how much of the added N stays; the tolerance was written in frac. (2) [scholar] A random multiplier puts most of its added N into components the lump cannot bind, and those leave as radiation; what stays is what projects onto the lump. From the saved series (not reviewed, no run made), the lump holds +0.78% and +1.86% above the control at t = 30 (78% and 83% of the imposed), +0.37% and +0.95% at t = 80 (37% and 42%), +0.34% and +0.86% at t = 130 and +0.36% and +0.88% at t = 2000. The unbound part leaves in the first 80 time units, and the kept fraction is nearly the same at the two amplitudes (0.36 and 0.39), so the kept N is about linear in a^2. I did not read how the multiplier is built (the generator is in `swirl/engine/boost.py`), so "broadband" is an inference from the shed fraction. (3) The input range was misjudged for the seed: Delta E / E at t = 0 reached 2.4e-2 at a = 0.15, twice the block's stated maximum, so more energy had to be radiated than the block's range implied; this changes the transient, and I did not separate its effect on the kept fraction. (4) One seed: the t = 0 energy changes sign and size with the seed, so the kept fraction may too.
- Diagnostics run now: the reviewer's numbers; the [scholar] tabulation above. [scholar] The late rates sit near the Block L1-7 family value for the high-N side (about 1e-7 at N/N_min 1.87 to 1.90): 4.5e-8 and 2.3e-7 are within a factor 2.3 of it, with the caveat of F-027 about the 3D control's N/N_min. This is the part of N32 that held.
- Diagnostics proposed (expensive ones), not run: seeds 2 and 3 at a = 0.1 and 0.15 (cloud jobs, about 3.9 job-hours each at N = 160). Cheap, one (a calculation, no PDE run): regenerate the t = 0 multiplier for seed 1 and a = 0.1 with the generator in `swirl/engine/boost.py`, and project the added N onto the lump's own profile and onto wavenumbers below the inverse lump width. If that projection is about 36 to 39% of the added N, the kept fraction is the part the lump can bind.
- What the miss rules in, and what it rules out: Rules in: the family reading (omega-N to 4%); late rates near the high-N-side family value; no break with +2.25% of N imposed. Rules out: "frac within 2e-3 for every a up to 0.15", and the assumption that the added N is fully shed. The kept fraction (0.36 and 0.39) and its time scale (under 80 time units) are measured; its dependence on the seed is not.
- Literature check (scholar): the excess of a perturbed start is radiated and the lump settles on its family: [Gleiser & Sicilia 2008](https://arxiv.org/abs/0804.0791) (READ in part, Addendum I: an oscillon first radiates its excess initial energy to a plateau), [Salmi & Hindmarsh 2012](https://arxiv.org/abs/1201.1934) (READ, Addenda H and I: a Gaussian start sheds a fraction of its energy before settling), [Andersen & Tranberg 2012](https://arxiv.org/abs/1210.2227) (READ in part, Addendum I: Gaussian starts from disconnected regions of (C, r0) in 3D phi^4 asymptote to one omega(t) trajectory) and [Multamaki 2001](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-ph/0102339) (T1, READ in the Session 3 brief: for an excess of about 10 E0 more than 75% of the charge stays in one lump). Random noise on a lump: [Ibe et al. 2019b](https://arxiv.org/abs/1908.11103) (READ, sec. IV): 1% random fluctuations on the exact I-ball in a logarithmic potential, radial, are harmless outside the Floquet bands and leave a smaller I inside them; no retained-fraction number. No source found for the fraction of added N or charge that a lump keeps after a broadband perturbation, for a random multiplier on an oscillon, or for the sextic family. Searches 215, 221, 222 (`PRIOR_ART.md` Addendum J).
- Interpretation notes (scholar): (i) The frac clause and the omega clause test different things: the omega clause (hit) says the settled lump is a family lump of its N, the frac clause asks how much N it kept. (ii) The t = 0 value Delta N / N = +a^2 is exact but is not the settled value. In the radial L1 runs smooth amplitude kicks kept about 96% (relaxed slope 1.908 epsilon against 1.986 epsilon at t = 0, Block L1 basis), against 36 to 39% here, so the kept fraction depends strongly on the kind of disturbance. (iii) The dip start was chosen for L3-3 because N32 predicted a benefit from added N; the rates agree with N32 and the frac does not.
- Cross-reference: F-027, F-029; F-024 (family rate); `PRIOR_ART.md` N32, T1 and Addendum J (J3); Block L3 rows L3-1, L3-3; Block L1 rows L1-6, L1-7; `SESSION_03_STATUS.md` ("Block L3 scored").
- Status: explained for the frac clause (kept fraction and input range); seed dependence open (seeds 2 and 3 lost); one cheap calculation proposed; rerun not needed for the score.
- Diagnostic run (2026-10-05, 400d1ec; calculation, no PDE): the added N is exactly a^2 N0, with zero first-order overlap with the lump's profile. Share of the added N below k = 1/hw: 9.9, 7.9, 5.3% for seeds 1 to 3; below kappa: 24, 19, 17%; below 2/hw: 55, 46, 45%. The kept 36 to 39% lies between the kappa and 2/hw cuts, so the proposed 1/hw cut does not explain it; shares vary up to 2 times between seeds.

### F-029  WP-L3: in-phase lumps colliding at 0.2 per lump merge, against "two out, no merger" (Block L3, row L3-7, phase-0 clause)

- Date, session, work package, step: 2026-10-05, Session 3, WP-L3; Mac set (see F-027). Results `results/coll_primary_p0_v0.2.json` (phase 0), `coll_primary_p180_v0.2.json`, `coll_primary_p90_v0.2.json`, `coll_primary_p0_v0.02.json` (with the slow phase 180 and 90 runs), `twin_coll_primary_p0_v0.02.json` and `gatecheck_primary.json`, in `.claude/worktrees/integration/outputs/session3/wpL3/`. The Mac runs used the primary start for the collisions (settled source omega 0.909532, N/N_min 1.754, snapshot copies from the window (26, 30)); the dip collisions were lost with the cloud runs. Grid N = 180, L = 99, dx 0.55, dt 0.04, lumps at x = -15 and +15 each moving at 0.2 (closing 0.4 by Galilean addition, 0.385 c relativistically), absorbing sponge (gamma 2, width 12), T = 1560. Single box and single resolution: the 1.5 times box and the resolution check (L3-11) are missing (lost). The reviewer's recomputation (`SESSION_03_STATUS.md`, "Block L3 scored") corrected `summary.json`: the contact times there (60.4 and 410.4) sit on a 25-unit count grid and the reviewer's are 56 and 393.6; the v = 0.2 "contact" times for phases 180 and 90 are sponge artefacts and those lumps leave intact; speeds and energies "at the end" of the fast runs are read inside the sponge.
- Prediction on record: `docs/SESSION_03_PREDICTIONS.md`, Block L3, row L3-7, "Fast collisions (0.2 per lump): two out at every phase, no merger": at phase 0 the lumps reach contact and come out as two lumps (by symmetry indistinguishable from passing through), each with at least 0.7 of its start energy; at 180 they come within 2.5 hw and turn back; at 90 two unequal lumps come out. Basis: "kinetic energy 116 is a third of the in-phase overlap energy at 2 hw by the tail law (186), and the meeting lasts about 30 time units, a few oscillations". Literature cell: merger seen up to about 0.1 c head-on (Amin & Mou); pass-through at higher speed (2D, Q-balls) (T3); Claude Desktop's "in phase absorbs, anti-phase bounces" with no speed qualifier, and the row says in-phase does not absorb at 0.2. The block's definition: at phases 0 and 180 mirror symmetry makes bounce and pass-through the same field, so the classes are "merged" and "two out".
- Expected value or outcome, with its tolerance: phase 0: count 2 after the meeting, each lump with at least 0.7 of its start energy; no merger.
- Measured value or outcome (reviewer): phase 0 merged. Contact at t = 56 (free estimate 58.3). The count is 1 from t = 60 to 1535, with a single remnant at rest at the origin: 0.593 of 2 gamma E0 (5793.7), omega 0.8975, still shedding. The pair radiated about 32% of E0 at the collision; the nonlinear share peaked at 0.46 (0.32 at t = 0). Phase 180 turned at 2.24 hw and phase 90 came out as two unequal lumps (N asymmetry 0.35), both as predicted. At 0.02 per lump phase 0 also merged (contact 393.6 against the free 582.8; remnant 0.614, omega 0.8967; L3-4 hit). The gate on the production grid passes at both speeds (speed error at most 4e-4, E/(gamma E0) within 2.3e-5, omega ratio within 3.6e-6).
- Size of the miss against the tolerance: a categorical miss on the phase-0 clause (merged against two out); the energy clause (each lump at least 0.7) fails with it. Phases 180 and 90 hit.
- Ranked causes: (1) [scholar] The row compared a kinetic energy with an interaction energy, which does not decide merging. For an attractive conservative interaction a pair with positive energy at infinite separation (kinetic energy 116) comes out again whatever the depth of the well (186); only dissipation can bind it. The measured dissipation is about 32% of the pair energy, about 1,850: 16 times the kinetic energy and 10 times the overlap energy. The loss hardly depends on the speed: the remnant holds 0.593 of the pair at 0.385 c relative against 0.614 at 0.04 c (a difference of 0.021); in energy, 3,437 against 3,485 (group-sphere fractions times 5,793.7 and 5,678.2), so the extra 116 of kinetic energy ends up in the radiation, not in the remnant. At equal times since contact the fast remnant is below the slow one by 0.04 to 0.08 of the pair energy up to 400 time units and by 0.027 at 1,400 (0.591 against 0.618; energy inside r < 3 hw from the saved series, [scholar], not reviewed). My reading, untested: the in-phase overlap brings two lumps' worth of N (2 x 2,900) into one lump's volume, the nonlinear share rises to 0.46, and the lump cannot hold it; most of the loss comes in one burst (energy inside 3 hw falls from 0.99 of E0 at t = 56 to 0.76 at t = 100 and 0.67 at t = 200). The same Block's row L3-4 had already used the published loss of about 30% in a slow in-phase merger (Amin & Mou). (2) The passage is not sudden at 0.2 per lump. The meeting time in the basis, about 30, is 4 oscillation periods (T = 2 pi / omega = 6.9), and crossing 2 hw takes 17 (2.4 periods). Evidence in the same data: the anti-phase pair at the same speed turns at 2.24 hw (7.50 length units; the tail law gave 7.6), so the interaction at that separation is strong enough to reverse lumps moving at 0.2 each, and an in-phase pair of the same strength is not a weak perturbation of a straight path. The published pass-through speeds are far higher (0.5 per lump in 2D real phi^4, 0.6 c to about 0.9 for complex Q-balls, the ultra-relativistic limit in 1+1D; `PRIOR_ART.md` J2.2). (3) Considered, and it goes the wrong way: the label "phase 0" holds only at the meeting point; at offset x the relative phase is 2 omega gamma v x = 0.371 per unit length (README formula; DERIVED), pi/2 at 4.2 units (1.3 hw) and pi at 8.5 (2.5 hw). That weakens the coherent overlap and would favour passing; the merger happens anyway, so the miss is not an over-estimate of the coherence. (4) Not separated, unchecked: numerics. The class is fixed by t = 60, before the sponge sees the lumps (free lumps arrive at 153) and before radiation reaches it (the first sizeable absorbed energy, 108, is at t = 120), so the box cannot change the class. The remnant fraction could change with the box (reflection from the sponge) and with the resolution (the burst at nonlinear share 0.46 has not been tested at dx 0.367); both checks are missing. (5) Start: the primary start, not the dip; a merger decided before t = 100 should not depend on the start's loss rate (DERIVED, untested).
- Diagnostics run now: the reviewer's numbers; the [scholar] tabulation from the saved series (not reviewed, no run made): energy inside r < 10.03 over E0 at t = 56, 70, 80, 100, 150, 200, 300, 500, 1000, 1500 of 0.987, 0.962, 0.843, 0.759, 0.703, 0.674, 0.655, 0.641, 0.608, 0.590; sponge-absorbed energy of 0.7 (t = 60), 2.1 (100), 108 (120), 643 (150), 1,208 (200), 1,632 (300), 1,955 (500), 2,221 (1000) and 2,351 (1500) out of 5,793.7; for the slow run 0.78 at t = 500, 0.703 at 800, 0.675 at 1000, 0.633 at 1500 and 0.610 at 2000 of its 5,678.2.
- Diagnostics proposed (expensive ones), not run: the lost checks (the 1.5 times box and the resolution run of the phase-0 collision at 0.2, T = 560 on grids of N = 270; the README's job list gives 0.98 to 11.17 job-hours for the checks). Cheap, one: a speed bracket for the phase-0 pair at 0.4 per lump (closing 0.8, gamma 1.09), same start and grid, T = 150, after a gate check at that speed (a Galilean boost can fail at higher speed, [Xue et al. 2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.01597)). About 15 to 20 minutes per run at N = 180 (the v = 0.2 gate took 556 s at T = 120 and N = 160). It tells whether the merger gives way to two out by 0.4 per lump, and places this potential against the 0.5 per lump (2D phi^4) and 0.6 c (Q-ball) pass-through values.
- What the miss rules in, and what it rules out: Rules in: in-phase absorption at 0.2 per lump with a remnant fraction within 3.5% of the slow value, so Claude Desktop's "in phase absorbs" holds at both speeds in this model; a dissipation of about 30% of the pair energy in the in-phase overlap that does not change over a factor of 10 in speed (0.04 to 0.385 c relative); the tail-law turning distance for anti-phase (7.6 against 7.50). Rules out: the row's comparison of a kinetic energy with an overlap energy as a merger criterion; the qualifier that in-phase does not absorb at 0.2; any pass-through speed below 0.2 per lump for this potential, if the outcome does not alternate with speed (1+1D false-vacuum oscillons show windows of reflection and crossing in speed, [Campos et al. 2026](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12633), T3 READ). Not ruled out: pass-through at higher speed (no run).
- Literature check (scholar): has published work already seen and explained this? The slow-speed half yes, the 0.385 c half no. (1) In-phase merger with about 30% of the energy radiated: [Amin & Mou 2020](https://arxiv.org/abs/2009.11337) (READ, sec. 4; 3+1D real, tanh potential, omega = 0.82 m): at v/c = 1e-2 E_final is about 0.7 of the sum and omega_final = 0.76 m; the merged lump starts with an oscillating quadrupole pattern; the statement that relative velocities of up to 0.1c led to mergers has no figure behind it, is the largest speed they state and is not a threshold; higher speeds lengthen the merger time; ultra-relativistic collisions pass through, citing [Amin et al. 2014](https://arxiv.org/abs/1410.1822) (1+1D small amplitude, T3 READ). [Xue et al. 2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.01597): every in-phase pair merges at 0.01 to 0.02 c each (T3, READ); the speed dependence is left as future work (READ, fetch answer). (2) Pass-through speeds: in 2D real phi^4 two oscillons at 0.5 each, head-on, pass through (phase not stated), and an off-axis pair at about 0.2 merges with strong leakage, the 2008 paper stating a critical velocity below which they merge, with no value ([Hindmarsh & Salmi 2006](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-th/0606016) sec. V, [2008](https://arxiv.org/abs/0712.0614) sec. VI, READ). In 3D complex Q-balls (V = M^2 abs(phi)^2 - A abs(phi)^3 + B abs(phi)^4, omega = 0.806 and 0.847): coalescence at 0.14c and 0.15c, a ring with two smaller Q-balls ejected at 0.2c, a triple ring at 0.4c, pass-through at 0.6c, speed convention unclear ([Hong & Lonsdale 2024](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.12342), READ (fetch answer)); axisymmetric gauged Q-balls coalesce up to about 0.125 and pass at about 0.9 and above ([Kinach & Choptuik 2024](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.04323), READ (fetch answer)); 2D Q-balls merge at 0.4 and pass at 0.8 ([Battye & Sutcliffe 2000](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-th/0003252), T3 READ). So 0.385 c relative is 3.9 times the largest merger speed stated for a real 3D lump and below every published pass-through speed; no published number conflicts with the merger, and none predicted it. A ring or ejection, as Hong & Lonsdale find at 0.2c to 0.4c, is not seen here (one lump from t = 60 to 1535). (3) The energy criterion and its caveat: [Cotner 2016](https://arxiv.org/abs/1608.00547) (READ, secs. III to V A; non-relativistic solitons): pairs with positive effective energy pass and negative-energy pairs merge, and the losses to excitation and radiation act as friction that the effective potential does not contain; this row used the energy comparison without the friction (N38). (4) No source found for a real 3D in-phase collision between 0.1c and 0.5c relative, for the sextic family, or for the dependence of the radiated fraction of an in-phase merger on speed. Searches 213, 214, 217 to 219, 223, 224, 226 (`PRIOR_ART.md` Addendum J, J2.2).
- Interpretation notes (scholar): (i) The "a third" in the basis does not follow from 116 and 186 (a ratio of 0.62), and I found no source for the factor. (ii) Rows L3-4 and L3-7 are one physical statement (the in-phase overlap radiates about 30% and binds) written with a speed qualifier in one row and without it in the other; the plan's guess carried no qualifier and was right. (iii) The F-029 numbers are one start, one box, one resolution.
- Cross-reference: F-027, F-028; `PRIOR_ART.md` T3, T7 and Addendum J (J2.2, N37, N38); `docs/SESSION_03_PREDICTIONS.md` Block L3 (rows L3-4 to L3-7, L3-9, L3-11); `SESSION_03_STATUS.md` ("Block L3 scored").
- Status: explained (the wrong criterion and the dissipation, from the reviewer's and the saved numbers); the speed at which in-phase pairs pass is open; one cheap diagnostic proposed; the box and resolution checks are missing.
- Diagnostic run (2026-10-05, 400d1ec): gate at 0.4 per lump passes (omega ratio error 8.0e-5 and -1.1e-4, within 3 times the 1e-4 target). Phase-0 collision at 0.4 per lump (0.69c relative), T = 150: the pair meets at separation 2.8 (t about 35), rebounds to 10 (t = 65), returns and sits at 3.0 to 3.4 to t = 150; no two free outgoing lumps; group-sphere energy 0.41 E0 at t = 150, still falling; 0.34 E0 absorbed. No pass-through up to 0.4 per lump at T = 150; the long-time remnant is not measured; one box, one resolution.

**Hits of Block L3 that bear on F-027 to F-029 (no miss; reviewer's numbers from `SESSION_03_STATUS.md`).**

- **L3-3, omega clause (4% against 30%): hit.** It bears on F-027 and F-028: after a stretch and after a random multiplier the settled lump is a family lump of its new N, so the energy bookkeeping dE = omega dN is the right frame for both frac misses.
- **L3-1 at the dip: hit** (no break; a2 decays, read by the reviewer as a power law; the literal a3 clause fails for the stretch runs at 1e-10 to 1e-8, read as numerical). It bears on F-027: the quadrupole rings down and the cost is in N, not in a shape instability. The primary and b5 parts were lost.
- **L3-4, L3-5 and L3-6 (slow collisions): hits.** Phase 0 merged (contact 393.6 against the free 582.8, remnant 0.614, omega 0.8967); phase 180 two out (closest 5.02 hw, speed 0.0200, N equal); phase 90 two out with no contact (closest 4.29 hw, N asymmetry 0.115). They bear on F-029: the slow remnant fraction is the comparison for the fast one, and the tail-law distance held at both speeds.
- **L3-9 and L3-10: hits.** The production-grid gate passes at both speeds and phases, so the merger is not an initial-data artefact; the twins disperse (0.021, and 0.016 to 0.033).
- **Lost, not misses:** L3-8 (spot checks), L3-11 (box and resolution checks); P3 was not run (premise: the lumps do not hold their N).

### F-030  Test B prediction B-P2: "drifting up slowly" does not hold for the primary after t = 10,000 (the numeric clause hits narrowly)

- Date, session, work package, step: 2026-11-09, Session 4, Test B, B-run 1 (primary) and B-run 2 (dip), plan sections B2 and B3. Results `.claude/worktrees/s4-testB/outputs/session4/testB/b1_primary_analysis.json` and `b2_dip_analysis.json` (corrected analysis, ff0cf8f). Radial solver, beta = 4/3, dr 0.05, dt 0.0125, sponge from r = 200, box 400, T = 20,000, start = static profile with phi_t = 0 (primary omega 0.909149, dip 0.9065045). Reviewer's recomputation: `SESSION_04_STATUS.md`, 12:18.
- Prediction on record: `SESSION_04_PLAN.md` section 3, B5, row B-P2: "Core: fundamental at 0.9091 (primary) and 0.9065 (dip), each within 0.001, drifting up slowly; odd harmonics only (the potential is even)". Basis: Session 1 to 3 measurements; symmetry.
- Expected value or outcome, with its tolerance: fundamental within 0.001 of the start values; upward drift described as slow (no number). Odd harmonics only.
- Measured value or outcome: dip omega_core 0.906836 in every epoch (+0.00033 on 0.9065), drift 6.0e-7 to 6.4e-7 per 5000. Primary, epoch Welch peaks for [0, 5k], [5k, 10k], [10k, 15k], [15k, 20k]: 0.91005 (+0.00095 on 0.9091; +0.00090 on the profile's 0.909149), 0.91262, 0.91980, 0.95214; steps between epochs 0.0026, 0.0072, 0.0323; per 2000-unit window 0.90965 (first, +0.0005) to 0.9551 (last); drift inside each epoch 0.00087, 0.0025, 0.0118, 0.0138. The mean of the four window values in the last epoch is 0.9486 (the reviewer's "0.948"), the Welch peak 0.9521. At t = 20,000 E_in(3 hw)/E0 = 0.597 and the Session 1 control dies near 22,932. Odd harmonics only at both points.
- Size of the miss against the tolerance: numeric clause: a hit, primary at 0.95 of the tolerance, dip at 0.33. "Slowly": the sentence has no number; against the only scale in it (0.001) the epoch steps are 2.6, 7.2 and 32 times it per 5000 time units, so by the 3-times rule of CLAUDE.md the first step passes with a note and the last two are misses. The dip is slow by 3 orders (6e-7 against 1e-3). A partial miss on an unquantified clause, at one of the two points.
- Ranked causes (scholar; the orchestrator's text was not supplied for this entry): (1) The clause was written for the dip and for the early life of the primary; B-run 1 itself states that the control dies near 22,932 and runs to 20,000, so its last window ends 2,900 time units before death, where "slowly" cannot hold (N47a: for a steady loss of N, d omega/dt diverges at the fold of the family). (2) The qualifier has no number, so the score is a judgment; the reviewer saw the late drift ("primary late drift not slow") and scored a hit on the numbers. (3) The "narrow" margin on the numeric clause is partly averaging: the first window is +0.0005, the epoch peak +0.00090 to +0.00095 because it includes 0.00087 of in-epoch drift; the dip settles up by +0.00033 and Session 2 found 0.90689 to 0.90707 from 0.906505 (N47c), so a start with phi_t = 0 settles above its profile frequency by 3e-4 to 5e-4.
- Diagnostics run now: the per-window values above (from the analysis JSON); the scholar's differences between epoch values; no new run.
- Diagnostics proposed (expensive ones), not run: none. Cheap, one (calculation, no PDE): d omega/dt = (dN/dt)/(dN/d omega) with dN/dt from the measured loss rate divided by omega and dN/d omega from the Session 1 family table, against the three steps (D-B3, `PRIOR_ART.md` V5).
- What the miss rules in, and what it rules out: Rules in: the dip as a stationary point of the dynamics (6e-7 per 5000, nearly four orders below the line resolution of 0.0031 in the 2000-unit window); a primary whose epoch step grows 12-fold between the first and last epochs, as the fold of the family requires; a small settle-up of 3e-4 to 5e-4 after a phi_t = 0 start. Rules out: "slow drift" as a description of the primary beyond t = 10,000, and the epoch-mean frequency as a measure of the start value (it carries the drift).
- Literature check (scholar): the shape of the late drift has been seen in this model: [Li, Yamaguchi, Zhang 2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.13276) Fig. 3 (`READ (figure)`; omega_ini = 0.91 starts at lambda E/m about 35 and dies at m t about 2.1e4; the text says that at the critical energy the remaining charge and energy decay at once) and [Ibe et al. 2019](https://arxiv.org/abs/1901.06130) sec. IV B and Fig. 6 (`READ`; omega_ini = 0.910: the charge falls to about 80 at t about 1e4 and the lump then decays quickly; read from a log axis). [Andersen & Tranberg 2012](https://arxiv.org/abs/1210.2227) sec. 5 (`READ`, phi^4): the fundamental's peak widens and the radiation pattern changes about 100 time units before collapse. [Salmi & Hindmarsh 2012](https://arxiv.org/abs/1201.1934) Table IV (`READ`): omega* - omega ~ t^-gamma with gamma 0.18 (2D phi^4) and 0.12 (2D sine-Gordon), **context** only (2D, no death). No source found for a measured omega(t) of the beta = 4/3 lump in its last 15%, or for a number that would define "slowly". Searches 249 to 252, 254 (`PRIOR_ART.md`, "Session 4, Test B against the literature", V1 row 2).
- Interpretation notes (scholar): (i) I would score B-P2 as a hit on both numbers and a partial miss on "slowly" for the primary, rather than a plain hit; the status line's "hit (narrow; primary late drift not slow)" records the same facts. (ii) The dip is the long-lived start the runs were chosen for; its frequency stability is the strongest result of the clause. (iii) The epoch frequency is a Welch peak of four overlapping windows and, for a line that moves by 0.014, differs from the mean of the window values by 0.003 to 0.0035 (0.9198 against 0.9228 in the third epoch, 0.9521 against 0.9486 in the fourth).
- Cross-reference: `PRIOR_ART.md` "Session 4, Test B against the literature" (V1 row 2, N47, D-B3); `SESSION_04_STATUS.md` (12:18); F-011 (the dip's long life).
- Status: explained (an unquantified clause, and the fold of the family); rerun not needed.

### F-031  Test B prediction B-P3: "no fundamental" in the far field, against a bound tail present to r = 45 and 60

- Date, session, work package, step: 2026-11-09, Session 4, Test B, B-run 1 (primary), plan sections B3 and B5. Results `b1_primary_analysis.json` (epochs 0 to 3), `b1_box2_analysis.json` (doubled box, epoch matched), in `.claude/worktrees/s4-testB/outputs/session4/testB/`. Parameters as in F-030.
- Prediction on record: `SESSION_04_PLAN.md` section 3, B5, row B-P3: "Far field: 3 omega (2.727) dominant at the primary; 5 omega present and at least 10x weaker in amplitude; no even harmonics above the leakage floor; no fundamental". Source column: "Oscillon radiation at 3 omega leading (Fodor et al. 2009; Hertzberg 2010; Ibe et al. 2019). Scholar to check". In the same plan, B3: "Evanescent floor: the fundamental's predicted bound tail A e^(-kappa r)/r at each probe (7e-6 at r = 30, 1e-11 at r = 60 for the primary)".
- Expected value or outcome, with its tolerance: four clauses; the last, no line at the fundamental in the far-field spectrum (probes r = 30 to 120).
- Measured value or outcome: 3 omega dominant (99.94% of the flux, epoch 0, r times amplitude 0.0167): hit. 5 omega 2.49e-4, 67 times weaker in amplitude: hit. No even harmonic above the floor: hit. A line at the fundamental is present: amplitude 7.51e-3 (r = 15), 7.1e-4 (20), 7.50e-6 (30), 1.0e-8 (45) in epoch 0, and 6.35e-9 at r = 60 in epoch 3 (omega 0.9549, kappa 0.306); measured over predicted A exp(-kappa r)/r 0.98 (r = 30) and 0.97 (45) (orchestrator, reviewer-recomputed); two-point fits of the line amplitudes between r = 15 and 45 give kappa 0.4144 against the epoch value 0.4145 at the primary (scholar; the reviewer's 0.4214 against 0.4215 is the dip's, where the line is also found to r = 45); the sub-threshold power at r = 30 is 0.997 of the evanescent floor. At r at least 60 (epoch 0) and 80 (epoch 3) the sub-threshold power is at the code's leakage floor and the true content is 2 to 4 orders below it (reviewer). Sub-threshold flux over the loss rate at r at least 30 is below 1e-6 (scholar, corrected JSON). Also present: power just above threshold at every far probe (band 1.0004 to 1.0077, r times amplitude 4.9e-4 at r = 30 and 1.8e-3 at r = 120 in epoch 0), attributed to the start transient (B-P7).
- Size of the miss against the tolerance: three clauses hit, one miss (a quarter of the row). The miss is categorical as a spectral statement and empty as a statement about radiation: the line is bound, carries no flux, and falls as exp(-kappa r)/r.
- Ranked causes (scholar; the reviewer's finding is "partial miss, bound tail present to r = 60"): (1) The clause was meant as "no radiated (1/r) fundamental" and written as a spectral statement. (2) It contradicts B3 of the same plan, which lists the tail (7e-6 at r = 30) as an evanescent floor to be subtracted; the measured 7.5e-6 is that number to 3%. So the plan predicted the tail in one paragraph and denied it in the next. (3) The Session 4 brief (U0 item 4, U3 row B-P3, U5 item 5) flagged the conflict with the published near-threshold wing from the fundamental's width ([Salmi & Hindmarsh 2012](https://arxiv.org/abs/1201.1934), [Andersen & Tranberg 2012](https://arxiv.org/abs/1210.2227), both `READ`) and proposed "no line at the fundamental; a wing above threshold scaling with the line width may be present". That wording would also have missed: the tail is a line at the fundamental, and the brief did not name the bound tail as a far-field feature at r = 30 to 60. (4) The wing did appear (band 1.0004 to 1.0077), but its source here is the start transient, not the width (V1 row 13).
- Diagnostics run now: sub-threshold power against the evanescent floor at each far probe and epoch (`sub_over_floor`: 0.997 at r = 30 and 0.34 to 0.78 at r = 45 to 120 in epoch 0, where the leakage floor takes over); the exponent test (criterion 2 of the plan's B4: the line falls faster than 1/r; fitted kappa within 0.0001 of the prediction); the doubled box and half dr (verdict unchanged); the scholar's reading of the line amplitudes per probe from the JSON.
- Diagnostics proposed (expensive ones), not run: none needed. The tail at r = 60 in epoch 0 (predicted 1e-11) is below the leakage floor, so it cannot be measured with this pipeline.
- What the miss rules in, and what it rules out: Rules in: the linear far field (a bound tail with the family's own kappa to 3 or 4 digits, no radiated line at omega_core); B-P5 and the verdict "emission only above threshold"; the plan's B3 evanescent floor as written. Rules out: "no fundamental" as a statement about the far-field spectrum at r up to about 7 decay lengths (kappa r of 12.6 at r = 30). The clause should read: no outgoing line at the fundamental; a bound tail exp(-kappa r)/r is present out to the point where it meets the leakage floor.
- Literature check (scholar): the linear statement is standard: below m the harmonic equation has only decaying solutions ([Fodor review](https://arxiv.org/abs/1911.03340) eq. 2.199 and sec. 2.7.1, `READ`), and radiation leaves only at n-bar omega ([Hertzberg 2010](https://arxiv.org/abs/1003.3459) eqs. 17, 18, `READ`). The published far-field spectra (S&H at r = 80; A&T Fig. 9 at r = 80) show the fundamental only as the part of its peak above m, which they trace to the width; at r = 80 a tail with kappa 0.3 to 0.4 is e^-24 to e^-32 and cannot be seen. No source found for a measurement of the bound tail in a probe series (search 256). The near-threshold power was seen in kind by S&H, A&T and [Zhang et al. 2020](https://arxiv.org/abs/2004.01202) fn. 15 (`READ`).
- Interpretation notes (scholar): (i) The fundamental line at r = 30 is 74 times below the 3 omega line in amplitude and 9e-10 of the flux, so its presence does not touch the verdict. (ii) The plan's B4 criterion 1 and 2 were built for exactly this tail (evanescent floor, exponent steeper than -1.3), and both passed. (iii) The B-P3 row is the one place where the brief and the plan disagreed before the run; the run sided with the plan's B3 and with linear theory, and against the plain reading of the row.
- Cross-reference: `PRIOR_ART.md` "Session 4 brief" U0 item 4, U3, U5 item 5, and "Session 4, Test B against the literature" (V1 rows 12 and 13, N49); `SESSION_04_STATUS.md` (12:18).
- Status: explained (wording; the plan's own B3 predicted the tail); rerun not needed.

### F-032  Test B prediction B-P6 as run: above-threshold line flux over the loss rate is 1.63 to 1.66 in the primary's last epoch (an instrument defect; 0.97 to 1.00 after the fix)

- Date, session, work package, step: 2026-11-09, Session 4, Test B, B-run 1 (primary) and B-run 2 (dip), plan section B3 (flux against the loss rate). As run: s4-testB a68b0ab. After the fix: ff0cf8f (same series files, same options, run code unchanged). Results in `.claude/worktrees/s4-testB/outputs/session4/testB/`; fix notes `docs/S4_TESTB_NOTES.md` section 8 in that worktree. Parameters as in F-030.
- Prediction on record: `SESSION_04_PLAN.md` section 3, B5, row B-P6: "The above-threshold line flux matches the measured loss rate within 30% at the primary". Basis: "Energy conservation, with E + absorbed already conserved to 1e-5".
- Expected value or outcome, with its tolerance: flux over loss between 0.7 and 1.3 in every epoch at the primary.
- Measured value or outcome: harmonic-band flux over -dE_in(3 hw)/dt, method 1, at r = 30 and r = 120. As run: 0.996 and 0.980 ([0, 5k]); 0.994 and 0.971 ([5k, 10k]); 0.926 and 1.149 ([10k, 15k]); 1.657 and 1.647 ([15k, 20k]). After the fix: 0.996 and 0.980; 0.993 and 0.971; 0.992 and 0.977; 0.971 and 1.003. Dip, bands included, as run then fixed: epoch 0 1.004 then 1.005 (r = 30) and 1.197 then 1.401 (r = 120, where the near-threshold band now holds its whole hump: the start transient passing, 3.7e-6 against a loss of 9.2e-6); 0.997 in epochs 1 to 3; harmonic lines only, after the fix, 0.9964 to 0.9972 in every epoch at both radii. Loss rate -dE_in(3 hw)/dt: primary 0.0122, 0.0332, 0.1138, 0.0674; dip 9.20e-6 to 9.79e-6.
- Size of the miss against the tolerance: the last epoch as run, 1.657, is 0.357 outside the band, 2.2 times the tolerance of 0.3: by the 3-times rule of CLAUDE.md a pass with a note; it was scored a miss by the reviewer and the orchestrator and is kept as a miss here because its cause is a defect. After the fix every epoch and radius lies within 3% of 1, ten times inside the tolerance. The verdict did not change at any stage (criterion 3 allows a factor of 3).
- Ranked causes (reviewer, by the fix notes): (1) The line bands in `lines_in` were intervals of +-(3 + n drift/2) bins about each peak. When the in-epoch drift split a line into sub-peaks the bands overlapped and counted bins twice: b1_primary [15k, 20k], r = 30, the 3 omega bands [896, 903], [901, 908], [898, 919] counted 10 bins twice, line flux 0.1116 against 0.0646 in their union. The same rule left gaps elsewhere (epoch 2, r = 45 and 60: 28% of the flux outside every band). (2) Considered and not the cause: sub-threshold flux (below 1e-6 of the loss at r at least 30), the sponge (E_h never rises: largest step rise 2.4e-10 of E_h(0)), resolution (half dr agrees), the window weights (the loss rate is weighted like the spectra).
- Diagnostics run now: the reviewer's recomputation and the fix with disjoint bands grown strongest first (line fluxes now add up to the in-band flux exactly); test `test_drifting_and_split_lines_flux_adds_up`: a 3 omega line drifting by 0.04, a 5 omega by 0.067, two tones 4 bins apart and a near-threshold chirp, summed line flux equal to the total to 1.3e-5 at r = 30 and 120 (target 1e-3); the six analyses rerun from the same series; the scholar's check on the corrected JSON (not reviewed): the total spectral flux over -dE_in(r)/dt at the same r is 0.99995 to 1.00013 for the primary at r at least 30 and 0.9973 to 0.9986 for the dip in every epoch, so the flux, the window weights and the energy bookkeeping were sound throughout and the defect was in the band assignment alone.
- Diagnostics proposed (expensive ones), not run: none.
- What the miss rules in, and what it rules out: Rules in: closure of the harmonic-line flux to the loss within 3% at both points and every epoch after the fix; all of the flux sits in the n omega lines (flux outside every band at most 2.4e-4 of the total at the primary, 1.6e-5 at the dip apart from the start transient). Rules out: an unresolved or sub-threshold carrier of a few per cent or more of the loss at r at least 30; the basis of the row as an independent check: in the radial runs `absorbed` is stored as E_h(0) - E_h(t), so "E + absorbed conserved to 1e-5" is an identity there (reviewer), and the real check is that E_h never rises.
- Literature check (scholar): no source found for a measured far-field flux compared with the measured decrease of the energy inside, in one run (search 253; `PRIOR_ART.md` U2.5). The published comparisons are analytic against measured: [Ibe et al. 2019](https://arxiv.org/abs/1901.06130) sec. IV B (the Poynting-flux decay rate agrees with the lattice dE/dt for charge below about 10^2.5 and deviates above, `READ`), [Zhang et al. 2020](https://arxiv.org/abs/2004.01202) (the analytic rate overestimates in the dips, `READ`), [Fodor review](https://arxiv.org/abs/1911.03340) sec. 2.10 (one to two orders in 1D phi^4, `READ`). [Andersen & Tranberg 2012](https://arxiv.org/abs/1210.2227) Fig. 1 tracks the energy inside, the radiation and the total (`READ`). Nothing found on double-counted line bands, which is a bookkeeping fault.
- Interpretation notes (scholar): (i) The fix is post hoc. It was looked for because 1.66 was implausible; a bookkeeping fault that gave 0.7 would have drawn less attention. The independent total-flux identity above is what guards against that, and epochs 0 and 1, where the lines did not split, passed with the as-run code and still do. (ii) The corrected 0.971 to 1.003 is a check on the instrument, not a physics result (energy conservation fixes the total; V3 N48 in `PRIOR_ART.md`). (iii) The remaining 1.5 to 3% difference between r = 30 and r = 120 has the sign of the light-travel lag (about 97 time units between the two radii) against a changing loss rate: expected -1.9%, -2.4%, about 0, +1.0%, seen -1.6%, -2.3%, -1.5%, +3.3% in epochs 0 to 3 (N47b; derived, rough in epochs 2 and 3).
- Cross-reference: `PRIOR_ART.md` "Session 4, Test B against the literature" (V1 row 11, N47b, N48); `docs/S4_TESTB_NOTES.md` section 8 (s4-testB); `SESSION_04_STATUS.md` (12:02, 12:18, 12:27).
- Status: explained (instrument defect, fixed, reviewer-attributed); the as-run values stay on record; rerun not needed.

**Test B hits that bear on F-030 to F-032 (no miss; reviewer's scoring in `SESSION_04_STATUS.md`, 12:18, numbers from the corrected JSON).**

- **B-P1 (threshold):** hit. omega_th = m to 5e-8 in all three solvers; measured wavenumbers match the finite-difference dispersion to 1e-8.
- **B-P3, radiation clauses:** hit. 3 omega carries 99.94% of the flux at the primary; 5 omega is 67 times weaker in amplitude; no even harmonic. Only "no fundamental" is F-031.
- **B-P4:** hit. 3 omega flux at the dip is 1/2007 of the primary's (6.04e-6 against 1.213e-2, epoch 0, r = 30); the 5 omega share is 34% against 0.065% (x520). At the dip 3 omega still carries 1.9 times the 5 omega flux, which differs from the takeover in [Zhang et al. 2020](https://arxiv.org/abs/2004.01202) Fig. 5 (tanh potential): `PRIOR_ART.md` V1 row 8.
- **B-P5:** hit. "Emission only above threshold" at both points in every epoch; the doubled box (epoch matched) and half dr agree.
- **B-P7:** hit within 0.016 (the reviewer's resolution). The transient follows m t/sqrt(t^2 - r^2) from above.
